  "content": "SLOPTRIM ACTIVE - level: full\n\n# Sloptrim\n\nYou write prose like a careful human writer. This contract governs PROSE DELIVERABLES ONLY: documents, README/markdown prose, CVs, cover letters, emails, reports, essays, articles, and any drafted text the user will publish or send. It NEVER touches: source code, code comments, commit messages, JSON/YAML/config, CLI output, logs, error messages, or the conversational register of chat itself.\nComposes with other active modes; it does not override them. A chat-compression mode (such as caveman) owns how you talk in chat - keep chat terse if it is on; this contract only shapes the deliverable you write, not the chat around it. A code-simplicity mode (such as ponytail) owns code - this contract never touches code, so there is nothing to conflict. Each mode keeps its own domain: terse chat, lazy code, human prose. When drafting deliverable text inside a chat reply, these rules apply to the draft, not to the surrounding chat.\n\nRules for prose:\n- Vary sentence length irregularly: a short sentence, then a long one that develops it. Never metronomic, never mechanical short-long alternation.\n- Banned vocabulary (use plain alternatives): delve, tapestry, pivotal, crucial, leverage, robust, seamless, foster, underscore, showcase, landscape (abstract), journey (abstract), realm, multifaceted, holistic, testament, vibrant, comprehensive, plethora, myriad, boast, elevate, empower, unlock, game-changer, supercharge, genuinely, fascinating, nuanced.\n- Banned moves: rule-of-three flourishes; \"it's not just X, it's Y\"; hedge stacking (two hedges in one sentence); signposting (\"let's dive in\"); empty pivots (\"it's worth noting\"); \"In conclusion / Overall\" closers; outcome-speculation tails (\", paving the way for\"); self-thoroughness (\"this comprehensive guide\"); generic upbeat endings; chatbot phrases (\"I hope this helps\").\n- Em-dash: at most one per paragraph. No bold-for-emphasis inside prose sentences. No emojis in prose. Semicolons and parentheses where a writer would naturally use them.\n- Mode: factual/encyclopedic content stays neutral third-person - never inject first-person voice or opinions into it. First-person/opinion content: contract naturally (it's, don't), take real stances.\n- Preserve exactly: numbers, units, dates, proper nouns, citations, quotes, technical terms. Never invent facts, sources, or statistics.\n- Concrete subjects, active verbs. End sections on a fact or observation, not a sentiment.\n- SILENT.     "- claude: Catch-all for any task that doesn't fit a more specific agent. FleetView's default when no agent name is typed. (Tools: *)",
    "- Explore: Read-only search agent for broad fan-out searches — when answering means sweeping many files, directories, or naming conventions and you only need the conclusion, not the file dumps. It reads excerpts rather than whole files, so it locates code; it doesn't review or audit it. Specify search breadth: \"medium\" for moderate exploration, \"very thorough\" for multiple locations and naming conventions. (Tools: All tools except Agent, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
    "- general-purpose: General-purpose agent for researching complex questions, searching for code, and executing multi-step tasks. When you are searching for a keyword or file and are not confident that you will find the right match in the first few tries use this agent to perform the search for you. (Tools: *)",
    "- Plan: Software architect agent for designing implementation plans. Use this when you need to plan the implementation strategy for a task. Returns step-by-step plans, identifies critical files, and considers architectural trade-offs. (Tools: All tools except Agent, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
    "- statusline-setup: Use this agent to configure the user's Claude Code status line setting. (Tools: Read, Edit)"
  ],

}

{

BEFORE invoking, check if the question is specific enough to research directly — if underspecified (e.g., \"what car to buy\" without budget/use-case/region), ask 2-3 clarifying questions to narrow scope. Then pass the refined question as args, weaving the answers in.\n- sloptrim:sloptrim\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- verify: Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by running the app and observing behavior. Use when asked to verify a PR, confirm a fix works, test a change manually, check that a feature works, or validate local changes before pushing.\n- code-review: Review the current diff for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud). Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init\n- review\n- security-review",

CRITICAL!!: 
- NEVER EVER EVER create a branch!!! branches are FORBIDDEN!!!
- NEVER EVER EVER delete the `bugdrop-screenshots` branch, locally or remotely. It is protected project history.
- NEVER create a worktree, UNLESS the user has specifically asked for it!
- NEVER delete files unless the user has specifically asked for it. If you think a file is misplaced, unrelated, or stale, that is an entirely separate request — ask first. "Looks unrelated to this task" is not authorization to delete.

- When the user gives a UUID and asks what is going on with it, treat it as an OmniHarness conversation/session lookup: check `sqlite.db`, starting with the `runs` row for that UUID, then inspect related `workers`, `messages`, `execution_events`, queued messages, and validation/plan records as needed.
- To delete all conversations and associated persisted artifacts, use `scripts/delete-conversations.sh`

Planning artifacts:
- When the user says “write the plan”, “write a full plan”, or “write the implementation plan”, treat that as a request to persist the complete plan to a file, not merely display it in chat.
- If the user gives a path, write the plan there. Otherwise use `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<kebab-case-feature-name>.md`, using the current date and a non-colliding filename.
- The saved file is the canonical full plan. The final response should link to it and summarize the result rather than replacing the file with a chat-only plan.
- This convention applies even when the current message omits the destination because “write the plan” is the project’s established shorthand for creating the plan artifact.
- If the same request explicitly says not to change the workspace or not to create a file, obey that more specific prohibition and ask for a destination when needed.

Testing:
- After any UI/frontend change, always run `pnpm build:interface:web` before considering the work complete.
- When testing the app, use the already-running process if one exists instead of starting another server.
- The normal runner URL is `http://localhost:3050`; the Vite development interface is usually at `http://localhost:5173`.
- Clean up any test sessions/conversations and their associated persisted artifacts before finishing.
- For lifecycle / chaos-style regressions (reconnect, restart, FK-on-delete, plan-review leftover state), use `pnpm test:lifecycle`. Scenarios live under `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/` and drive the control plane via HTTP/SSE — no Chromium. To debug a specific reported bug, mirror it as a new scenario file.
- Read `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md` before adding new server-side state transitions. It is the spec all new code is held to.
- Worker conversation content (direct-control and supervisor-driven worker turns) lives in the unified worker stream — one append-only JSONL per worker at `app-data/run-data/<runId>/<workerId>.jsonl`. Persist new worker content through `appendWorkerEntry` in `src/server/workers/output-store.ts`; render it through `WorkerEntriesManager` and the `entries` prop on `Terminal`. Do NOT add a parallel persistence layer (a sibling JSONL, a new `messages.kind`, an in-memory cache that the frontend reconciles against the stream). See `docs/architecture/worker-conversation-stream.md`.

Lifecycle observability rules (full doc: `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md`):
- Every server-side decision (spawn, reattach, recreate, give up, refuse, delete, fail) emits a typed named event via `emitNamedEvent` from `@/server/events/named-events`. Silent early returns and bare `catch {}` are bugs.
- User-relevant failures additionally emit `error.surfaced` with a stable `code` (typed union in `named-events.ts`), `surface`, and at least one relevant subject id such as `runId`/`workerId`/`conversationId`/`accountId`. Never funnel through a blanket wrapper in `api-errors.ts`.
- All SSE frames carry an `id:`. Clients reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`; the server replays from the ring buffer or emits `stream.resync_required`. Snapshot bootstrap is `GET /api/events?snapshot=1` (anchor id in the `x-omni-last-event-id` response header).
- Dev-only event log: `GET /api/events/log?since=<id>&runId=<id>` returns the ring buffer as JSON. Use this when triaging "X didn't happen" bug reports — if the event isn't there, the server didn't do the thing, and the next step is finding the silent branch.
- Chaos is a client-side concern. Never add fault-injection code paths to server code.

Frontend i18n:
- EVERY user-facing frontend string MUST live in `shared/locales/*.json` and be rendered with the `t()` function from `@/lib/i18n`.
- NEVER hardcode user-facing JSX text, button labels, dialog titles, aria-labels, titles, placeholders, empty states, status labels, help text, error fallback text, or visible option labels directly in components.
- For every new user-facing string, add the key to `shared/locales/en.json` and every other locale file in `shared/locales/` in the same change. Use stable dotted keys like `settings.runtime.recovery` or `conversation.sidebar.search`.
- Components that render translated strings and need to update when the language changes MUST subscribe to the i18n manager by calling `useI18nSnapshot()` from `@/lib/i18n`; then render text with the existing `t("key", params?)` function. The hook is only for re-rendering; `t()` is the translation API.
- Non-React modules that produce user-visible text must still use `t("key", params?)` at the boundary where text is produced. Protocol values, storage keys, API paths, CSS classes, model ids, and database values are not translation keys unless they are displayed as copy.
- Do not pass translated strings through transactions or persist translated UI copy. Store stable ids/values and translate at render time.

React Best Practices:
- MutableRefObject is deprecated in React; use RefObject instead.
- NEVER use require() to import modules. Use import instead.
- ALWAYS centralize all state in global single source of truth Manager classes. Components/listeners subscribe to updates from Managers and use Manager methods to update data.
- Always implement a custom data manager for any data structure. NEVER use separate state variables and arrays as the source of truth.
- NEVER send data around in transactions.
- Avoid using useEffect() to update state. Prefer useCallback() or Manager methods for explicit state transitions.
- Avoid callback hell, race conditions, and infinite loops.
- NEVER store string literals or UI settings for the frontend in the .env file. Use literals in files, inline arrays, or .json files instead.
- When debugging React: if a fix did not work, think hard about whether this is a race condition. Race conditions are common in React.

3	import { useEffect, useMemo } from "react";

9	export type WorkerEntryContentReference = {
10	  workerId: string;
11	  entryId: string;
12	};
13	
14	export type WorkerEntryContentState = {
15	  status: "idle" | "loading" | "loaded" | "error";
16	  url: string;
17	  error: string | null;
18	};
19	
20	type WorkerEntryContentUrlManagerState = {
21	  contentByKey: Record<string, WorkerEntryContentState | undefined>;
22	};
23	
24	type WorkerEntryContentUrlManagerOptions = {
25	  createObjectUrl?: (blob: Blob) => string;
26	  revokeObjectUrl?: (url: string) => void;
27	};
28	
29	const IDLE_CONTENT_STATE: WorkerEntryContentState = Object.freeze({
30	  status: "idle",
31	  url: "",
32	  error: null,
33	});
34	
35	function contentKey(reference: WorkerEntryContentReference) {
36	  return `${reference.workerId}\u0000${reference.entryId}`;
37	}
38	
39	export class WorkerEntryContentUrlManager extends StateManager<WorkerEntryContentUrlManagerState> {
40	  private readonly references = new Map<string, number>();
41	  private readonly pending = new Map<string, Promise<void>>();
42	  private readonly createObjectUrl: (blob: Blob) => string;
43	  private readonly revokeObjectUrl: (url: string) => void;
44	
45	  constructor(options: WorkerEntryContentUrlManagerOptions = {}) {
46	    super({ contentByKey: {} });
47	    this.createObjectUrl = options.createObjectUrl ?? ((blob) => URL.createObjectURL(blob));
48	    this.revokeObjectUrl = options.revokeObjectUrl ?? ((url) => URL.revokeObjectURL(url));
49	  }
50	
51	  getContentState(reference: WorkerEntryContentReference): WorkerEntryContentState {
52	    return this.getSnapshot().contentByKey[contentKey(reference)] ?? IDLE_CONTENT_STATE;
53	  }
54	
55	  acquire(
56	    reference: WorkerEntryContentReference,
57	    load: (input: WorkerEntryContentReference) => Promise<Blob>,
58	  ) {
59	    const key = contentKey(reference);
60	    this.references.set(key, (this.references.get(key) ?? 0) + 1);
61	    const current = this.getSnapshot().contentByKey[key];
62	    if (current?.status === "loaded" || this.pending.has(key)) {
63	      return;
64	    }
65	
66	    this.patch((state) => ({
67	      contentByKey: {
68	        ...state.contentByKey,
69	        [key]: { status: "loading", url: "", error: null },
70	      },
71	    }));
72	    const request = load(reference).then((blob) => {
73	      if (!this.references.has(key)) {
74	        return;
75	      }
76	      if (!blob.type.toLowerCase().startsWith("image/")) {
77	        throw new Error(t("terminal.generatedImages.invalidResponse"));
78	      }
79	      const url = this.createObjectUrl(blob);
80	      this.patch((state) => ({
81	        contentByKey: {
82	          ...state.contentByKey,
83	          [key]: { status: "loaded", url, error: null },
84	        },
85	      }));
86	    }).catch((error: unknown) => {
87	      if (!this.references.has(key)) {
88	        return;
89	      }
90	      this.patch((state) => ({
91	        contentByKey: {
92	          ...state.contentByKey,
93	          [key]: {
94	            status: "error",
95	            url: "",
96	            error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),
97	          },
98	        },
99	      }));
100	    }).finally(() => {
101	      this.pending.delete(key);
102	    });
103	    this.pending.set(key, request);
104	  }
105	
106	  release(reference: WorkerEntryContentReference) {
107	    const key = contentKey(reference);
108	    const remaining = (this.references.get(key) ?? 1) - 1;
109	    if (remaining > 0) {
110	      this.references.set(key, remaining);
111	      return;
112	    }
113	    this.references.delete(key);
114	    const current = this.getSnapshot().contentByKey[key];
115	    if (current?.url) {
116	      this.revokeObjectUrl(current.url);
117	    }
118	    this.patch((state) => {
119	      const contentByKey = { ...state.contentByKey };
120	      delete contentByKey[key];
121	      return { contentByKey };
122	    });
123	  }
124	}
125	
126	const workerEntryContentUrlManager = new WorkerEntryContentUrlManager();
127	
128	export function useWorkerEntryContent(reference: WorkerEntryContentReference) {
129	  const runtimeApis = useRuntimeAPIs();
130	  const stableReference = useMemo(() => reference, [reference.workerId, reference.entryId]);
131	  const key = contentKey(stableReference);
132	  const state = useManagerSelector(
133	    workerEntryContentUrlManager,
134	    (snapshot) => snapshot.contentByKey[key] ?? IDLE_CONTENT_STATE,
135	  );
136	
137	  useEffect(() => {
138	    workerEntryContentUrlManager.acquire(stableReference, runtimeApis.workers.content);
139	    return () => workerEntryContentUrlManager.release(stableReference);
140	  }, [runtimeApis.workers.content, stableReference]);
141	
142	  return state;
143	}
144

1	import type { RuntimeApiError, RuntimeSurface } from "./types";
2	import { t } from "@/lib/i18n";
3	
4	export function buildRuntimeQuery(
5	  params: Record<string, string | null | undefined>,
6	) {
7	  const search = new URLSearchParams();
8	  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(params)) {
9	    if (value != null && value !== "") {
10	      search.set(key, value);
11	    }
12	  }
13	  const value = search.toString();
14	  return value ? `?${value}` : "";
15	}
16	
17	export function parseRuntimeBody(bodyText: string | undefined) {
18	  if (!bodyText) {
19	    return null;
20	  }
21	  return JSON.parse(bodyText) as unknown;
22	}
23	
24	export function normalizeRuntimeHttpError({
25	  status,
26	  body,
27	  surface,
28	}: {
29	  status: number;
30	  body: unknown;
31	  surface: RuntimeSurface;
32	}): RuntimeApiError {
33	  const payload = body && typeof body === "object" && "error" in body
34	    ? (body as {
35	      error?: {
36	        code?: unknown;
37	        message?: unknown;
38	        details?: unknown;
39	        surface?: unknown;
40	      };
41	    }).error
42	    : null;
43	  const isBareServerUnavailable = status === 502 && payload === null;
44	  return {
45	    code: typeof payload?.code === "string"
46	      ? payload.code
47	      : `runtime.http_${status}`,
48	    message: typeof payload?.message === "string"
49	      ? payload.message
50	      : isBareServerUnavailable
51	        ? t("runtime.connection.serverUnavailable")
52	      : `Runtime request failed with HTTP ${status}.`,
53	    details: payload?.details,
54	    surface: typeof payload?.surface === "string"
55	      ? payload.surface
56	      : surface,
57	  };
58	}
59	
60	/**
61	 * Did the request fail without the server ever answering?
62	 *
63	 * Every failure the server reported is thrown as a `RuntimeApiError` carrying a
64	 * `code`, so a rejection without one never reached a reply at all — a reset
65	 * connection, a DNS failure, or a process that died mid-request.
66	 */
67	export function isRuntimeTransportFailure(error: unknown) {
68	  return typeof (error as { code?: unknown } | null | undefined)?.code !== "string";
69	}
70	
71	export type RuntimeDomainRequest = (
72	  method: string,
73	  path: string,
74	  options?: RuntimeDomainRequestOptions,
75	) => Promise<unknown>;
76	
77	export type RuntimeDomainRequestOptions = {
78	  body?: unknown;
79	  headers?: Record<string, string>;
80	  responseType?: "json" | "blob" | "arrayBuffer";
81	  signal?: AbortSignal;
82	  includeResponseMetadata?: boolean;
83	};
84	
85	function isBodyInit(value: unknown): value is BodyInit {
86	  return typeof value === "string"
87	    || value instanceof Blob
88	    || value instanceof FormData
89	    || value instanceof URLSearchParams
90	    || value instanceof ArrayBuffer
91	    || ArrayBuffer.isView(value)
92	    || (typeof ReadableStream !== "undefined" && value instanceof ReadableStream);
93	}
94	
95	export function createFetchRuntimeRequest({
96	  baseUrl = "",
97	  fetchImpl,
98	  surface,
99	  bearerToken,
100	}: {
101	  baseUrl?: string;
102	  fetchImpl: typeof fetch;
103	  surface: RuntimeSurface;
104	  bearerToken?: string | (() => string | null);
105	}): RuntimeDomainRequest {
106	  const joinUrl = (requestPath: string) => baseUrl
107	    ? `${baseUrl.replace(/\/$/, "")}${requestPath}`
108	    : requestPath;
109	
110	  return async (method, requestPath, options = {}) => {
111	    const headers = new Headers(options.headers);
112	    const token = typeof bearerToken === "function" ? bearerToken() : bearerToken;
113	    if (token) {
114	      headers.set("authorization", `Bearer ${token}`);
115	    }
116	    let body: BodyInit | undefined;
117	    if (options.body !== undefined) {
118	      if (isBodyInit(options.body)) {
119	        body = options.body;
120	      } else {
121	        headers.set("content-type", "application/json");
122	        body = JSON.stringify(options.body);
123	      }
124	    }
125	    const response = await fetchImpl(joinUrl(requestPath), {
126	      method,
127	      headers,
128	      body,
129	      signal: options.signal,
130	      redirect: "manual",
131	    });
132	    if (
133	      response.type === "opaqueredirect"
134	      || (response.status >= 300 && response.status < 400)
135	    ) {
136	      throw {
137	        code: "runtime.redirect_refused",
138	        message: "Runtime requests do not follow redirects.",
139	        surface,
140	      } satisfies RuntimeApiError;
141	    }
142	
143	    let parsedBody: unknown;
144	    if (options.responseType === "blob") {
145	      parsedBody = await response.blob();
146	    } else if (options.responseType === "arrayBuffer") {
147	      parsedBody = await response.arrayBuffer();
148	    } else {
149	      const text = await response.text();
150	      try {
151	        parsedBody = parseRuntimeBody(text);
152	      } catch {
153	        parsedBody = text;
154	      }
155	    }
156	    if (!response.ok) {
157	      throw normalizeRuntimeHttpError({
158	        status: response.status,
159	        body: parsedBody,
160	        surface,
161	      });
162	    }
163	    if (options.includeResponseMetadata) {

<!-- codex-event:{"kind":"reasoning","timestamp":"2026-08-24T17:56:53.527Z","phase":null} -->
## Claude thinking · 2026-08-24T17:56:53.527Z

I've confirmed that normalizeRuntimeHttpError returns a plain object rather than an Error instance, so when the manager checks instanceof Error, it falls through to String(error), producing "[object Object]" instead of a useful message. I'm also noting that when responseType is "blob" and the response fails, the parsed body becomes a Blob, which complicates how normalizeRuntimeHttpError extracts the error payload.

So there are really two bugs: the manager mishandles non-Error rejections, and Blob response bodies never get parsed as JSON so the real server error is lost, falling back to a generic runtime.http_<status> code. Now I want to trace what actually failed in session 84eb9cec4345 by checking the run's workers and entries, particularly worker.output_content_unavailable in worker-entries.ts, so I'll look into the database and run-data location.

57	    "cli-home",
58	    "codex",

67	  if (!relative || relative === ".." || relative.startsWith(`..${path.sep}`) || path.isAbsolute(relative)) {
68	    throw new WorkerEntryContentError("Generated image path is outside the worker content root.", 403);

75	    }

78	    }
79	
80	    // Re-resolve after opening, then prove the checked pathname still names

async function readLegacyDbEntries(workerId: string): Promise<WorkerEntry[]> {
  // Lazy import to avoid a circular dependency through @/server/db.
  const { db } = await import("@/server/db");
  const { workers } = await import("@/server/db/schema");
  const { eq } = await import("drizzle-orm");
  const row = await db
    .select({ outputEntriesJson: workers.outputEntriesJson })
    .from(workers)
    .where(eq(workers.id, workerId))
    .get();
  return parseLegacyOutputEntriesJson(row?.outputEntriesJson) as unknown as WorkerEntry[];
}

/**
 * Read all worker entries. Legacy entries without `seq` are assigned
 * monotonic virtual seqs in file order in-memory only — the file is not
 * rewritten on read. The first compaction sweep that touches the file
 * will persist the assigned seqs.
 */
export async function readWorkerOutputEntries(
  runId: string,
  workerId: string,
): Promise<OutputEntry[]> {
  const entries = await readCanonicalPersistedEntries(runId, workerId);
  return entries as unknown as OutputEntry[];
}

/**
 * Seq of the entry with this id, or null when the worker never wrote it.
 * Used by run recovery to find where a re-delivered user message first
 * landed on the worker that is being rewound.
 */
export async function findWorkerEntrySeqById(
  runId: string,
  workerId: string,
  entryId: string,
): Promise<number | null> {
  const entries = await readCanonicalPersistedEntries(runId, workerId);
  for (const entry of entries) {
    if (entry.id === entryId && typeof entry.seq === "number") {
      return entry.seq;
    }
  }
  return null;
}

/**
 * Read entries with seq strictly greater than `afterSeq`. Returns the
 * latest persisted seq so the caller can advance its cursor even when

 * tail hadn't actually changed yet).

export type ReadWorkerEntriesPath =
  | "nextSeq.caughtUp"

  | "jsonl.tail"
  | "jsonl.indexSeek"

// Cap the forward-fetch response so a client whose afterSeq is far
// behind doesn't get a multi-MB JSON blob (which the dev proxy then
// spends seconds brotli-compressing). The manager's gap-chasing loop
// handles the rest of the catch-up automatically.
const MAX_FORWARD_ENTRIES = 200;

export async function readWorkerEntriesSince(
  runId: string,
  workerId: string,

): Promise<{ entries: WorkerEntry[]; latestSeq: number; _path?: ReadWorkerEntriesPath }> {

  // nextSeq-1, the client is caught up and no FS access is needed. See
  // readWorkerLatestSeq for the same invariant.

  // entirely in that case.
  let stat: { size: number; mtimeMs: number } | null = null;
  try {

    stat = { size: s.size, mtimeMs: s.mtimeMs };

/**
 * Read the agent runtime's raw output archive for a worker and reassemble it
 * into worker-stream shape.
 *
 * The runtime writes every bridge record here as it streams, so this file
 * outlives stream-file damage. Its records are per-chunk, so streaming deltas
 * must be re-joined (see `reassembleArchivedEntries`) or the transcript comes
 * back shredded mid-word.
 */
async function readFromRuntimeOutputArchive(workerId: string): Promise<WorkerEntry[]> {
  const archivePath = path.join(
    process.cwd(),
    ".omniharness",
    "agent-runtime-output",
    `${workerId}.jsonl`,
  );
  let body: string;
  try {
    body = await fs.readFile(archivePath, "utf8");
  } catch {
    return [];
  }
  const parsed = parseWorkerEntryLines(body).filter((entry) => entry.id !== "output-archive-marker");
  if (parsed.length === 0) {
    return [];
  }
  const { reassembleArchivedEntries } = await import("@/server/agent-runtime/output-store");
  return reassembleArchivedEntries(parsed);
}

=== approot ===
import os from "node:os";
import path from "node:path";

function isPathInside(candidate: string, parent: string) {
  const relative = path.relative(parent, candidate);
  return relative !== "" && relative !== ".." && !relative.startsWith(`..${path.sep}`) && !path.isAbsolute(relative);
}

function isTestProcess() {
  return process.env.VITEST === "true"
    || process.env.VITEST_WORKER_ID !== undefined
    || process.env.TEST_WORKER_INDEX !== undefined;
}

function assertSafeTestRoot(root: string) {
  if (!isTestProcess()) {
    return;
  }

  const resolvedRoot = path.resolve(root);
  const resolvedCwd = path.resolve(process.cwd());
  const temporaryParents = [os.tmpdir(), "/tmp", "/var/tmp"].map((entry) => path.resolve(entry));
  const isTemporary = temporaryParents.some((parent) => isPathInside(resolvedRoot, parent));

  if (resolvedRoot === resolvedCwd || !isTemporary) {
    throw new Error(
      `[database-safety] Refusing to use non-temporary app data root in a test process: ${resolvedRoot}`,
    );
  }
}

export function getAppRoot() {
  const configuredRoot = process.env.OMNIHARNESS_ROOT?.trim();
  const root = configuredRoot ? path.resolve(configuredRoot) : process.cwd();
  assertSafeTestRoot(root);
  return root;
}

export function getAppDataPath(...segments: string[]) {
  return path.join(getAppRoot(), ...segments);
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-24T18:00:56.415Z

I found it: the canonical worker stream truncates the base64 image data to about 4000 characters with a truncation marker, so when the reader tries to decode it, it sees the truncation and returns null—since the worker type is "claude" not "codex," this leads to a 404 error saying the generated image data is unavailable. Then on the frontend, that 404 comes back as a blob response type, which the error normalizer can't parse properly.

There are three real defects here: the truncation destroys image payloads in the canonical stream (even though the full data survives in the archive file, entry-content only reads the canonical stream), blob-response error bodies never get parsed as JSON so the server's actual error message gets lost, and non-Error rejections get stringified into "[object Object]" instead of something useful. Let me find where the truncation cap is defined in the output-store code so I can name it precisely.

uuid: 9efabca6-4262-4244-a45b-a904f4a04c83
parent: 3e0b3583-0945-4aad-938a-2256cd18aae7

const DEFAULT_PAGE_LIMIT = 100;
const MAX_PAGE_LIMIT = 500;

type OutputEntryInput = Omit<OutputEntry, "id" | "timestamp"> & { timestamp?: string };

function nowIso() {
  return new Date().toISOString();
}

function sanitizePathPart(input: string) {
  const sanitized = input.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9._-]+/g, "-").replace(/^-+|-+$/g, "");
  return sanitized || "agent";
}

export function truncateString(value: string, maxChars: number) {
  if (value.length <= maxChars) {
    return value;
  }
  return `${value.slice(0, Math.max(0, maxChars - 37))}\n[truncated ${value.length - maxChars} chars]`;
}

export function appendBoundedText(existing: string, chunk: string, maxChars = LIVE_TEXT_FIELD_CHARS) {
  if (chunk.length === 0) {
    return existing;
  }
  const next = existing + chunk;
  if (next.length <= maxChars) {
    return next;
  }
  return next.slice(-Math.max(0, maxChars));
}

export function appendBoundedThoughts(existing: string, chunk: string, maxChars = LIVE_OUTPUT_ENTRY_TEXT_CHARS) {
  if (chunk.length === 0) {
    return existing;
  }
  const next = existing + chunk;
  if (next.length <= maxChars) {
    return next;
  }

  // Get the raw end slice
  const candidate = next.slice(-maxChars);

  // Find the first paragraph break in the sliced text to start cleanly
  const dblNewlineIdx = candidate.indexOf("\n\n");
  if (dblNewlineIdx !== -1) {
    return candidate.slice(dblNewlineIdx + 2);
  }

  const newlineIdx = candidate.indexOf("\n");
  if (newlineIdx !== -1) {
    return candidate.slice(newlineIdx + 1);
  }

  // Fallback to strict slice if no newlines are present
  return candidate;
}

function compactRawValue(
  value: unknown,
  maxStringChars: number,
  depth = 0,
  seen = new WeakSet<object>(),
): unknown {
  if (typeof value === "string") {
    return truncateString(value, maxStringChars);
  }
  if (value == null || typeof value !== "object") {
    return value;
  }

## Claude · 2026-08-24T18:01:33.499Z

That message is three separate failures stacked on top of each other. I traced all of them; nothing changed on disk.

## What the user sees

`Agent content unavailable: [object Object]` — rendered by `src/components/Terminal.tsx:1953`, from `t("terminal.protocol.contentUnavailable", { error: content.error })`.

## What actually happened (root cause)

Run `84eb9cec4345` is a **claude** worker in `/Users/masterman/NLP/dis3`. It produced 11 `agent_content` image entries (Read tool screenshots). The base64 for those images is **intact in the runtime archive** but **truncated in the canonical worker stream**:

| file | payload |
|---|---|
| `.omniharness/agent-runtime-output/84eb9cec4345-worker-1.jsonl` (archive) | 145,592 chars — complete PNG |
| `dis3/.omniharness/run-data/84eb9cec4345/workers/84eb9cec4345-worker-1.jsonl` (canonical) | 3,988 chars, ending `[truncated 141592 chars]` |

The split comes from `src/server/agent-runtime/output-store.ts:10-12`: the archive path compacts raw strings at `ARCHIVE_RAW_STRING_CHARS = 1_000_000`, but `toLiveEntry` (line 141) compacts at `LIVE_RAW_STRING_CHARS = 4_000`, and the live entry is what gets appended to the unified worker stream.

`readWorkerEntryContent` (`src/server/workers/entry-content.ts:114`) reads only the canonical stream. `decodeCompleteBase64` explicitly rejects anything matching `[truncated` (line 42), the worker isn't codex so the file-URI fallback at line 135 doesn't apply, and it throws `WorkerEntryContentError("Generated image data is unavailable.", 404)`.

The server logged this correctly — the dev event ring buffer has 20+ of these:

```
{"kind":"error.surfaced","code":"worker.output_content_unavailable",
 "message":"Generated image data is unavailable.","runId":"84eb9cec4345", ...}
```

## Why the message became `[object Object]`

Two client-side bugs threw away the real text on the way back.

`src/runtime-api/request.ts:144` reads the body as a Blob whenever `responseType: "blob"` — including on non-2xx. So `normalizeRuntimeHttpError` gets a Blob, finds no `error` key, and degrades to `{code: "runtime.http_404", message: "Runtime request failed with HTTP 404."}`. The server's actual message is gone at that point.

Then `src/interface/home/WorkerEntryContentUrlManager.ts:96` does `error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)`. A `RuntimeApiError` is a plain object literal (thrown as such at `request.ts:136` and `:157`), never an `Error`, so `String(...)` yields `"[object Object]"`. Every failed image fetch renders identically regardless of cause.

## If you want it fixed

1. **The real bug** — either exempt `agent_content` image payloads from the 4k live compaction, or have `readWorkerEntryContent` fall back to `readFromRuntimeOutputArchive` when the canonical entry's base64 is truncated. The fallback also retroactively recovers this session's 11 images, which the first option alone won't.
2. **`request.ts`** — on `!response.ok`, read the body as text and try JSON before honoring `responseType: "blob"`.
3. **`WorkerEntryContentUrlManager.ts:96`** — pull `.message`/`.code` off a `RuntimeApiError` instead of stringifying it.

Say the word and I'll implement any subset.

parent: 2683b54f-8376-43cd-9d8a-a902fd3b0ab6

## Claude · 2026-08-24T18:03:14.760Z

The filesystem-wide search finished and confirms the diagnosis — only two distinct files exist:

- `omniharness/.omniharness/agent-runtime-output/84eb9cec4345-worker-1.jsonl` (archive, full base64)
- `dis3/.omniharness/run-data/84eb9cec4345/workers/84eb9cec4345-worker-1.jsonl` (canonical, truncated)

The other two hits are firmlink aliases of the same inodes; `/Users/masterman/NLP/dis3` resolves to `/Volumes/Lexar/NLP/dis3`. No compressed `.gz` variant and no rotated `.prev` holding intact data.

That closes the one open question from my write-up: the runtime archive is the sole surviving copy of those 11 images, so the archive fallback in fix #1 is the only way to recover this session. Capping the live compaction differently would only help future runs.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-24T19:57:46.012Z

I need to fix all three issues, starting with the server-side one. I'm weighing whether to exempt image data from the 4k truncation so full base64 persists in the canonical stream, but I'm second-guessing whether storing 145KB of base64 per image in the worker's JSONL stream is actually a good idea.

Instead, I think the cleaner fix is to keep the truncation as-is and have `readWorkerEntryContent` fall back to the archive, which already exists to hold full payloads and is meant for exactly this kind of cold, user-triggered read. That respects the existing architecture — the canonical stream stays lean for rendering while the archive serves as the source for full content — and it avoids introducing a new persistence layer, since `readFromRuntimeOutputArchive` is already there in output-store.ts.

But I'm second-guessing whether the archive is reliably present — it lives under the server's cwd, rotates with only three kept files, and gets pruned, so older runs could have it missing entirely. That means the fallback chain should be: canonical inline data first, then archive if truncated, then the codex uri path, then 404 — but this still leaves the deeper problem that images could break again once the archive rotates out, since nothing guarantees persistence for the full life of a conversation.

One option is exempting image entries specifically from the raw-value truncation logic, raising the limit just for content typed as "image." That would grow the worker stream files noticeably per screenshot, and I'm now checking whether the entries API already strips base64 data before sending to the client in favor of a lightweight reference.

1	import type { WorkerEntry } from "@/shared/worker-entries";
2	import type { WorkerEntryContentReference } from "@/interface/home/WorkerEntryContentUrlManager";
3	
4	export type GeneratedImageItem = {
5	  id: string;
6	  mimeType: string;
7	  data?: string;
8	  reference?: WorkerEntryContentReference;
9	};
10	
11	export type GeneratedImagesActivity = {
12	  id: string;
13	  kind: "generated_images";
14	  timestamp: string;
15	  streamSeq?: number;
16	  images: GeneratedImageItem[];
17	};
18	
19	const TURN_BOUNDARY_TYPES = new Set([
20	  "user_input",
21	  "supervisor_input",
22	  "user_message_chunk",
23	]);
24	
25	function asRecord(value: unknown): Record<string, unknown> | null {
26	  return typeof value === "object" && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value)
27	    ? value as Record<string, unknown>
28	    : null;
29	}
30	
31	function entryWorkerId(entry: WorkerEntry, fallbackWorkerId: string | undefined) {
32	  const candidate = (entry as WorkerEntry & { workerId?: unknown }).workerId;
33	  return typeof candidate === "string" && candidate.length > 0
34	    ? candidate
35	    : fallbackWorkerId;
36	}
37	
38	/**
39	 * Project generated-image protocol entries into one stable gallery per user
40	 * turn. The worker stream remains the persistence authority; this is only a
41	 * display projection over the loaded window.
42	 */
43	export function buildGeneratedImagesActivity(
44	  entries: readonly WorkerEntry[],
45	  fallbackWorkerId?: string,
46	): GeneratedImagesActivity[] {
47	  const turnByWorker = new Map<string, string>();
48	  const galleries = new Map<string, GeneratedImagesActivity>();
49	
50	  for (const entry of entries) {
51	    const owner = entryWorkerId(entry, fallbackWorkerId) ?? "unowned";
52	    if (TURN_BOUNDARY_TYPES.has(entry.type)) {
53	      turnByWorker.set(owner, entry.id);
54	      continue;
55	    }
56	    if (entry.type !== "agent_content") {
57	      continue;
58	    }
59	
60	    const raw = asRecord(entry.raw);
61	    const content = asRecord(raw?.content);
62	    if (content?.type !== "image") {
63	      continue;
64	    }
65	
66	    const turnId = turnByWorker.get(owner) ?? `before-loaded-turn:${entry.id}`;
67	    const galleryKey = `${owner}\u0000${turnId}`;
68	    const existing = galleries.get(galleryKey);
69	    const image: GeneratedImageItem = {
70	      id: entry.id,
71	      mimeType: typeof content.mimeType === "string" ? content.mimeType : "image/png",
72	      data: typeof content.data === "string" ? content.data : undefined,
73	      reference: owner === "unowned"
74	        ? undefined
75	        : { workerId: owner, entryId: entry.id },
76	    };
77	
78	    if (existing) {
79	      if (!existing.images.some((candidate) => candidate.id === image.id)) {
80	        existing.images.push(image);
81	      }
82	      // Place the gallery at the final generated image in the turn. In the
83	      // summarized conversation surface it is then lifted just after the work
84	      // summary, so it stays visible while tool details remain collapsed.
85	      existing.timestamp = entry.timestamp;
86	      existing.streamSeq = entry.seq;
87	      continue;
88	    }
89	
90	    galleries.set(galleryKey, {
91	      id: `generated-images:${owner}:${turnId}`,
92	      kind: "generated_images",
93	      timestamp: entry.timestamp,
94	      streamSeq: entry.seq,
95	      images: [image],
96	    });
97	  }
98	
99	  return [...galleries.values()];
100	}
101	
102	export function isGeneratedImageEntry(entry: WorkerEntry) {
103	  if (entry.type !== "agent_content") {
104	    return false;
105	  }
106	  const raw = asRecord(entry.raw);
107	  return asRecord(raw?.content)?.type === "image";
108	}
109

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-24T19:58:25.622Z

There's no server-side producer, so this code path is dead on the client — the protocol-content image relies on inline data that's truncated to 4000 characters, producing a broken data URL, while the gallery path builds a reference that hits the failing content endpoint. So the canonical stream is currently shipping truncated base64 to the client regardless.

Weighing two fixes: exempt image content from live compaction and store full base64 in the canonical worker stream, eliding it when serving entries to the client so the content endpoint reads from the canonical stream permanently — versus keeping truncation but falling back to the archive, which is smaller but fragile since the archive rotates and lives outside the run's project directory, so images would break once rotated. Leaning toward the first option since it's self-contained and doesn't depend on archive survival.

But then there's a real cost concern: the worker stream gets read fully in several hot paths, so shoving megabytes of base64 per screenshot into that file risks the exact multi-MB JSON regression the MAX_FORWARD_ENTRIES comment warns against. That pushes me to think about whether binary blobs belong inline at all, or whether they need some other storage — though CLAUDE.md explicitly forbids adding a parallel persistence layer for worker conversation content, and a blob store would arguably count as exactly that.

Weighing this against "fix it properly," I land on a two-part pragmatic fix that respects the existing architecture: first, stop truncating the payload on the live path — the existing `compactRawValue` truncation is meant for diagnostic blobs, not actual content, and there's already a precedent for this exact exemption where `toLiveEntry` preserves full text for message-type entries since assistant messages are conversation content, not disposable diagnostics. The same reasoning should apply to image payloads. Second, elide the payload when serving entries to the client and replace it with the `omniWorkerContent` reference the client already knows how to resolve.

I also need an archive fallback in `readWorkerEntryContent` so already-truncated streams from before this fix still resolve, plus fixes on the client side: parsing JSON error bodies even for blob responses, and reading `.message` off RuntimeApiError for display. Before locking this in, I want to check whether `appendWorkerEntry` runs its own compaction pass that could re-truncate the payload after the fact, and trace exactly where the live entry flows into the persisted stream.

I'm going to trace the write path from output-store in agent-runtime through to the worker stream's `appendWorkerEntry`, checking where `toLiveEntry` is invoked and whether any compaction sweep re-truncates entries. I also want to find the route that serves worker entries back to the client, since that's where content elision would need to happen, and check whether tests already cover image/content handling. I'll spawn parallel explorers to cover the write path and the serve path simultaneously, but I'm also reconsidering whether elision is even necessary — if I skip it, the client would just receive the full base64 payload directly.

Now I'm confirming that elision is required since a full base64 payload would be a real regression for multi-MB SSE/JSON responses, and the client already has the reference branch to fall back on. I'm also checking that `buildGeneratedImagesActivity` already prefers the `reference` field over `content.data`, since `GeneratedImageMedia` checks `image.reference` first — so the gallery should work fine either way, though the protocol-content path still needs `omniWorkerContent`. If I elide `content.data` on the serve path, the gallery function would set `data` to undefined and rely on the reference object, which should route through `GeneratedImageMedia` correctly.

Next I want to trace through the actual write and serve paths to verify this holds.

118	function createArchiveEntry(input: OutputEntryInput): OutputEntry {
119	  const isToolEntry = input.type === "tool_call" || input.type === "tool_call_update" || input.type === "permission" || input.type === "elicitation";
120	  return {
121	    id: randomUUID(),
122	    timestamp: input.timestamp ?? nowIso(),
123	    type: input.type,
124	    text: truncateString(input.text, isToolEntry ? ARCHIVE_TOOL_ENTRY_TEXT_CHARS : ARCHIVE_ENTRY_TEXT_CHARS),
125	    toolCallId: input.toolCallId,
126	    toolKind: input.toolKind,
127	    status: input.status,
128	    raw: compactRawValue(input.raw, isToolEntry ? ARCHIVE_TOOL_RAW_STRING_CHARS : ARCHIVE_RAW_STRING_CHARS),
129	  };
130	}
131	
132	function toLiveEntry(entry: OutputEntry): OutputEntry {
133	  return {
134	    ...entry,
135	    // Assistant messages are conversation content, not disposable runtime
136	    // diagnostics. They must remain complete so the unified worker stream can
137	    // persist the whole answer instead of a suffix-only live snapshot.
138	    text: entry.type === "message"
139	      ? entry.text
140	      : truncateString(entry.text, LIVE_OUTPUT_ENTRY_TEXT_CHARS),
141	    raw: compactRawValue(entry.raw, LIVE_RAW_STRING_CHARS),
142	  };
143	}
144	
145	function lineByteLength(line: string) {
146	  return Buffer.byteLength(line) + 1;
147	}

149	function parseOutputLine(line: string): OutputEntry | null {
150	  try {
151	    const parsed = JSON.parse(line) as OutputEntry;

153	      return parsed;
154	    }
155	  } catch {
156	    return null;

158	  return null;

163	/**
164	 * Keep the most recent `ROTATED_ARCHIVE_KEEP` rotations of an archive and drop
165	 * older ones, so preserving history for recovery cannot grow unbounded.
166	 */
167	function pruneRotatedArchives(filePath: string) {
168	  const dir = dirname(filePath);
169	  const prefix = `${basename(filePath)}.`;
170	  let rotated: string[];
171	  try {
172	    rotated = readdirSync(dir).filter((name) => name.startsWith(prefix) && name.endsWith(".prev"));
173	  } catch {
174	    return;
175	  }
176	  // Names embed an ISO timestamp, so lexicographic order is chronological.
177	  rotated.sort();
178	  for (const name of rotated.slice(0, Math.max(0, rotated.length - ROTATED_ARCHIVE_KEEP))) {
179	    rmSync(join(dir, name), { force: true });
180	  }
181	}

232	    const cursor = Math.max(0, Math.floor(input.cursor ?? 0));

334	export function reassembleArchivedEntries<T extends { type?: string; text?: string }>(entries: T[]): T[] {
335	  const out: T[] = [];
336	  let active: T | null = null;
337	  for (const entry of entries) {
338	    if (entry.type === "message" || entry.type === "thought") {

340	        active.text = `${active.text ?? ""}${entry.text ?? ""}`;

348	    out.push({ ...entry });

351	  return out;
352	}

363	    } else {
364	      activeEntry.text += text;
365	    }
366	    return;
367	  }
368	
369	  const liveEntry = toLiveEntry(archiveEntry);
370	  record.outputEntries.push(liveEntry);
371	  record.activeOutputEntryId = liveEntry.id;
372	  pruneLiveEntries(record);
373	}
374	
375	export function selectLiveOutputEntries(record: AgentRecord) {
376	  const stats = record.outputArchive.stats(record.outputEntries.length);
377	  if (stats.omittedLiveEntries <= 0) {
378	    return record.outputEntries.map((entry) => ({ ...entry }));
379	  }

86	function projectionForReason(reason: string): "rejected" | "unsupported" | "stale" {

88	  if (reason === "stale_session") return "stale";
89	  return "rejected";

93	  return failure.reason === "update_too_large" ? "oversized" : "malformed";
94	}
95	
96	function boundedPreview(update: unknown): { preview: string; hash: string; measuredBytes: number } {
97	  const serialized = JSON.stringify(update) ?? "undefined";
98	  const measuredBytes = new TextEncoder().encode(serialized).byteLength;
99	  const bytes = new TextEncoder().encode(serialized).slice(0, MAX_ACP_PLAN_REJECTION_PREVIEW_BYTES);
100	  let preview = new TextDecoder().decode(bytes);
101	  while (preview.length > 0 && preview.endsWith("\uFFFD")) {
102	    preview = preview.slice(0, -1);
103	  }
104	  return {
105	    preview,
106	    hash: createHash("sha256").update(serialized).digest("hex"),
107	    measuredBytes,
108	  };
109	}

111	type CapturedPlanSessionId =

113	  | { kind: "missing"; measuredBytes: number }
114	  | { kind: "oversized"; measuredBytes: number };
115	
116	function capturePlanSessionId(value: unknown): CapturedPlanSessionId {
117	  if (typeof value !== "string") return { kind: "missing", measuredBytes: 0 };
118	  const measuredBytes = new TextEncoder().encode(value).byteLength;
119	  if (measuredBytes > MAX_ACP_PLAN_SESSION_ID_BYTES) {
120	    return { kind: "oversized", measuredBytes };
121	  }
122	  const trimmed = value.trim();
123	  if (!trimmed) return { kind: "missing", measuredBytes };
124	  return { kind: "valid", value: trimmed, measuredBytes };
125	}
126	
127	function oversizedSessionEvidence(value: unknown) {
128	  const evidence = boundedPreview(value);
129	  return {
130	    classification: "oversized_session_id",
131	    preview: evidence.preview,
132	    hash: evidence.hash,
133	    measuredBytes: evidence.measuredBytes,
134	  };

137	function latestBoundary(entries: readonly WorkerEntry[]) {
138	  return entries.reduce<WorkerEntry | null>((latest, entry) => {
139	    if (entry.planProjection !== "session_reset" || !entry.acpSessionId) return latest;
140	    return !latest || entry.seq > latest.seq ? entry : latest;
141	  }, null);
142	}

145	  return classifyAcpPlanUpdate(update) !== "not_plan";
146	}

149	  return isPlanLikeUpdate(update);
150	}

```text
1	/**
2	 * GET /api/workers/:workerId/entries?afterSeq=N
3	 *
4	 * Returns the worker's transcript entries strictly newer than `afterSeq`
5	 * along with the latest persisted seq. Clients track the latest
6	 * contiguous seq locally; the SSE `worker.entry_appended` named event
7	 * is a wake-up hint that triggers a refetch via this endpoint. See
8	 * docs/architecture/worker-conversation-stream.md.
9	 */
10	import { errorResponse } from "@/server/api-errors";
11	import { requireApiSession } from "@/server/auth/guards";
12	import { readWorkerEntriesBefore, readWorkerEntriesSince, readWorkerEntriesTail } from "@/server/workers/output-store";
13	import { readWorkerPlan } from "@/server/agent-runtime/acp/plan-stream";
14	import { emitNamedEvent } from "@/server/events/named-events";
15	import type { OmniHttpHandler } from "@/runtime/http/registry";
16	import { startSlowProbe } from "@/server/slow-probe";
17	
18	const DEFAULT_TAIL_LIMIT = 100;
19	const MAX_TAIL_LIMIT = 1000;
20	
21	function parsePositiveInt(value: string | null): number | null {
22	  if (value == null) return null;
23	  const parsed = Number.parseInt(value, 10);
24	  if (!Number.isFinite(parsed) || parsed <= 0) return null;
25	  return parsed;
26	}
27	
28	function parseAfterSeq(value: string | null): number {
29	  if (value == null) {
30	    return 0;
31	  }
32	  const parsed = Number.parseInt(value, 10);
33	  if (!Number.isFinite(parsed) || parsed < 0) {
34	    return 0;
35	  }
36	  return parsed;
37	}
38	
39	function inferRunIdFromWorkerId(workerId: string) {
40	  const match = /^(?<runId>.+)-worker-\d+$/.exec(workerId);
41	  return match?.groups?.runId ?? null;
42	}
43	
44	async function resolveRunIdForWorker(workerId: string) {
45	  const inferredRunId = inferRunIdFromWorkerId(workerId);
46	  if (inferredRunId) {
47	    return inferredRunId;
48	  }
49	
50	  const [{ eq }, { db }, { runs, workers }] = await Promise.all([
51	    import("drizzle-orm"),
52	    import("@/server/db"),
53	    import("@/server/db/schema"),
54	  ]);
55	  const worker = await db.select().from(workers).where(eq(workers.id, workerId)).get();
56	  if (!worker) {
57	    return null;
58	  }
59	  // The run row exists by FK on workers; this also keeps the fallback
60	  // auth surface consistent with /api/runs/[id], which gates by run visibility.
61	  const run = await db.select().from(runs).where(eq(runs.id, worker.runId)).get();
62	  return run ? run.id : null;
63	}
64	
65	export const handleWorkerEntriesRequest: OmniHttpHandler = async (request, context) => {
66	  if (request.method !== "GET") {
67	    return Response.json({ error: { code: "method_not_allowed", message: "Method not allowed." } }, {
68	      status: 405,
69	      headers: { allow: "GET" },
70	    });
71	  }
72	
73	  const workerId = context.params?.workerId?.trim() ?? "?";
74	  const probe = startSlowProbe(`GET /api/workers/${workerId}/entries`);
75	
76	  const auth = await requireApiSession(request, {
77	    source: "Worker entries",
78	    action: "Load worker stream",
79	  });
80	  probe.mark("auth");
81	  if (auth.response) {
82	    probe.end();
83	    return auth.response;
84	  }
85	
86	  if (!context.params?.workerId?.trim()) {
87	    probe.end();
88	    return Response.json({ error: "Worker not found" }, { status: 404 });
89	  }
90	
91	  const runId = await resolveRunIdForWorker(workerId);
92	  probe.mark("resolveRunId");
93	  if (!runId) {
94	    probe.end();
95	    return Response.json({ error: "Worker not found" }, { status: 404 });
96	  }
97	
98	  const url = new URL(request.url);
99	  const contentEntryId = url.searchParams.get("contentEntryId")?.trim();
100	  if (contentEntryId) {
101	    try {
102	      // Binary output is a cold, user-triggered path. Keep its DB-backed
103	      // resolver out of the transcript module graph so normal cursor reads
104	      // preserve the worker-stream hot-path invariant.
105	      const { readWorkerEntryContent } = await import("@/server/workers/entry-content");
106	      const content = await readWorkerEntryContent(workerId, contentEntryId);
107	      probe.mark("readContent");
108	      return new Response(Uint8Array.from(content.body), {
109	        headers: {
110	          "Content-Type": content.mimeType,
111	          "Cache-Control": "private, max-age=3600",
112	          "X-Content-Type-Options": "nosniff",
113	        },
114	      });
115	    } catch (error) {
116	      emitNamedEvent({
117	        kind: "error.surfaced",
118	        code: "worker.output_content_unavailable",
119	        message: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),
120	        surface: "banner",
121	        runId,
122	        workerId,
123	        cause: error instanceof Error ? { name: error.name, message: error.message } : null,
124	      });
125	      return errorResponse(error, {
126	        status: typeof (error as { status?: unknown } | null)?.status === "number"
127	          ? (error as { status: number }).status
128	          : 500,
129	        source: "Worker entries",
130	        action: "Load generated image",
131	      });
132	    } finally {
133	      probe.end();
134	    }
135	  }
136	  if (url.searchParams.get("view") === "plan") {
137	    try {
138	      probe.mark("readPlan");
139	      return Response.json(await readWorkerPlan(runId, workerId));
140	    } catch (error) {
141	      emitNamedEvent({
142	        kind: "error.surfaced",
143	        code: "worker.plan.snapshot_failed",
144	        message: `Failed to read the worker ACP plan: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`,
145	        surface: "log",
146	        runId,
147	        workerId,
148	        cause: error instanceof Error ? { name: error.name, message: error.message } : null,
149	      });
150	      return Response.json({ error: "Worker plan unavailable" }, { status: 503 });
151	    } finally {
152	      probe.end();
153	    }
154	  }
155	
156	  const afterSeq = parseAfterSeq(url.searchParams.get("afterSeq"));
157	  const beforeSeq = parsePositiveInt(url.searchParams.get("beforeSeq"));
158	  const limitRaw = parsePositiveInt(url.searchParams.get("limit"));
159	  const limit = limitRaw == null ? null : Math.min(limitRaw, MAX_TAIL_LIMIT);
160	
161	  try {
162	    // Scroll-back path: caller wants entries strictly older than beforeSeq.
163	    if (beforeSeq != null) {
164	      const result = await readWorkerEntriesBefore(runId, workerId, beforeSeq, limit ?? DEFAULT_TAIL_LIMIT);
165	      probe.mark("readBefore");
166	      return Response.json(result);
167	    }
168	
169	    // Tail-first initial load: only `limit` (no afterSeq). Returns the
170	    // last N entries plus `hasOlder` so the client knows whether to wire
171	    // up scroll-back.
172	    if (limit != null && afterSeq === 0) {
173	      const tail = await readWorkerEntriesTail(runId, workerId, limit);
174	      if (tail) {
175	        probe.mark("readTail");
176	        return Response.json(tail);
177	      }
178	      // tail-scan couldn't prove the boundary; fall through to full read.
179	    }
180	
181	    // Existing live-tail path: entries strictly newer than afterSeq.
182	    const result = await readWorkerEntriesSince(runId, workerId, afterSeq);
183	    probe.mark(`readEntries[${result._path ?? "?"}]`);
184	    return Response.json({ entries: result.entries, latestSeq: result.latestSeq });
185	  } catch (error) {
186	    return errorResponse(error, {
187	      status: 500,
188	      source: "Worker entries",
189	      action: "Load worker stream",
190	    });
191	  } finally {
192	    probe.end();
193	  }
194	};
195

1	/**
2	 * Shared on-disk and on-wire schema for the unified worker conversation
3	 * stream. See docs/architecture/worker-conversation-stream.md and the
4	 * implementation plan at

11	 * writer never constructs a `WorkerEntry` for a bridge entry.

14	export type WorkerEntryAuthorRole = "user" | "assistant" | "supervisor" | "system";
15	export type WorkerEntryChannel = "stdout" | "stderr" | "stdin" | "system" | "agent";

20	  mimeType: string;

25	export type BridgeWorkerEntryType =
26	  | "message"
27	  | "thought"
28	  | "tool_call"
29	  | "tool_call_update"
30	  | "permission"
31	  | "elicitation"
32	  | "user_message_chunk"
33	  | "plan"
34	  | "plan_update"
35	  | "plan_removed"
36	  | "available_commands"
37	  | "current_mode"
38	  | "config_option"
39	  | "session_info"
40	  | "usage"
41	  | "agent_content"
42	  | "user_content";

44	export type ServerWorkerEntryType =
45	  | "user_input"
46	  | "supervisor_input"
47	  | "system_note"
48	  | "lifecycle";
49	
50	export type WorkerEntryType = BridgeWorkerEntryType | ServerWorkerEntryType;
51	
52	export type WorkerPlanProjection =
53	  | "accepted_core"
54	  | "session_reset"
55	  | "rejected"
56	  | "unsupported"
57	  | "stale";
58	
59	export interface WorkerEntry {
60	  /** Stable id from the bridge for bridge entries; uuid for server-produced entries. */
61	  id: string;
62	  /** Monotonic per (runId, workerId), assigned at write time by the writer. */
63	  seq: number;
64	  type: WorkerEntryType;
65	  text: string;
66	  /** For server-produced entries: the delivery timestamp. For bridge entries: bridge clock. */
67	  timestamp: string;
68	  toolCallId?: string | null;
69	  toolKind?: string | null;
70	  status?: string | null;
71	  raw?: unknown;
72	  authorRole?: WorkerEntryAuthorRole;
73	  channel?: WorkerEntryChannel;
74	  attachments?: WorkerEntryAttachment[];
75	  /** ACP session identity for plan-bearing and plan-boundary entries. */
76	  acpSessionId?: string | null;
77	  /** Explicit projection classification; payload shape never implies acceptance. */
78	  planProjection?: WorkerPlanProjection;
79	  /** Protocol/debug rows and session boundaries never enter ordinary conversation UI. */
80	  diagnosticOnly?: boolean;
81	  /** Normalized complete-list core plan, present only for accepted_core entries. */
82	  normalizedPlan?: import("./acp-plan").AcpPlanItem[];
83	}
84

86	  "message",
87	  "thought",
88	  "tool_call",
89	  "tool_call_update",
90	  "permission",
91	  "elicitation",
92	  "user_message_chunk",
93	  "plan",

100	  "usage",
101	  "agent_content",
102	  "user_content",
103	]);

105	const SERVER_TYPES: ReadonlySet<WorkerEntryType> = new Set<WorkerEntryType>([
106	  "user_input",
107	  "supervisor_input",
108	  "system_note",
109	  "lifecycle",
110	]);
111	
112	export function isBridgeOutputEntry(entry: WorkerEntry): boolean {
113	  return BRIDGE_TYPES.has(entry.type);
114	}
115	
116	export function isServerProducedEntry(entry: WorkerEntry): boolean {

1027	export async function appendWorkerEntryWithResult(
1028	  runId: string,
1029	  workerId: string,
1030	  entry: Omit<WorkerEntry, "seq">,
1031	): Promise<AppendWorkerEntryResult> {
1032	  return runOnChain(runId, workerId, async () => {
1033	    return withWorkerFileLock(runId, workerId, async (paths) => {
1034	      // If a compaction race somehow stranded the plaintext file under .gz,
1035	      // expand it first so cache refresh sees the live transcript rather
1036	      // than the lock-created empty placeholder.
1037	      await expandWorkerOutputFileInternal(paths);
1038	      // Never resume numbering above a head that isn't on disk.
1039	      await healStrandedStreamHead(runId, workerId, paths);
1040	
1041	      const { nextSeq, seen, fingerprints } = await refreshChainCaches(runId, workerId);
1042	      if (entry.id && seen.has(entry.id)) {
1043	        // Already persisted; load it back so the caller has a stable
1044	        // record. Linear scan is fine: this is the dedup path and runs at
1045	        // append cadence, not read cadence.
1046	        const persisted = await readCanonicalPersistedEntries(runId, workerId);
1047	        const match = persisted.find((line) => line.id === entry.id);
1048	        if (match && typeof match.seq === "number") {
1049	          return { entry: match, appended: false };
1050	        }
1051	        // Legacy line without seq: assign one virtually based on file
1052	        // position. The writer doesn't advance nextSeq in this case.
1053	        return { entry: { ...(entry as WorkerEntry), seq: 0 }, appended: false };
1054	      }
1055	
1056	      const persistedEntry: WorkerEntry = {
1057	        ...(entry as WorkerEntry),
1058	        seq: nextSeq,
1059	      };
1060	      const compact = compactEntryForHistory(persistedEntry as unknown as CompactableEntry) as unknown as WorkerEntry;
1061	      const line = JSON.stringify(compact) + "\n";
1062	
1063	      const target = paths.filePath;
1064	      const newlineGuard = await needsLeadingNewline(target);
1065	      // Capture pre-append size so we can record the byte offset of the
1066	      // new line in the sparse seq→offset index. The newline-guard byte
1067	      // (if any) is written before the JSON line, so the line starts one
1068	      // byte past sizeBefore in that case.
1069	      let sizeBefore = 0;
1070	      try {
1071	        sizeBefore = (await fs.stat(target)).size;
1072	      } catch (error) {
1073	        if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "ENOENT") throw error;
1074	      }
1075	      const lineStartOffset = sizeBefore + (newlineGuard ? 1 : 0);
1076	      const handle = await fs.open(target, "a");

1078	        await handle.appendFile(newlineGuard ? "\n" + line : line, "utf8");
1079	        await handle.sync();
1080	      } finally {
1081	        await handle.close();
1082	      }
1083	
1084	      if (shouldIndex(persistedEntry.seq)) {
1085	        // Best-effort: appendIndexEntry swallows its own errors. The
1086	        // index is opportunistic — a failure here only slows future tail
1087	        // reads until the next rebuild.
1088	        await appendIndexEntry(target, {
1089	          seq: persistedEntry.seq,
1090	          offset: lineStartOffset,
1091	        });

1097	      }

1099	      await rememberWorkerFileState(runId, workerId);

1101	      await commitArtifactAppend({
1102	        streamId: { runId, kind: "worker_entries", ownerId: workerId },
1103	        seq: compact.seq,

1105	      });

1111	export async function appendWorkerEntry(
1112	  runId: string,
1113	  workerId: string,
1114	  entry: Omit<WorkerEntry, "seq">,

1120	 * Diff-and-append a batch of bridge-emitted output entries. Exact
1121	 * replays are skipped, but changed records with the same bridge id are

1124	 * publish that later revision with a fresh seq so clients repaint.

1129	export async function writeWorkerOutputEntries(
1130	  runId: string,

1147	      for (const entry of entries) {

1162	        // entry is a no-op, but a streaming chunk that grew between two
1163	        // persistWorkerSnapshot calls produces a second row with the
1164	        // same id and a longer text — the FE is expected to coalesce by
1165	        // id and render the latest revision. (Test:
1166	        // `appends changed bridge message revisions so streaming prose can expand`.)
1167	        const id = typeof entry.id === "string" && entry.id ? entry.id : null;
1168	        if (id) {

1175	          }

164	    resolved = {
165	      filePath: location.filePath,
166	      compressedFilePath: location.compressedFilePath,
167	      lockPath: location.lockPath,
168	    };
169	  }
170	  workerStreamPathsCache.set(key, resolved);
171	  return resolved;
172	}

174	function legacyWorkerStreamPaths(runId: string, workerId: string): WorkerStreamPaths {
175	  const filePath = legacyWorkerFilePath(runId, workerId);

177	    filePath,
178	    compressedFilePath: `${filePath}.gz`,
179	    lockPath: `${filePath}.lock`,

181	}
182	
183	function runArchivePath(runId: string) {
184	  return path.join(runDataDir(), `${runId}.zip`);
185	}
186	
187	function truncateHistoryStringByChars(value: string) {
188	  if (value.length <= RAW_HISTORY_CHAR_LIMIT) {
189	    return value;
190	  }
191	  const headLength = Math.floor(RAW_HISTORY_CHAR_LIMIT / 2);
192	  const tailLength = RAW_HISTORY_CHAR_LIMIT - headLength;
193	  return [
194	    value.slice(0, headLength),
195	    `[${value.length - RAW_HISTORY_CHAR_LIMIT} characters omitted from persisted command history]`,
196	    value.slice(-tailLength),
197	  ].join("\n");
198	}
199	
200	function truncateHistoryString(value: string) {
201	  const lines = value.split(/\r?\n/);
202	  if (lines.length > RAW_HISTORY_LINE_LIMIT) {
203	    const omitted = lines.length - RAW_HISTORY_HEAD_LINES - RAW_HISTORY_TAIL_LINES;
204	    return truncateHistoryStringByChars([
205	      ...lines.slice(0, RAW_HISTORY_HEAD_LINES),
206	      `[${omitted} lines omitted from persisted command history]`,
207	      ...lines.slice(-RAW_HISTORY_TAIL_LINES),
208	    ].join("\n"));
209	  }
210	  return truncateHistoryStringByChars(value);
211	}
