213	function isPlainRecord(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {
214	  return typeof value === "object" && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value);
215	}
216	
217	const DIFF_CONTEXT_LINES = 3;
218	const DIFF_SIDE_CHAR_LIMIT = 4_000;
219	
220	// Edit tool payloads arrive as { type: "diff", oldText, newText, path }
221	// where oldText/newText are typically the full pre/post file contents. The
222	// generic head+tail truncation in truncateHistoryString collapses both sides
223	// to byte-identical strings when the divergence sits in the middle of a
224	// large file — so the frontend then sees oldText === newText and renders no
225	// diff pane at all. Strip the common leading and trailing lines before
226	// truncation so the persisted oldText/newText retain their actual
227	// divergence, with a few lines of context on each side.
228	function compressDiffContentPair(record: Record<string, unknown>): Record<string, unknown> | null {
229	  const type = typeof record.type === "string" ? record.type : null;
230	  if (type !== "diff" && type !== "modify") {
231	    return null;
232	  }
233	  const oldText = typeof record.oldText === "string" ? record.oldText : null;
234	  const newText = typeof record.newText === "string" ? record.newText : null;
235	  if (oldText == null || newText == null || oldText === newText) {
236	    return null;
237	  }
238	
239	  const oldLines = oldText.split("\n");
240	  const newLines = newText.split("\n");
241	  const maxPrefix = Math.min(oldLines.length, newLines.length);
242	  let prefix = 0;
243	  while (prefix < maxPrefix && oldLines[prefix] === newLines[prefix]) {
244	    prefix += 1;
245	  }
246	  const remainingOld = oldLines.length - prefix;
247	  const remainingNew = newLines.length - prefix;
248	  let suffix = 0;
249	  const maxSuffix = Math.min(remainingOld, remainingNew);
250	  while (
251	    suffix < maxSuffix
252	    && oldLines[oldLines.length - 1 - suffix] === newLines[newLines.length - 1 - suffix]
253	  ) {
254	    suffix += 1;
255	  }
256	
257	  // Always take this path when oldText !== newText. The previous shortcut
258	  // that returned null for short prefix/suffix sent us into the generic
259	  // truncateHistoryString fallback, which is a line-count head+tail
260	  // truncator — when the divergent region sits in the middle of a long
261	  // file it gets dropped and both sides collapse to byte-identical
262	  // strings, erasing the diff signal.
263	
264	  const droppedPrefix = Math.max(0, prefix - DIFF_CONTEXT_LINES);
265	  const droppedSuffix = Math.max(0, suffix - DIFF_CONTEXT_LINES);
266	  const keptPrefix = prefix - droppedPrefix;
267	  const keptSuffix = suffix - droppedSuffix;
268	
269	  const headLines = oldLines.slice(prefix - keptPrefix, prefix);
270	  const tailLines = oldLines.slice(oldLines.length - suffix, oldLines.length - suffix + keptSuffix);
271	  const oldMiddleLines = oldLines.slice(prefix, oldLines.length - suffix);
272	  const newMiddleLines = newLines.slice(prefix, newLines.length - suffix);
273	
274	  const headPrefixMarker = droppedPrefix > 0 ? `[${droppedPrefix} unchanged lines omitted]` : null;
275	  const tailSuffixMarker = droppedSuffix > 0 ? `[${droppedSuffix} unchanged lines omitted]` : null;
276	  const before = [
277	    ...(headPrefixMarker ? [headPrefixMarker] : []),
278	    ...headLines,
279	  ].join("\n");
280	  const after = [
281	    ...tailLines,
282	    ...(tailSuffixMarker ? [tailSuffixMarker] : []),
283	  ].join("\n");
284	
285	  const oldMiddle = oldMiddleLines.join("\n");
286	  const newMiddle = newMiddleLines.join("\n");
287	
288	  const fixedOverhead = before.length + (before ? 1 : 0) + (after ? 1 : 0) + after.length;
289	
290	  // Character-level common prefix/suffix between the two middles. After
291	  // line-level prefix/suffix have been stripped, the divergent region
292	  // still often sits inside one long line (think: minified JS, JSON
293	  // dumps, single-line edits). Treating the middle as opaque text and
294	  // truncating its head+tail can chop the divergent slice out entirely
295	  // and leave both sides byte-identical. Pin the truncation around the
296	  // divergent slice itself.
297	  const middleCommonLimit = Math.min(oldMiddle.length, newMiddle.length);
298	  let middleCommonPrefix = 0;
299	  while (
300	    middleCommonPrefix < middleCommonLimit
301	    && oldMiddle.charCodeAt(middleCommonPrefix) === newMiddle.charCodeAt(middleCommonPrefix)
302	  ) {
303	    middleCommonPrefix += 1;
304	  }
305	  let middleCommonSuffix = 0;
306	  const maxMiddleSuffix = middleCommonLimit - middleCommonPrefix;
307	  while (
308	    middleCommonSuffix < maxMiddleSuffix
309	    && oldMiddle.charCodeAt(oldMiddle.length - 1 - middleCommonSuffix)
310	      === newMiddle.charCodeAt(newMiddle.length - 1 - middleCommonSuffix)
311	  ) {
312	    middleCommonSuffix += 1;
313	  }
314	
315	  function shrink(middle: string) {
316	    const middleBudget = Math.max(0, DIFF_SIDE_CHAR_LIMIT - fixedOverhead);
317	    if (middle.length <= middleBudget) {
318	      return joinParts([before, middle, after]);
319	    }
320	
321	    const divergentStart = Math.max(0, middleCommonPrefix - 32);
322	    const divergentEnd = Math.min(middle.length, middle.length - middleCommonSuffix + 32);
323	    const divergent = middle.slice(divergentStart, divergentEnd);
324	
325	    if (divergent.length <= middleBudget) {
326	      // Anchor the truncation on the divergent slice itself, with a
327	      // little surrounding context. Both sides keep the bytes that
328	      // actually differ, so the diff signal survives.
329	      const surroundingBudget = middleBudget - divergent.length;
330	      const leadingBudget = Math.floor(surroundingBudget / 2);
331	      const trailingBudget = surroundingBudget - leadingBudget;
332	      const leading = middle.slice(Math.max(0, divergentStart - leadingBudget), divergentStart);
333	      const trailing = middle.slice(divergentEnd, divergentEnd + trailingBudget);
334	      const leadingOmitted = divergentStart - leading.length;
335	      const trailingOmitted = (middle.length - divergentEnd) - trailing.length;
336	      const segments = [
337	        leadingOmitted > 0 ? `[${leadingOmitted} characters omitted before divergent region]` : null,
338	        leading + divergent + trailing,
339	        trailingOmitted > 0 ? `[${trailingOmitted} characters omitted after divergent region]` : null,
340	      ].filter((segment): segment is string => Boolean(segment));
341	      return joinParts([before, segments.join("\n"), after]);
342	    }
343	
344	    // The divergent slice itself outruns the budget. Keep its head and
345	    // tail so both sides still differ — each half is taken from the same
346	    // anchor offsets in old and new middles, so as long as those bytes
347	    // disagree the two sides do too.
348	    const half = Math.max(40, Math.floor(middleBudget / 2));
349	    const headSlice = divergent.slice(0, half);
350	    const tailSlice = divergent.slice(-half);
351	    const omittedInside = divergent.length - headSlice.length - tailSlice.length;
352	    return joinParts([
353	      before,
354	      [
355	        divergentStart > 0
356	          ? `[${divergentStart} characters omitted before divergent region]`
357	          : null,
358	        headSlice,
359	        omittedInside > 0
360	          ? `[${omittedInside} characters omitted from divergent region]`
361	          : null,
362	        tailSlice,
363	        (middle.length - divergentEnd) > 0
364	          ? `[${middle.length - divergentEnd} characters omitted after divergent region]`
365	          : null,
366	      ].filter((segment): segment is string => Boolean(segment)).join("\n"),
367	      after,
368	    ]);
369	  }
370	
371	  return {
372	    ...record,
373	    oldText: shrink(oldMiddle),
374	    newText: shrink(newMiddle),
375	    __diffCompressed: true,
376	  };
377	}
378	
379	function joinParts(parts: Array<string | null | undefined>) {
380	  return parts.filter((part): part is string => typeof part === "string" && part.length > 0).join("\n");
381	}
382	
383	function compactHistoryRawValue(value: unknown): unknown {
384	  if (typeof value === "string") {
385	    return truncateHistoryString(value);
386	  }
387	  if (Array.isArray(value)) {
388	    return value.map(compactHistoryRawValue);
389	  }
390	  if (!isPlainRecord(value)) {
391	    return value;
392	  }
393	  const compressed = compressDiffContentPair(value);
394	  if (compressed) {
395	    // oldText/newText have already been compacted with diff-aware logic
396	    // that preserves their divergence. Skipping the generic per-string
397	    // truncator on those two fields is required — line-based truncation
398	    // would re-collapse them to identical head/tail strings and erase the
399	    // diff signal.
400	    return Object.fromEntries(
401	      Object.entries(compressed)
402	        .filter(([key]) => key !== "__diffCompressed")
403	        .map(([key, nested]) => (
404	          key === "oldText" || key === "newText"
405	            ? [key, nested]
406	            : [key, compactHistoryRawValue(nested)]
407	        )),
408	    );
409	  }
410	  return Object.fromEntries(
411	    Object.entries(value).map(([key, nested]) => [key, compactHistoryRawValue(nested)]),
412	  );
413	}
414	
415	type CompactableEntry = {
416	  type?: string;
417	  text?: string;
418	  raw?: unknown;
419	  [key: string]: unknown;
420	};
421	
422	export function compactEntryForHistory<T extends CompactableEntry>(entry: T): T {
423	  // Accepted ACP core plans are already ingress-bounded and their original raw
424	  // notification is part of the durable replay contract. Generic history
425	  // compaction must not silently rewrite that accepted evidence.
426	  if (entry.planProjection === "accepted_core") {
427	    return entry;
428	  }
429	  const text = (entry.type === "tool_call" || entry.type === "tool_call_update") && typeof entry.text === "string"
430	    ? truncateHistoryString(entry.text)
431	    : entry.text;
432	  if (entry.raw === undefined && text === entry.text) {
433	    return entry;
434	  }
435	  return {
436	    ...entry,
437	    text,
438	    raw: entry.raw === undefined ? undefined : compactHistoryRawValue(entry.raw),
439	  };
440	}
441	
442	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
443	// Per-worker write chain.
444	//
445	// Mutating operations on `${runId}/${workerId}` files acquire both an
446	// in-process chain and an OS-visible lock. The chain keeps local callers
447	// ordered; the file lock covers Next/server module instances and dev
448	// process boundaries. Bridge entry deduplication depends on this because
449	// every writer must re-read the current transcript before assigning seqs.
450	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
451	
452	const writeChainByKey = new Map<string, Promise<void>>();
453	let tmpCounter = 0;
454	
455	// Cache of the next-seq to assign per (runId, workerId). Filled lazily on
456	// first append by reading existing entries. Kept valid by comparing file
457	// stat before each chained write so normal streaming appends do not parse
458	// the whole transcript every tick.
459	const nextSeqByKey = new Map<string, number>();
460	// Read-side cache: maps a (runId, workerId) to its latest persisted seq,
461	// pinned to the file's (size, mtimeMs). On a read we stat the file once;
462	// if the stat matches the pinned values, we return the cached seq without
463	// opening the file. Writes happen in a separate bridge process, so this
464	// cache cannot piggyback on nextSeqByKey — but the stat-based invariant
465	// catches any external writer's append correctly.
466	const readLatestSeqByKey = new Map<string, { latestSeq: number; size: number; mtimeMs: number }>();
467	// Same shape but for the full parsed entries list. Used by
468	// readWorkerEntriesSince to skip read+parse when (size, mtimeMs) matches.
469	// The entries list also lets us filter by afterSeq in memory, so even an
470	// afterSeq=0 first-load is served from this cache after the initial read.
471	const readEntriesByKey = new Map<string, { entries: WorkerEntry[]; latestSeq: number; size: number; mtimeMs: number }>();
472	// Cap how many full-entries chains we keep resident. Each value is the
473	// entire parsed transcript for one worker; with long-running supervisor
474	// runs across many workers this Map was the dominant resident-memory
475	// source. Map iteration order is insertion order, so we evict the
476	// oldest-inserted key on overflow. Cache hits also "touch" the key by
477	// re-inserting so genuinely-active chains stay resident.
478	const READ_ENTRIES_CACHE_MAX = 32;
479	function setReadEntriesCache(
480	  key: string,
481	  value: { entries: WorkerEntry[]; latestSeq: number; size: number; mtimeMs: number },
482	) {
483	  if (readEntriesByKey.has(key)) {
484	    readEntriesByKey.delete(key);
485	  } else if (readEntriesByKey.size >= READ_ENTRIES_CACHE_MAX) {
486	    const oldestKey = readEntriesByKey.keys().next().value;
487	    if (oldestKey !== undefined) readEntriesByKey.delete(oldestKey);
488	  }
489	  readEntriesByKey.set(key, value);
490	}
491	function touchReadEntriesCache(key: string) {
492	  const value = readEntriesByKey.get(key);
493	  if (value !== undefined) {
494	    readEntriesByKey.delete(key);
495	    readEntriesByKey.set(key, value);
496	  }
497	  return value;
498	}
499	// Cache of bridge entry ids that have already been written, used to make
500	// append-from-snapshot idempotent. Built lazily from the file on first
501	// use and updated on every successful append.
502	const seenIdsByKey = new Map<string, Set<string>>();
503	const fingerprintsByKey = new Map<string, Map<string, string>>();
504	const fileStateByKey = new Map<string, { size: number; mtimeMs: number }>();
505	let chainCacheDiskRefreshesForTests = 0;
506	
507	// Bound the write-side chain caches the same way as readEntriesByKey:
508	// supervisor runs can spawn hundreds of workers and seenIds/fingerprints
509	// each grow linearly with entry count per worker. Eviction is safe — the
510	// next refreshChainCaches call rebuilds from disk and from the DB-tracked
511	// latestSeq. Keep insertion order so cache hits "touch" by re-inserting.
512	const CHAIN_CACHE_MAX = 64;
513	function rememberChainKey(key: string) {
514	  // Insertion-order LRU across the four maps that share this key set.
515	  if (nextSeqByKey.has(key)) {
516	    nextSeqByKey.delete(key);
517	    seenIdsByKey.delete(key);
518	    fingerprintsByKey.delete(key);
519	    fileStateByKey.delete(key);
520	  } else if (nextSeqByKey.size >= CHAIN_CACHE_MAX) {
521	    const oldest = nextSeqByKey.keys().next().value;
522	    if (oldest !== undefined) {
523	      nextSeqByKey.delete(oldest);
524	      seenIdsByKey.delete(oldest);
525	      fingerprintsByKey.delete(oldest);
526	      fileStateByKey.delete(oldest);
527	    }
528	  }
529	}
530	
531	function chainKey(runId: string, workerId: string) {
532	  return `${runId}/${workerId}`;
533	}
534	
535	function runOnChain<T>(runId: string, workerId: string, task: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
536	  const key = chainKey(runId, workerId);
537	  const previous = writeChainByKey.get(key) ?? Promise.resolve();
538	  const next = previous.then(() => task());
539	  const tracked = next.then(() => undefined, () => undefined);
540	  writeChainByKey.set(key, tracked);
541	  return next.finally(() => {
542	    if (writeChainByKey.get(key) === tracked) {
543	      writeChainByKey.delete(key);
544	    }
545	  });
546	}
547	
548	function clearChainCaches(runId: string, workerId: string) {
549	  const key = chainKey(runId, workerId);
550	  nextSeqByKey.delete(key);
551	  seenIdsByKey.delete(key);
552	  fingerprintsByKey.delete(key);
553	  fileStateByKey.delete(key);
554	  readLatestSeqByKey.delete(key);
555	  readEntriesByKey.delete(key);
556	}
557	
558	function workerFileLockDelay(attempt: number) {
559	  const delay = WORKER_FILE_LOCK_MIN_TIMEOUT_MS * (WORKER_FILE_LOCK_FACTOR ** attempt);
560	  return Math.min(Math.round(delay), WORKER_FILE_LOCK_MAX_TIMEOUT_MS);
561	}
562	
563	function sleep(ms: number) {
564	  return new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
565	}
566	
567	function isRecoverableLockRaceError(error: unknown) {
568	  const code = (error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code;
569	  return code === "ENOENT" || code === "ENOTDIR" || code === "EINVAL";
570	}
571	
572	async function isWorkerFileLockStale(lockPath: string) {
573	  try {
574	    const stat = await fs.stat(lockPath);
575	    try {
576	      await fs.access(path.join(lockPath, "owner.json"));
577	    } catch (error) {
578	      if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT") {
579	        return true;
580	      }
581	      throw error;
582	    }
583	    return stat.mtimeMs < Date.now() - WORKER_FILE_LOCK_STALE_MS;
584	  } catch (error) {
585	    if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT") {
586	      return true;
587	    }
588	    throw error;
589	  }
590	}
591	
592	async function acquireWorkerFileLock(lockPath: string): Promise<() => Promise<void>> {
593	  const ownerPath = path.join(lockPath, "owner.json");
594	  const ownerToken = randomUUID();
595	  const owner = JSON.stringify({
596	    token: [REDACTED]
597	    pid: process.pid,
598	    createdAt: new Date().toISOString(),
599	  });
600	
601	  for (let attempt = 0; attempt <= WORKER_FILE_LOCK_RETRIES; attempt += 1) {
602	    try {
603	      await fs.mkdir(lockPath);
604	      try {
605	        await fs.writeFile(ownerPath, owner, "utf8");
606	      } catch (error) {
607	        await fs.rm(lockPath, { recursive: true, force: true });
608	        if (isRecoverableLockRaceError(error)) {
609	          continue;
610	        }
611	        throw error;
612	      }
613	
614	      let released = false;
615	      const refresh = async () => {
616	        if (released) {
617	          return;
618	        }
619	        const now = new Date();
620	        await fs.utimes(lockPath, now, now);
621	        await fs.utimes(ownerPath, now, now);
622	      };
623	      const interval = setInterval(() => {
624	        void refresh().catch(() => {});
625	      }, WORKER_FILE_LOCK_UPDATE_MS);
626	      interval.unref?.();
627	
628	      return async () => {
629	        if (released) {
630	          return;
631	        }
632	        released = true;
633	        clearInterval(interval);
634	        try {
635	          const currentOwner = await fs.readFile(ownerPath, "utf8");
636	          if (currentOwner !== owner) {
637	            return;
638	          }
639	        } catch (error) {
640	          if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT") {
641	            return;
642	          }
643	          throw error;
644	        }
645	        await fs.rm(lockPath, { recursive: true, force: true });
646	      };
647	    } catch (error) {
648	      const code = (error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code;
649	      if (code !== "EEXIST") {
650	        throw error;
651	      }
652	
653	      if (await isWorkerFileLockStale(lockPath)) {
654	        await fs.rm(lockPath, { recursive: true, force: true });
655	        continue;
656	      }
657	
658	      if (attempt === WORKER_FILE_LOCK_RETRIES) {
659	        throw Object.assign(new Error(`Timed out waiting for worker output lock: ${lockPath}`), {
660	          code: "ELOCKED",
661	        });
662	      }
663	
664	      await sleep(workerFileLockDelay(attempt));
665	    }
666	  }
667	
668	  throw Object.assign(new Error(`Timed out waiting for worker output lock: ${lockPath}`), {
669	    code: "ELOCKED",
670	  });
671	}
672	
673	async function withWorkerFileLock<T>(
674	  runId: string,
675	  workerId: string,
676	  task: (paths: WorkerStreamPaths) => Promise<T>,
677	): Promise<T> {
678	  const paths = await workerStreamPaths(runId, workerId, "write");
679	  await fs.mkdir(path.dirname(paths.filePath), { recursive: true });
680	  // Appending an empty string with `a` is an atomic touch.
681	  await fs.writeFile(paths.filePath, "", { flag: "a" });
682	  const release = await acquireWorkerFileLock(paths.lockPath);
683	  try {
684	    return await task(paths);
685	  } finally {
686	    await release();
687	  }
688	}
689	
690	async function readWorkerFileState(runId: string, workerId: string) {
691	  const paths = await workerStreamPaths(runId, workerId, "read");
692	  try {
693	    const stat = await fs.stat(paths.filePath);
694	    return { size: stat.size, mtimeMs: stat.mtimeMs };
695	  } catch (error) {
696	    if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT") {
697	      return { size: 0, mtimeMs: 0 };
698	    }
699	    throw error;
700	  }
701	}
702	
703	async function refreshChainCaches(runId: string, workerId: string): Promise<{ nextSeq: number; seen: Set<string>; fingerprints: Map<string, string> }> {
704	  const key = chainKey(runId, workerId);
705	  const currentFileState = await readWorkerFileState(runId, workerId);
706	  const cachedNextSeq = nextSeqByKey.get(key);
707	  const cachedSeen = seenIdsByKey.get(key);
708	  const cachedFingerprints = fingerprintsByKey.get(key);
709	  const cachedFileState = fileStateByKey.get(key);
710	  if (
711	    cachedNextSeq !== undefined
712	    && cachedSeen
713	    && cachedFingerprints
714	    && cachedFileState
715	    && cachedFileState.size === currentFileState.size
716	    && cachedFileState.mtimeMs === currentFileState.mtimeMs
717	  ) {
718	    // Touch the LRU so an actively-used chain stays resident.
719	    rememberChainKey(key);
720	    nextSeqByKey.set(key, cachedNextSeq);
721	    seenIdsByKey.set(key, cachedSeen);
722	    fingerprintsByKey.set(key, cachedFingerprints);
723	    fileStateByKey.set(key, cachedFileState);
724	    return {
725	      nextSeq: cachedNextSeq,
726	      seen: cachedSeen,
727	      fingerprints: cachedFingerprints,
728	    };
729	  }
730	
731	  chainCacheDiskRefreshesForTests += 1;
732	  // Use RAW persisted entries (pre-dedupe, pre-renumber) for the seq cursor
733	  // so we never advance backward. A previous in-disk corruption (e.g.
734	  // duplicate seqs from a cross-process race) used to be "corrected" by
735	  // normalizeWorkerEntrySeqs renumbering from 1, which then caused the
736	  // writer to land on seqs already present on disk — making the dupe
737	  // problem self-reinforcing. Read dedup signals (seen ids, fingerprints)
738	  // from the canonicalized view, but take maxSeq from the raw stream.
739	  const rawExisting = await readAllPersistedEntries(runId, workerId);
740	  let rawMaxSeq = 0;
741	  for (const entry of rawExisting) {
742	    if (typeof entry.seq === "number" && Number.isFinite(entry.seq) && entry.seq > rawMaxSeq) {
743	      rawMaxSeq = entry.seq;
744	    }
745	  }
746	  const canonical = normalizePersistedEntries(rawExisting);
747	  const seen = new Set<string>();
748	  const fingerprints = new Map<string, string>();
749	  for (const entry of canonical) {
750	    if (typeof entry.id === "string" && entry.id) {
751	      seen.add(entry.id);
752	      fingerprints.set(entry.id, workerEntryFingerprint(entry));
753	    }
754	  }
755	  // Cross-process safety: artifact_streams.latest_seq is updated after every
756	  // successful commit, so consulting it ensures we never reuse a seq another
757	  // process already claimed even if our file view is stale.
758	  let dbLatestSeq = 0;
759	  try {
760	    const { readArtifactStreamMetadata } = await import("@/server/artifacts/stream-metadata");
761	    const metadata = await readArtifactStreamMetadata({
762	      runId,
763	      kind: "worker_entries",
764	      ownerId: workerId,
765	    });
766	    if (metadata && typeof metadata.latestSeq === "number" && metadata.latestSeq > 0) {
767	      dbLatestSeq = metadata.latestSeq;
768	    }
769	  } catch {
770	    // Best-effort — if the metadata read fails, fall back to the file-only
771	    // cursor. The file lock still serializes within a process.
772	  }
773	  const nextSeq = Math.max(rawMaxSeq, dbLatestSeq) + 1;
774	  rememberChainKey(key);
775	  nextSeqByKey.set(key, nextSeq);
776	  seenIdsByKey.set(key, seen);
777	  fingerprintsByKey.set(key, fingerprints);
778	  fileStateByKey.set(key, await readWorkerFileState(runId, workerId));
779	  return { nextSeq, seen, fingerprints };
780	}
781	
782	async function rememberWorkerFileState(runId: string, workerId: string) {
783	  fileStateByKey.set(chainKey(runId, workerId), await readWorkerFileState(runId, workerId));
784	}
785	
786	async function readAllPersistedEntries(runId: string, workerId: string): Promise<WorkerEntry[]> {
787	  // Mirrors readWorkerOutputEntries's plaintext-first fallback chain but
788	  // returns the raw on-disk shape (WorkerEntry-ish — seq may be absent on
789	  // legacy lines; that's fine since the writer treats missing seq as 0).
790	  const paths = await workerStreamPaths(runId, workerId, "read");
791	  try {
792	    const body = await fs.readFile(paths.filePath, "utf8");
793	    const parsed = parseWorkerEntryLines(body);
794	    if (body.trim() || parsed.length > 0) {
795	      return parsed;
796	    }
797	  } catch (error) {
798	    if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "ENOENT") {
799	      throw error;
800	    }
801	  }
802	  const compressed = await readFromCompressedFile(paths.compressedFilePath);
803	  if (compressed.length > 0) {
804	    return compressed;
805	  }
806	  if (paths.filePath !== legacyWorkerFilePath(runId, workerId)) {
807	    const legacy = await readFromLegacyWorkerFiles(runId, workerId);
808	    if (legacy.length > 0) {
809	      return legacy;
810	    }
811	  }
812	  const archived = await readFromArchive(runId, workerId);
813	  if (archived.length > 0) {
814	    return archived;
815	  }
816	  return readLegacyDbEntries(workerId);
817	}
818	
819	/**
820	 * Guard against resuming a transcript mid-stream.
821	 *
822	 * `refreshChainCaches` seeds the seq cursor from
823	 * `max(fileMaxSeq, artifact_streams.latest_seq) + 1`. That `max()` exists for
824	 * cross-process safety, but it has a failure mode: when the plaintext stream
825	 * file is missing or empty while the DB cursor is already advanced, the writer
826	 * happily starts numbering at `latest_seq + 1` and appends forward forever. The
827	 * entries below that seq are never written, so the transcript silently loses its
828	 * head — the UI then renders the survivors as if the conversation began there.
829	 * This is what destroyed the head of runs e51514929b72 / e74cf7a81b7f /
830	 * ef23fac96fdb / 1795ba24270e.
831	 *
832	 * The fallback chain in `readAllPersistedEntries` can usually still see that
833	 * history (compacted `.gz`, the legacy path, or the raw runtime archive), but it
834	 * is only consulted while the plaintext is empty — the moment one entry lands,
835	 * the partial file shadows every fallback permanently. So the only safe moment
836	 * to repair is right here, before the first append.
837	 *
838	 * Must be called with the worker file lock held.
839	 */
840	async function healStrandedStreamHead(
841	  runId: string,
842	  workerId: string,
843	  paths: WorkerStreamPaths,
844	): Promise<void> {
845	  // Fast path: a non-empty file means the head (if any) is already
846	  // materialized. Normal streaming appends pay one stat and nothing else.
847	  // Stat `paths.filePath` (the write target) rather than going through
848	  // `readWorkerFileState`, whose read-mode resolution can point at the legacy
849	  // flat path and hide an empty live stream.
850	  const size = await fs.stat(paths.filePath).then((stat) => stat.size).catch(() => 0);
851	  if (size > 0) {
852	    return;
853	  }
854	
855	  let dbLatestSeq = 0;
856	  try {
857	    const { readArtifactStreamMetadata } = await import("@/server/artifacts/stream-metadata");
858	    const metadata = await readArtifactStreamMetadata({ runId, kind: "worker_entries", ownerId: workerId });
859	    if (metadata && typeof metadata.latestSeq === "number" && metadata.latestSeq > 0) {
860	      dbLatestSeq = metadata.latestSeq;
861	    }
862	  } catch {
863	    // Metadata unavailable — nothing to reconcile against, so nothing to heal.
864	    return;
865	  }
866	  if (dbLatestSeq <= 0) {
867	    // Genuinely a brand-new stream. Numbering starts at 1 as usual.
868	    return;
869	  }
870	
871	  // Empty file + advanced cursor = the head is stranded. Recover whatever the
872	  // fallback chain can still reach and write it back before numbering resumes.
873	  let recovered = await readAllPersistedEntries(runId, workerId);
874	  if (recovered.length === 0) {
875	    // Last resort: the agent runtime's raw output archive. It is the only
876	    // source that survived the outage this guard exists to prevent, and it is
877	    // deliberately NOT in readAllPersistedEntries's chain — `readFromArchive`
878	    // there reads the run `.zip`, a different artifact entirely.
879	    recovered = await readFromRuntimeOutputArchive(workerId);
880	  }
881	  if (recovered.length === 0) {
882	    emitNamedEvent({
883	      kind: "worker.stream_head_unrecoverable",
884	      runId,
885	      workerId,
886	      expectedLatestSeq: dbLatestSeq,
887	    });
888	    console.error(
889	      `[worker-output] ${workerId}: stream file is empty but artifact_streams.latest_seq=${dbLatestSeq}`
890	      + ` and no fallback source (gz/legacy/archive) holds the missing head.`
891	      + ` Resuming would strand ${dbLatestSeq} entries; resetting the cursor to 0 so numbering restarts at 1.`,
892	    );
893	    // Better to renumber from 1 than to leave a permanent unfillable hole.
894	    await setStreamCursor(runId, workerId, 0, null);
895	    return;
896	  }
897	
898	  const renumbered = recovered.map((entry, index) => {
899	    const { seq: _dropped, ...rest } = entry as WorkerEntry & { seq?: number };
900	    return compactEntryForHistory({ ...rest, seq: index + 1 } as unknown as CompactableEntry) as unknown as WorkerEntry;
901	  });
902	
903	  const body = renumbered.map((entry) => JSON.stringify(entry)).join("\n") + "\n";
904	  const tmpPath = `${paths.filePath}.heal-${process.pid}-${(tmpCounter += 1)}.tmp`;
905	  const handle = await fs.open(tmpPath, "w");
906	  try {
907	    await handle.writeFile(body, "utf8");
908	    await handle.sync();
909	  } finally {
910	    await handle.close();
911	  }
912	  await fs.rename(tmpPath, paths.filePath);
913	
914	  // Set the cursor directly: the recovered transcript is authoritative, and it
915	  // is usually SHORTER than the stale cursor (streaming chunks collapse into
916	  // assembled entries). `commitArtifactAppend` never moves a cursor backward,
917	  // so using it here would leave the writer numbering above the file again.
918	  const last = renumbered.at(-1);
919	  await setStreamCursor(runId, workerId, renumbered.length, last?.id ?? `seq-${renumbered.length}`);
920	
921	  // Force the next refreshChainCaches to re-read from the healed file.
922	  const key = chainKey(runId, workerId);
923	  nextSeqByKey.delete(key);
924	  seenIdsByKey.delete(key);
925	  fingerprintsByKey.delete(key);
926	  fileStateByKey.delete(key);
927	
928	  emitNamedEvent({
929	    kind: "worker.stream_head_healed",
930	    runId,
931	    workerId,
932	    expectedLatestSeq: dbLatestSeq,
933	    recoveredEntries: renumbered.length,
934	  });
935	  console.warn(
936	    `[worker-output] ${workerId}: recovered ${renumbered.length} stranded entries into an empty stream file`
937	    + ` (cursor was at ${dbLatestSeq}); transcript head preserved.`,
938	  );
939	}
940	
941	/**
942	 * Read the agent runtime's raw output archive for a worker and reassemble it
943	 * into worker-stream shape.
944	 *
945	 * The runtime writes every bridge record here as it streams, so this file
946	 * outlives stream-file damage. Its records are per-chunk, so streaming deltas
947	 * must be re-joined (see `reassembleArchivedEntries`) or the transcript comes
948	 * back shredded mid-word.
949	 */
950	async function readFromRuntimeOutputArchive(workerId: string): Promise<WorkerEntry[]> {
951	  const archivePath = path.join(
952	    process.cwd(),
953	    ".omniharness",
954	    "agent-runtime-output",
955	    `${workerId}.jsonl`,
956	  );
957	  let body: string;
958	  try {
959	    body = await fs.readFile(archivePath, "utf8");
960	  } catch {
961	    return [];
962	  }
963	  const parsed = parseWorkerEntryLines(body).filter((entry) => entry.id !== "output-archive-marker");
964	  if (parsed.length === 0) {
965	    return [];
966	  }
967	  const { reassembleArchivedEntries } = await import("@/server/agent-runtime/output-store");
968	  return reassembleArchivedEntries(parsed);
969	}
970	
971	/**
972	 * Force `artifact_streams.latest_seq` to match what is actually on disk.
973	 *
974	 * `commitArtifactAppend` deliberately never moves a cursor backward, which is
975	 * right for the append path but wrong for repair — after healing, the file is
976	 * authoritative and may legitimately be shorter than the stale cursor. This
977	 * writes the row directly and drops the chain caches so the next write re-reads
978	 * from the healed file.
979	 */
980	async function setStreamCursor(
981	  runId: string,
982	  workerId: string,
983	  latestSeq: number,
984	  latestRecordId: string | null,
985	): Promise<void> {
986	  try {
987	    const [{ db }, { artifactStreams }, { and: andOp, eq: eqOp }] = await Promise.all([
988	      import("@/server/db"),
989	      import("@/server/db/schema"),
990	      import("drizzle-orm"),
991	    ]);
992	    await db
993	      .update(artifactStreams)
994	      .set({ latestSeq, latestRecordId, updatedAt: new Date() })
995	      .where(
996	        andOp(
997	          eqOp(artifactStreams.runId, runId),
998	          eqOp(artifactStreams.kind, "worker_entries"),
999	          eqOp(artifactStreams.ownerId, workerId),
1000	        ),
1001	      );
1002	  } catch {
1003	    // Non-fatal: the append still proceeds, just at the stale cursor.
1004	  }
1005	  const key = chainKey(runId, workerId);
1006	  nextSeqByKey.delete(key);
1007	  seenIdsByKey.delete(key);
1008	  fingerprintsByKey.delete(key);
1009	  fileStateByKey.delete(key);
1010	}
1011	
1012	/**
1013	 * Append a single worker entry to the JSONL file. The writer assigns
1014	 * `seq` from the in-memory cursor (seeded from the file tail on first
1015	 * use). Acquires the per-worker chain so it cannot interleave with
1016	 * compaction/expand/delete.
1017	 *
1018	 * If `entry.id` is already present on disk (or in the seen-ids cache),
1019	 * the call is a no-op and the previously persisted entry is returned
1020	 * with `appended: false` so wake-up publishers do not re-announce old seqs.
1021	 */
1022	export type AppendWorkerEntryResult = {
1023	  entry: WorkerEntry;
1024	  appended: boolean;
1025	};
1026	
1027	export async function appendWorkerEntryWithResult(
1028	  runId: string,
1029	  workerId: string,
1030	  entry: Omit<WorkerEntry, "seq">,
1031	): Promise<AppendWorkerEntryResult> {
1032	  return runOnChain(runId, workerId, async () => {
1033	    return withWorkerFileLock(runId, workerId, async (paths) => {
1034	      // If a compaction race somehow stranded the plaintext file under .gz,
1035	      // expand it first so cache refresh sees the live transcript rather
1036	      // than the lock-created empty placeholder.
1037	      await expandWorkerOutputFileInternal(paths);
1038	      // Never resume numbering above a head that isn't on disk.
1039	      await healStrandedStreamHead(runId, workerId, paths);
1040	
1041	      const { nextSeq, seen, fingerprints } = await refreshChainCaches(runId, workerId);
1042	      if (entry.id && seen.has(entry.id)) {
1043	        // Already persisted; load it back so the caller has a stable
1044	        // record. Linear scan is fine: this is the dedup path and runs at
1045	        // append cadence, not read cadence.
1046	        const persisted = await readCanonicalPersistedEntries(runId, workerId);
1047	        const match = persisted.find((line) => line.id === entry.id);
1048	        if (match && typeof match.seq === "number") {
1049	          return { entry: match, appended: false };
1050	        }
1051	        // Legacy line without seq: assign one virtually based on file
1052	        // position. The writer doesn't advance nextSeq in this case.
1053	        return { entry: { ...(entry as WorkerEntry), seq: 0 }, appended: false };
1054	      }
1055	
1056	      const persistedEntry: WorkerEntry = {
1057	        ...(entry as WorkerEntry),
1058	        seq: nextSeq,
1059	      };
1060	      const compact = compactEntryForHistory(persistedEntry as unknown as CompactableEntry) as unknown as WorkerEntry;
1061	      const line = JSON.stringify(compact) + "\n";
1062	
1063	      const target = paths.filePath;
1064	      const newlineGuard = await needsLeadingNewline(target);
1065	      // Capture pre-append size so we can record the byte offset of the
1066	      // new line in the sparse seq→offset index. The newline-guard byte
1067	      // (if any) is written before the JSON line, so the line starts one
1068	      // byte past sizeBefore in that case.
1069	      let sizeBefore = 0;
1070	      try {
1071	        sizeBefore = (await fs.stat(target)).size;
1072	      } catch (error) {
1073	        if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "ENOENT") throw error;
1074	      }
1075	      const lineStartOffset = sizeBefore + (newlineGuard ? 1 : 0);
1076	      const handle = await fs.open(target, "a");
1077	      try {
1078	        await handle.appendFile(newlineGuard ? "\n" + line : line, "utf8");
1079	        await handle.sync();
1080	      } finally {
1081	        await handle.close();
1082	      }
1083	
1084	      if (shouldIndex(persistedEntry.seq)) {
1085	        // Best-effort: appendIndexEntry swallows its own errors. The
1086	        // index is opportunistic — a failure here only slows future tail
1087	        // reads until the next rebuild.
1088	        await appendIndexEntry(target, {
1089	          seq: persistedEntry.seq,
1090	          offset: lineStartOffset,
1091	        });
1092	      }
1093	
1094	      if (entry.id) {
1095	        seen.add(entry.id);
1096	        fingerprints.set(entry.id, workerEntryFingerprint(compact));
1097	      }
1098	      nextSeqByKey.set(chainKey(runId, workerId), nextSeq + 1);
1099	      await rememberWorkerFileState(runId, workerId);
1100	      const { commitArtifactAppend } = await import("@/server/artifacts/stream-metadata");
1101	      await commitArtifactAppend({
1102	        streamId: { runId, kind: "worker_entries", ownerId: workerId },
1103	        seq: compact.seq,
1104	        recordId: compact.id ?? `seq-${compact.seq}`,
1105	      });
1106	      return { entry: compact, appended: true };
1107	    });
1108	  });
1109	}
1110	
1111	export async function appendWorkerEntry(
1112	  runId: string,
1113	  workerId: string,
1114	  entry: Omit<WorkerEntry, "seq">,
1115	): Promise<WorkerEntry> {
1116	  return (await appendWorkerEntryWithResult(runId, workerId, entry)).entry;
1117	}
1118	
1119	/**
1120	 * Diff-and-append a batch of bridge-emitted output entries. Exact
1121	 * replays are skipped, but changed records with the same bridge id are
1122	 * appended as revisions. Streaming assistant prose can arrive first as
1123	 * "One" and later as the complete paragraph; the append-only stream must
1124	 * publish that later revision with a fresh seq so clients repaint.
1125	 *
1126	 * Existing callers (`persistWorkerSnapshot`) get the same observable
1127	 * behavior as the old overwrite-on-snapshot path with no file rewrite.
1128	 */
1129	export async function writeWorkerOutputEntries(
1130	  runId: string,
1131	  workerId: string,
1132	  entries: AgentRecord["outputEntries"],
1133	) {
1134	  if (!Array.isArray(entries) || entries.length === 0) {
1135	    return;
1136	  }
1137	
1138	  await runOnChain(runId, workerId, async () => {
1139	    await withWorkerFileLock(runId, workerId, async (paths) => {
1140	      await expandWorkerOutputFileInternal(paths);
1141	      // Never resume numbering above a head that isn't on disk.
1142	      await healStrandedStreamHead(runId, workerId, paths);
1143	
1144	      const { fingerprints } = await refreshChainCaches(runId, workerId);
1145	      const newEntries: AgentOutputEntry[] = [];
1146	      const acceptedFingerprintsById = new Map<string, string>();
1147	      for (const entry of entries) {
1148	        if (!entry) continue;
1149	        // The agent-runtime live view prepends a synthetic archive marker
1150	        // ("X older raw worker activity records are only in archived
1151	        // history…") when entries have been pruned past the live window.
1152	        // It is a render-time hint, not real conversation content — never
1153	        // persist it into the unified worker stream.
1154	        if (entry.id === "output-archive-marker") {
1155	          continue;
1156	        }
1157	        // Real bridge entries always carry an id; tooling/migration code that
1158	        // hands us synthetic snapshots may not. In that case, treat the entry
1159	        // as opaque and always append (no dedup possible).
1160	        //
1161	        // Dedup keyed on (id, fingerprint): re-writing the exact same
1162	        // entry is a no-op, but a streaming chunk that grew between two
1163	        // persistWorkerSnapshot calls produces a second row with the
1164	        // same id and a longer text — the FE is expected to coalesce by
1165	        // id and render the latest revision. (Test:
1166	        // `appends changed bridge message revisions so streaming prose can expand`.)
1167	        const id = typeof entry.id === "string" && entry.id ? entry.id : null;
1168	        if (id) {
1169	          const fingerprint = bridgeOutputEntryFingerprint(entry);
1170	          if (
1171	            fingerprints.get(id) === fingerprint
1172	            || acceptedFingerprintsById.get(id) === fingerprint
1173	          ) {
1174	            continue;
1175	          }
1176	          acceptedFingerprintsById.set(id, fingerprint);
1177	        }
1178	        newEntries.push(entry);
1179	      }
1180	      if (newEntries.length === 0) {
1181	        return;
1182	      }
1183	
1184	      const key = chainKey(runId, workerId);
1185	      let nextSeq = nextSeqByKey.get(key);
1186	      if (nextSeq === undefined) {
1187	        // refreshChainCaches just populated it; refuse to silently lose entries.
1188	        throw new Error(`writeWorkerOutputEntries: missing nextSeq for ${key}`);
1189	      }
1190	
1191	      const target = paths.filePath;
1192	      const newlineGuard = await needsLeadingNewline(target);
1193	
1194	      const lines: string[] = [];
1195	      const appendedEntries: WorkerEntry[] = [];
1196	      for (const entry of newEntries) {
1197	        const id = typeof entry.id === "string" && entry.id ? entry.id : `synthetic-${nextSeq}`;
1198	        // Preserve input shape: spread the entry first, then layer on
1199	        // id/seq. Adding null placeholders for absent optional fields
1200	        // would change the on-disk shape callers expect to round-trip.
1201	        const promoted = {
1202	          ...(entry as unknown as Record<string, unknown>),
1203	          id,
1204	          seq: nextSeq,
1205	        };
1206	        const compact = compactEntryForHistory(promoted as unknown as CompactableEntry) as unknown as WorkerEntry;
1207	        lines.push(JSON.stringify(compact));
1208	        appendedEntries.push(compact);
1209	        fingerprints.set(id, workerEntryFingerprint(compact));
1210	        nextSeq += 1;
1211	      }
1212	      const body = lines.join("\n") + "\n";
1213	
1214	      const handle = await fs.open(target, "a");
1215	      try {
1216	        await handle.appendFile(newlineGuard ? "\n" + body : body, "utf8");
1217	        await handle.sync();
1218	      } finally {
1219	        await handle.close();
1220	      }
1221	      nextSeqByKey.set(key, nextSeq);
1222	      await rememberWorkerFileState(runId, workerId);
1223	      const latestEntry = appendedEntries.at(-1);
1224	      if (latestEntry && typeof latestEntry.seq === "number" && Number.isFinite(latestEntry.seq)) {
1225	        const { commitArtifactAppend } = await import("@/server/artifacts/stream-metadata");
1226	        await commitArtifactAppend({
1227	          streamId: { runId, kind: "worker_entries", ownerId: workerId },
1228	          seq: latestEntry.seq,
1229	          recordId: latestEntry.id ?? `seq-${latestEntry.seq}`,
1230	        });
1231	      }
1232	      // One wake-up per append batch, not per entry. The frame only carries a
1233	      // cursor — the client responds by pulling everything after its own last
1234	      // seq — so the highest seq in the batch describes the batch completely.
1235	      // Emitting per entry multiplied the wake-up rate (and, before snapshot
1236	      // pacing, the full-rebuild rate) by the batch size for no added
1237	      // information.
1238	      let highestAppendedSeq = 0;
1239	      for (const entry of appendedEntries) {
1240	        if (typeof entry.seq === "number" && Number.isFinite(entry.seq) && entry.seq > highestAppendedSeq) {
1241	          highestAppendedSeq = entry.seq;
1242	        }
1243	      }
1244	      if (highestAppendedSeq > 0) {
1245	        emitNamedEvent({
1246	          kind: "worker.entry_appended",
1247	          runId,
1248	          workerId,
1249	          seq: highestAppendedSeq,
1250	        });
1251	      }
1252	    });
1253	  });
1254	}
1255	
1256	/**
1257	 * If the file ends with bytes that are not a newline (e.g. a previous
1258	 * append was truncated mid-line by a crash), the next append must start
1259	 * with a newline so the truncated line stays a separate broken line
1260	 * rather than being concatenated to our new JSON object. `parseWorkerEntryLines`
1261	 * already tolerates malformed lines, so isolating the broken bytes is
1262	 * enough to recover.
1263	 */
1264	async function needsLeadingNewline(target: string): Promise<boolean> {
1265	  try {
1266	    const stat = await fs.stat(target);
1267	    if (stat.size === 0) {
1268	      return false;
1269	    }
1270	    const handle = await fs.open(target, "r");
1271	    try {
1272	      const buf = Buffer.alloc(1);
1273	      await handle.read(buf, 0, 1, stat.size - 1);
1274	      return buf[0] !== 0x0a;
1275	    } finally {
1276	      await handle.close();
1277	    }
1278	  } catch (error) {
1279	    if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT") {
1280	      return false;
1281	    }
1282	    throw error;
1283	  }
1284	}
1285	
1286	function parseWorkerEntryLines(body: string): WorkerEntry[] {
1287	  const out: WorkerEntry[] = [];
1288	  for (const line of body.split("\n")) {
1289	    const trimmed = line.trim();
1290	    if (!trimmed) continue;
1291	    try {
1292	      const parsed = JSON.parse(trimmed);
1293	      if (parsed && typeof parsed === "object") {
1294	        out.push(parsed as WorkerEntry);
1295	      }
1296	    } catch {
1297	      // skip malformed line (e.g. truncated last line after crash)
1298	    }
1299	  }
1300	  return out;
1301	}
1302	
1303	async function readFromCompressedFile(compressed: string): Promise<WorkerEntry[]> {
1304	  try {
1305	    const buf = await fs.readFile(compressed);
1306	    return parseWorkerEntryLines(gunzipSync(buf).toString("utf8"));
1307	  } catch (error) {
1308	    if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT") {
1309	      return [];
1310	    }
1311	    throw error;
1312	  }
1313	}
1314	
1315	async function readFromLegacyWorkerFiles(runId: string, workerId: string): Promise<WorkerEntry[]> {
1316	  const filePath = legacyWorkerFilePath(runId, workerId);
1317	  try {
1318	    const body = await fs.readFile(filePath, "utf8");
1319	    const parsed = parseWorkerEntryLines(body);
1320	    if (body.trim() || parsed.length > 0) {
1321	      return parsed;
1322	    }
1323	  } catch (error) {
1324	    if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "ENOENT") {
1325	      throw error;
1326	    }
1327	  }
1328	  return readFromCompressedFile(legacyWorkerCompressedFilePath(runId, workerId));
1329	}
1330	
1331	async function readFromArchive(runId: string, workerId: string): Promise<WorkerEntry[]> {
1332	  const archive = runArchivePath(runId);
1333	  if (!existsSync(archive)) {
1334	    return [];
1335	  }
1336	  try {
1337	    const zip = new AdmZip(archive);
1338	    const entry = zip.getEntry(`${runId}/${workerId}.jsonl`);
1339	    if (!entry) {
1340	      return [];
1341	    }
1342	    return parseWorkerEntryLines(entry.getData().toString("utf8"));
1343	  } catch {
1344	    return [];
1345	  }
1346	}
1347	
1348	async function readLegacyDbEntries(workerId: string): Promise<WorkerEntry[]> {
1349	  // Lazy import to avoid a circular dependency through @/server/db.
1350	  const { db } = await import("@/server/db");
1351	  const { workers } = await import("@/server/db/schema");
1352	  const { eq } = await import("drizzle-orm");
1353	  const row = await db
1354	    .select({ outputEntriesJson: workers.outputEntriesJson })
1355	    .from(workers)
1356	    .where(eq(workers.id, workerId))
1357	    .get();
1358	  return parseLegacyOutputEntriesJson(row?.outputEntriesJson) as unknown as WorkerEntry[];
1359	}

1362	 * Read all worker entries. Legacy entries without `seq` are assigned
1363	 * monotonic virtual seqs in file order in-memory only — the file is not
1364	 * rewritten on read. The first compaction sweep that touches the file

1366	 */
1367	export async function readWorkerOutputEntries(
1368	  runId: string,

1370	): Promise<OutputEntry[]> {
1371	  const entries = await readCanonicalPersistedEntries(runId, workerId);
1372	  return entries as unknown as OutputEntry[];

1375	/**

224	// large file — so the frontend then sees oldText === newText and renders no
225	// diff pane at all. Strip the common leading and trailing lines before
226	// truncation so the persisted oldText/newText retain their actual
227	// divergence, with a few lines of context on each side.
228	function compressDiffContentPair(record: Record<string, unknown>): Record<string, unknown> | null {
229	  const type = typeof record.type === "string" ? record.type : null;

232	  }

238	
239	  const oldLines = oldText.split("\n");
240	  const newLines = newText.split("\n");
241	  const maxPrefix = Math.min(oldLines.length, newLines.length);

269	  const headLines = oldLines.slice(prefix - keptPrefix, prefix);

275	  const tailSuffixMarker = droppedSuffix > 0 ? `[${droppedSuffix} unchanged lines omitted]` : null;

422	export function compactEntryForHistory<T extends CompactableEntry>(entry: T): T {

432	  if (entry.raw === undefined && text === entry.text) {
433	    return entry;
434	  }
435	  return {
436	    ...entry,
437	    text,
438	    raw: entry.raw === undefined ? undefined : compactHistoryRawValue(entry.raw),
439	  };

2375	async function compactWorkerOutputFileInternal(paths: WorkerStreamPaths): Promise<boolean> {
2376	  const source = paths.filePath;
2377	  let body: Buffer;
2378	  try {
2379	    body = await fs.readFile(source);
2380	  } catch (error) {
2381	    if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT") {
2382	      return false;
2383	    }
2384	    throw error;
2385	  }
2386	  // Rewrite the on-disk lines with their resolved seqs so legacy
2387	  // pre-seq entries pick up durable sequence numbers in the gzip.
2388	  const parsed = parseWorkerEntryLines(body.toString("utf8"));
2389	  const withSeqs = normalizePersistedEntries(parsed);
2390	  const normalizedBody = Buffer.from(withSeqs.map((entry) => JSON.stringify(entry)).join("\n") + (withSeqs.length > 0 ? "\n" : ""), "utf8");
2391	
2392	  const target = paths.compressedFilePath;
2393	  tmpCounter = (tmpCounter + 1) >>> 0;
2394	  const tmp = `${target}.tmp-${process.pid}-${Date.now()}-${tmpCounter}`;
2395	  const compressed = gzipSync(normalizedBody);
2396	  try {
2397	    await fs.writeFile(tmp, compressed);
2398	    await fs.rename(tmp, target);
2399	  } catch (error) {
2400	    fs.unlink(tmp).catch(() => undefined);
2401	    throw error;
2402	  }
2403	  await fs.unlink(source).catch(() => undefined);
2404	  // Caches stay valid: compaction preserves seqs and ids; the file just
2405	  // moves from .jsonl to .gz. Caller holds the per-worker chain so no
2406	  // append is concurrent with this move.
2407	  return true;
2408	}
2409	
2410	async function expandWorkerOutputFileInternal(paths: WorkerStreamPaths): Promise<boolean> {
2411	  const source = paths.compressedFilePath;
2412	  let body: Buffer;
2413	  try {
2414	    body = await fs.readFile(source);
2415	  } catch (error) {
2416	    if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT") {
2417	      return false;
2418	    }
2419	    throw error;
2420	  }
2421	  const decompressed = gunzipSync(body);
2422	  const target = paths.filePath;
2423	  tmpCounter = (tmpCounter + 1) >>> 0;
2424	  const tmp = `${target}.tmp-${process.pid}-${Date.now()}-${tmpCounter}`;
2425	  try {
2426	    await fs.writeFile(tmp, decompressed);
2427	    await fs.rename(tmp, target);
2428	  } catch (error) {
2429	    fs.unlink(tmp).catch(() => undefined);
2430	    throw error;
2431	  }
2432	  await fs.unlink(source).catch(() => undefined);
2433	  // Caches stay valid: the bytes are the same, only the file shape changed.
2434	  return true;
2435	}

2440	    if (!existsSync(paths.filePath)) {
2441	      return false;
2442	    }
2443	    return withWorkerFileLock(runId, workerId, (lockedPaths) => compactWorkerOutputFileInternal(lockedPaths));
2444	  });
2445	}

2448	  return runOnChain(runId, workerId, () =>

2469	/**
2470	 * Per-worker gzip sweep. Compacts any plaintext `.jsonl` whose worker is in a
2471	 * terminal status and whose file hasn't been touched for at least
2472	 * `minAgeMs`. Idempotent — safe to call repeatedly.
2473	 *
2474	 * Acquires the per-worker write chain before touching each file. An
2475	 * earlier version of this sweep mutated files outside the chain, which
2476	 * could drop an append racing the rename; that latent loss bug is fixed
2477	 * here.
2478	 */
2479	export async function compactStaleWorkerOutputs(options: {
2480	  minAgeMs?: number;
2481	  now?: number;
2482	} = {}): Promise<{ compacted: Array<{ runId: string; workerId: string }> }> {
2483	  const minAgeMs = options.minAgeMs ?? WORKER_COMPACTION_MIN_AGE_MS;
2484	  const now = options.now ?? Date.now();
2485	  const root = runDataDir();
2486	  let runDirs: string[];
2487	  try {
2488	    runDirs = await fs.readdir(root);
2489	  } catch (error) {
2490	    if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT") {
2491	      return { compacted: [] };
2492	    }
2493	    throw error;
2494	  }
2495	
2496	  const { db } = await import("@/server/db");
2497	  const { workers } = await import("@/server/db/schema");
2498	  const { inArray } = await import("drizzle-orm");
2499	
2500	  // Collect candidate (runId, workerId) pairs from disk before hitting the DB.
2501	  const candidates: Array<{ runId: string; workerId: string; filePath: string }> = [];
2502	  for (const entry of runDirs) {
2503	    const runPath = path.join(root, entry);
2504	    let stat;
2505	    try {
2506	      stat = await fs.stat(runPath);
2507	    } catch {
2508	      continue;
2509	    }
2510	    if (!stat.isDirectory()) continue;
2511	    // Legacy transcripts sit directly in the run directory; once a run has an
2512	    // `artifact_streams` row they are written to a `workers/` subdirectory
2513	    // instead. Scanning only the flat layout meant no modern transcript was
2514	    // ever swept, so run-data grew without bound.
2515	    for (const dir of [runPath, path.join(runPath, WORKER_STREAM_DIR_NAME)]) {
2516	      let workerFiles: string[];
2517	      try {
2518	        workerFiles = await fs.readdir(dir);
2519	      } catch {
2520	        continue;
2521	      }
2522	      for (const file of workerFiles) {
2523	        if (!file.endsWith(".jsonl")) continue;
2524	        const filePath = path.join(dir, file);
2525	        let fileStat;
2526	        try {
2527	          fileStat = await fs.stat(filePath);
2528	        } catch {
2529	          continue;
2530	        }
2531	        if (!fileStat.isFile()) continue;
2532	        if (now - fileStat.mtimeMs < minAgeMs) continue;
2533	        candidates.push({
2534	          runId: entry,
2535	          workerId: file.slice(0, -".jsonl".length),
2536	          filePath,
2537	        });
2538	      }
2539	    }
2540	  }
2541	
2542	  if (candidates.length === 0) {
2543	    return { compacted: [] };
2544	  }
2545	
2546	  const workerIds = candidates.map((c) => c.workerId);
2547	  const rows: Array<{ id: string; status: string }> = workerIds.length > 0
2548	    ? await db.select({ id: workers.id, status: workers.status })
2549	        .from(workers)
2550	        .where(inArray(workers.id, workerIds))
2551	    : [];
2552	  const statusByWorker = new Map(rows.map((r) => [r.id, (r.status ?? "").toLowerCase()]));
2553	
2554	  const compacted: Array<{ runId: string; workerId: string }> = [];
2555	  for (const candidate of candidates) {
2556	    const status = (statusByWorker.get(candidate.workerId) ?? "").split(":")[0]?.trim() ?? "";
2557	    if (!TERMINAL_WORKER_STATUSES.has(status)) continue;
2558	    try {
2559	      const ok = await compactWorkerOutputFile(candidate.runId, candidate.workerId);
2560	      if (ok) compacted.push({ runId: candidate.runId, workerId: candidate.workerId });
2561	    } catch (error) {
2562	      console.warn(
2563	        `Failed to compact worker output ${candidate.runId}/${candidate.workerId}:`,
2564	        error,
2565	      );
2566	    }
2567	  }
2568	  return { compacted };
2569	}

1	import path from "node:path";

8	const MAX_WORKER_CONTENT_BYTES = 25 * 1024 * 1024;
9	const SUPPORTED_IMAGE_MIME_TYPES = new Set([
10	  "image/gif",
11	  "image/jpeg",
12	  "image/png",
13	  "image/webp",

16	export class WorkerEntryContentError extends Error {
17	  constructor(
18	    message: string,
19	    readonly status: number,
20	  ) {
21	    super(message);
22	    this.name = "WorkerEntryContentError";
23	  }
24	}
25	
26	function asRecord(value: unknown): Record<string, unknown> | null {
27	  return typeof value === "object" && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value)
28	    ? value as Record<string, unknown>
29	    : null;
30	}

32	function findLatestEntryById<T extends { id?: string }>(entries: T[], entryId: string): T | null {
33	  for (let index = entries.length - 1; index >= 0; index -= 1) {
34	    if (entries[index]?.id === entryId) {
35	      return entries[index] ?? null;
36	    }
37	  }
38	  return null;
39	}
40	
41	function decodeCompleteBase64(data: string): Buffer | null {
42	  if (/\[(?:truncated|\d+ characters omitted)/i.test(data)) {
43	    return null;
44	  }
45	  const normalized = data.replace(/\s+/g, "");
46	  if (!normalized || !/^[A-Za-z0-9+/]*={0,2}$/.test(normalized)) {
47	    return null;
48	  }
49	  const body = Buffer.from(normalized, "base64");
50	  return body.length > 0 ? body : null;
51	}
52	
53	async function readCodexGeneratedImage(cwd: string, uri: string): Promise<Buffer> {
54	  const generatedImagesRoot = path.join(
55	    cwd,
56	    ".omniharness",
57	    "cli-home",
58	    "codex",
59	    "home",
60	    "generated_images",
61	  );
62	  const [canonicalRoot, canonicalTarget] = await Promise.all([
63	    fs.realpath(generatedImagesRoot),
64	    fs.realpath(uri),
65	  ]);
66	  const relative = path.relative(canonicalRoot, canonicalTarget);
67	  if (!relative || relative === ".." || relative.startsWith(`..${path.sep}`) || path.isAbsolute(relative)) {
68	    throw new WorkerEntryContentError("Generated image path is outside the worker content root.", 403);
69	  }
70	  const handle = await fs.open(canonicalTarget, constants.O_RDONLY | constants.O_NOFOLLOW);
71	  try {
72	    const info = await handle.stat();
73	    if (!info.isFile()) {
74	      throw new WorkerEntryContentError("Generated image is not a regular file.", 404);
75	    }
76	    if (info.size > MAX_WORKER_CONTENT_BYTES) {
77	      throw new WorkerEntryContentError("Generated image exceeds the supported size limit.", 413);
78	    }
79	
80	    // Re-resolve after opening, then prove the checked pathname still names
81	    // the exact inode held by the descriptor. This closes the check/read race:
82	    // later path replacement cannot redirect reads from the already-open fd.
83	    const canonicalAfterOpen = await fs.realpath(canonicalTarget);
84	    const relativeAfterOpen = path.relative(canonicalRoot, canonicalAfterOpen);
85	    if (
86	      !relativeAfterOpen
87	      || relativeAfterOpen === ".."
88	      || relativeAfterOpen.startsWith(`..${path.sep}`)
89	      || path.isAbsolute(relativeAfterOpen)
90	    ) {
91	      throw new WorkerEntryContentError("Generated image path changed outside the worker content root.", 403);
92	    }
93	    const pathInfo = await fs.stat(canonicalAfterOpen);
94	    if (pathInfo.dev !== info.dev || pathInfo.ino !== info.ino) {
95	      throw new WorkerEntryContentError("Generated image changed while it was being opened.", 409);
96	    }
97	    return handle.readFile();
98	  } finally {
99	    await handle.close();
100	  }
101	}

103	export async function readWorkerEntryContent(workerId: string, entryId: string) {
104	  const worker = await db.select({
105	    id: workers.id,
106	    runId: workers.runId,
107	    type: workers.type,
108	    cwd: workers.cwd,
109	  }).from(workers).where(eq(workers.id, workerId)).get();
110	  if (!worker) {
111	    throw new WorkerEntryContentError("Worker not found.", 404);
112	  }
113	
114	  const entries = await readWorkerOutputEntries(worker.runId, worker.id);
115	  const entry = findLatestEntryById(entries, entryId);
116	  const raw = asRecord(entry?.raw);
117	  const content = asRecord(raw?.content);
118	  if (entry?.type !== "agent_content" || content?.type !== "image") {
119	    throw new WorkerEntryContentError("Generated image entry not found.", 404);
120	  }
121	
122	  const mimeType = typeof content.mimeType === "string" ? content.mimeType.toLowerCase() : "image/png";
123	  if (!SUPPORTED_IMAGE_MIME_TYPES.has(mimeType)) {
124	    throw new WorkerEntryContentError("Generated image type is not supported.", 415);
125	  }
126	
127	  const inlineBody = typeof content.data === "string" ? decodeCompleteBase64(content.data) : null;
128	  if (inlineBody) {
129	    if (inlineBody.length > MAX_WORKER_CONTENT_BYTES) {
130	      throw new WorkerEntryContentError("Generated image exceeds the supported size limit.", 413);
131	    }
132	    return { body: inlineBody, mimeType };
133	  }
134	
135	  if (worker.type !== "codex" || typeof content.uri !== "string" || !path.isAbsolute(content.uri)) {
136	    throw new WorkerEntryContentError("Generated image data is unavailable.", 404);
137	  }
138	
139	  try {
140	    return {
141	      body: await readCodexGeneratedImage(worker.cwd, content.uri),
142	      mimeType,
143	    };
144	  } catch (error) {
145	    if (error instanceof WorkerEntryContentError) {
146	      throw error;
147	    }

1	type LiveUpdateListener = () => void;
2	type EventStreamWaitResult = {
3	  notified: boolean;
4	};
5	
6	const listeners = new Set<LiveUpdateListener>();
7	let notificationVersion = 0;
8	let snapshotVersion = 0;
9	
10	/**
11	 * Wake the SSE streams.
12	 *
13	 * `snapshotRelevant: false` means "this change is fully described by the named
14	 * event frame itself; the client does not need a rebuilt snapshot to learn
15	 * about it". Worker transcript appends are the motivating case: content
16	 * reaches the client through `GET /api/workers/:id/entries`, driven by the
17	 * `worker.entry_appended` wake-up frame. Forcing a full snapshot rebuild for
18	 * every appended entry meant one streamed token cost every connected client a
19	 * ~40-query rebuild and a multi-hundred-KB payload, which is what made the UI
20	 * unusable once several sessions streamed at once.
21	 *
22	 * Delta-only wakes still bump `notificationVersion`, so parked streams wake
23	 * promptly and flush their cheap named frames. They just do not force the
24	 * expensive rebuild onto the critical path.
25	 */
26	export function notifyEventStreamSubscribers(options?: { snapshotRelevant?: boolean }) {
27	  notificationVersion += 1;
28	  if (options?.snapshotRelevant !== false) {
29	    snapshotVersion += 1;
30	  }
31	  for (const listener of listeners) {
32	    listener();
33	  }
34	}
35	
36	export function getEventStreamNotificationVersion() {
37	  return notificationVersion;
38	}
39	
40	/**
41	 * Advances only for changes that a client cannot learn about from a named
42	 * event frame alone. The SSE loop uses this to decide whether a wake-up
43	 * justifies rebuilding the snapshot.
44	 */
45	export function getEventStreamSnapshotVersion() {
46	  return snapshotVersion;
47	}
48	
49	export function waitForEventStreamNotification(timeoutMs: number, afterVersion = notificationVersion) {
50	  return new Promise<EventStreamWaitResult>((resolve) => {
51	    if (notificationVersion > afterVersion) {
52	      resolve({ notified: true });
53	      return;
54	    }
55	
56	    let settled = false;
57	    let timeout: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;
58	
59	    const cleanup = (result: EventStreamWaitResult) => {
60	      if (settled) {
61	        return;
62	      }
63	
64	      settled = true;
65	      if (timeout) {
66	        clearTimeout(timeout);
67	      }
68	      listeners.delete(listener);
69	      resolve(result);
70	    };
71	
72	    const listener = () => cleanup({ notified: true });
73	    listeners.add(listener);
74	    timeout = setTimeout(() => cleanup({ notified: false }), timeoutMs);
75	  });
76	}
77

1	/**
2	 * Single source of truth for the unified worker conversation stream on
3	 * the frontend.
4	 *
5	 * Per (workerId) we hold a contiguous *window* [lowestSeq..latestContiguousSeq]:
6	 *   - entries: the contiguous window in seq order, never with gaps.
7	 *   - lowestSeq: the lowest seq currently in `entries` (0 if empty).
8	 *   - latestContiguousSeq: the highest seq we have proven contiguous
9	 *     from lowestSeq. Used as the cursor for forward refetch.
10	 *   - latestKnownSeq: the highest seq the server has told us exists.
11	 *     Updates immediately on a wake-up frame; the fetch fills the gap.
12	 *   - hasOlder: true if older entries exist on the server before
13	 *     lowestSeq. Set by initial tail-load and `loadOlder`.
14	 *   - status: "idle" → "loading" → "loaded"; "error" on a fetch failure.
15	 *
16	 * Initial load fetches the last N entries via `?limit=N`. Forward
17	 * extension uses `?afterSeq=latestContiguousSeq`. Backward hydration
18	 * uses `?beforeSeq=lowestSeq&limit=N` via `loadOlder`.
19	 *
20	 * SSE frames carry only `{ workerId, seq }` — the content is fetched
21	 * via the HTTP endpoint. The frame is a wake-up hint; we never trust
22	 * it as the entry payload.
23	 */
24	import { useCallback, useEffect, useMemo, useSyncExternalStore } from "react";
25	import type { WorkerEntry } from "@/shared/worker-entries";
26	import type { RuntimeAPIs } from "@/runtime-api/types";
27	import { useRuntimeAPIs } from "@/runtime-api/provider";
28	
29	/**
30	 * Collapse multiple rows that share an entry `id` into a single
31	 * conversation item. The server's unified stream intentionally appends
32	 * a new row whenever an entry's text grows (so a streaming assistant
33	 * message can be revised in place — see the
34	 * `appends changed bridge message revisions so streaming prose can expand`
35	 * test in `tests/server/workers/output-store.test.ts`). It also
36	 * writes the same user_input id onto a newer worker's stream when a run
37	 * cycles through workers.
38	 *
39	 * Coalesce policy:
40	 *   - Keep the entry at its FIRST appearance in the input order, so
41	 *     conversational position doesn't jump when text grows.
42	 *   - Use the LATEST revision's text / metadata (text grows; latest
43	 *     revision is the finished one).
44	 *   - Take the timestamp from the copy on the NEWEST worker, because that
45	 *     is the one the live thread actually contains. Neither "earliest" nor
46	 *     "latest" works on its own: a rewind re-delivers a message with a new
47	 *     timestamp (newest is right, earliest pins it back above output it was
48	 *     rewound past), while `reconcileWorkerUserMessagesInStream` backfills
49	 *     history onto a fresh worker with the original `createdAt` (earliest is
50	 *     right, latest drags the opening prompt into the middle of the day).
51	 *     Worker order settles both. Copies from the same worker — streaming
52	 *     revisions, which share a timestamp anyway — keep the earliest.
53	 *   - Entries without an id pass through unchanged.
54	 *
55	 * `workerOrder` is the run's workers in creation order. Without it (a single
56	 * worker stream, or a caller that has no worker list yet) the earliest
57	 * timestamp is kept, which is the conservative choice: a message stays where
58	 * it already is.
59	 */
60	export function coalesceWorkerEntriesById(
61	  entries: ReadonlyArray<WorkerEntry>,
62	  workerOrder: ReadonlyArray<string> = [],
63	): WorkerEntry[] {
64	  const workerRank = new Map(workerOrder.map((workerId, index) => [workerId, index]));
65	  const rankOf = (entry: WorkerEntry) => {
66	    const workerId = (entry as { workerId?: unknown }).workerId;
67	    return typeof workerId === "string" ? workerRank.get(workerId) ?? -1 : -1;
68	  };
69	
70	  const positionByKey = new Map<string, number>();
71	  const placementByKey = new Map<string, { timestamp: string; rank: number }>();
72	  const result: WorkerEntry[] = [];
73	  for (const entry of entries) {
74	    const key = typeof entry.id === "string" && entry.id ? entry.id : null;
75	    if (!key) {
76	      result.push(entry);
77	      continue;
78	    }
79	    const existingPosition = positionByKey.get(key);
80	    if (existingPosition === undefined) {
81	      positionByKey.set(key, result.length);
82	      if (entry.timestamp) {
83	        placementByKey.set(key, { timestamp: entry.timestamp, rank: rankOf(entry) });
84	      }
85	      result.push(entry);
86	    } else {
87	      const held = placementByKey.get(key);
88	      const candidate = entry.timestamp;
89	      const candidateRank = rankOf(entry);
90	      let preservedTimestamp = candidate;
91	      if (held && candidate) {
92	        // Smaller ISO string compares as earlier datetime for the same
93	        // timezone offset — all our timestamps are emitted with a `Z` suffix,
94	        // so string compare matches Date compare.
95	        const keepCandidate = candidateRank > held.rank
96	          || (candidateRank === held.rank && candidate < held.timestamp);
97	        preservedTimestamp = keepCandidate ? candidate : held.timestamp;
98	        placementByKey.set(key, {
99	          timestamp: preservedTimestamp,
100	          rank: Math.max(candidateRank, held.rank),
101	        });
102	      } else if (held) {
103	        preservedTimestamp = held.timestamp;
104	      } else if (candidate) {
105	        placementByKey.set(key, { timestamp: candidate, rank: candidateRank });
106	      }
107	      result[existingPosition] = { ...entry, timestamp: preservedTimestamp };
108	    }
109	  }
110	  return result;
111	}
112	
113	type WorkerEntryStorage = Pick<Storage, "getItem" | "setItem" | "removeItem">;
114	
115	// v2: transcript recovery renumbered seqs and changed entry ids on repaired
116	// worker streams. This cache merges with server data by entry id, so a v1
117	// cache keeps rendering stale pre-repair entries (raw streaming fragments)
118	// alongside the fixed ones — a transcript shredded mid-word. Bumping the key
119	// retires those caches instead of requiring users to clear storage by hand.
120	const DEFAULT_STORAGE_KEY = "omni-worker-entries-cache:v2";
121	const DEFAULT_MAX_WORKERS = 16;
122	const DEFAULT_MAX_SERIALIZED_BYTES = 4_000_000;
123	const DEFAULT_TAIL_LIMIT = 100;
124	const DEFAULT_OLDER_LIMIT = 100;
125	// Entries are remembered on every append and every stream poll. Serializing
126	// the whole cache envelope synchronously on each one is main-thread work that
127	// competes with keystrokes, so the write is coalesced onto a trailing timer

129	const DEFAULT_FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS = 1_000;

133	  entries: WorkerEntry[];
134	}
135

139	}

141	export type WorkerStreamStatus = "idle" | "loading" | "loaded" | "error";
142	
143	export interface WorkerStreamState {

145	  entries: WorkerEntry[];

173	function latestSeq(entries: WorkerEntry[]) {
174	  return entries.reduce((latest, entry) => (

181	function initialState(

209	  // explicitly bounded consumers only.
