```text
285	    if (existing) {
286	      if (
287	        initialEntries.length > 0
288	        && existing.latestContiguousSeq === 0
289	        && existing.entries.length === 0
290	      ) {
291	        const hydrated = initialState(workerId, initialEntries);
292	        this.stateByWorker.set(workerId, hydrated);
293	        return hydrated;
294	      }
295	      return existing;
296	    }
297	    const cachedEntries = initialEntries.length > 0 ? [] : this.getCachedEntries(workerId);
298	    const rawNextEntries = initialEntries.length > 0 ? initialEntries : cachedEntries;
299	    // Trim to the tail window — see getCachedEntries for the rationale.
300	    // Same trim is applied to caller-supplied initialEntries (e.g. an
301	    // event-snapshot hydration) so a long transcript doesn't render in
302	    // full on mount.
303	    const nextEntries = rawNextEntries.length > DEFAULT_TAIL_LIMIT
304	      ? rawNextEntries.slice(-DEFAULT_TAIL_LIMIT)
305	      : rawNextEntries;
306	    const next = initialState(workerId, nextEntries, {
307	      needsTailValidation: initialEntries.length === 0 && cachedEntries.length > 0,
308	    });
309	    this.stateByWorker.set(workerId, next);
310	    if (nextEntries.length > 0) {
311	      this.rememberState(workerId, nextEntries);
312	    }
313	    return next;
314	  }
315	
316	  isLoaded(workerId: string): boolean {
317	    const state = this.stateByWorker.get(workerId);
318	    if (!state) {
319	      return false;
320	    }
321	    return (
322	      state.status === "loaded"
323	      && state.latestContiguousSeq === state.latestKnownSeq
324	      && !state.needsTailValidation
325	    );
326	  }
327	
328	  /**
329	   * Latched variant of `isLoaded`: once a worker's stream has completed a
330	   * validated load, this stays true through wake-up catch-up fetches and
331	   * refresh cycles. `isLoaded` flips false on every appended entry until the
332	   * gap is fetched, which makes it unusable as a render gate — anything
333	   * conditioned on it flickers while the worker streams output.
334	   */
335	  hasEverLoaded(workerId: string): boolean {
336	    if (this.everLoadedWorkers.has(workerId)) {
337	      return true;
338	    }
339	    const state = this.stateByWorker.get(workerId);
340	    if (state && state.status === "loaded" && !state.needsTailValidation) {
341	      this.everLoadedWorkers.add(workerId);
342	      return true;
343	    }
344	    return false;
345	  }
346	
347	  subscribe(workerId: string, listener: Listener): () => void {
348	    let bucket = this.listenersByWorker.get(workerId);
349	    if (!bucket) {
350	      bucket = new Set<Listener>();
351	      this.listenersByWorker.set(workerId, bucket);
352	    }
353	    bucket.add(listener);
354	    return () => {
355	      bucket?.delete(listener);
356	      if (bucket && bucket.size === 0) {
357	        this.listenersByWorker.delete(workerId);
358	      }
359	    };
360	  }
361	
362	  ensureLoaded(workerId: string): Promise<void> {
363	    const state = this.getState(workerId);
364	    if (state.status === "loading") {
365	      return this.inFlightByWorker.get(workerId) ?? Promise.resolve();
366	    }
367	    const loadedEmptyWithoutProof = state.status === "loaded"
368	      && state.latestContiguousSeq === 0
369	      && state.latestKnownSeq === 0
370	      && state.entries.length === 0;
371	    if (
372	      state.status === "loaded"
373	      && state.latestContiguousSeq === state.latestKnownSeq
374	      && !loadedEmptyWithoutProof
375	      && !state.needsTailValidation
376	    ) {
377	      return Promise.resolve();
378	    }
379	    // Cold start (no entries yet) → tail-load the last N entries instead
380	    // of pulling the whole transcript from seq 1.
381	    if (state.entries.length === 0 || state.needsTailValidation) {
382	      return this.fetchTail(workerId, DEFAULT_TAIL_LIMIT);
383	    }
384	    return this.fetchForward(workerId, state.latestContiguousSeq);
385	  }
386	
387	  /**
388	   * Fetch a page of older entries (strictly before lowestSeq). The
389	   * returned page is prepended to the window. Caller can poll
390	   * `state.hasOlder` to decide whether to keep loading on scroll-back.
391	   */
392	  loadOlder(workerId: string, limit: number = DEFAULT_OLDER_LIMIT): Promise<void> {
393	    const existing = this.inFlightByWorker.get(workerId);
394	    if (existing) {
395	      if (this.olderLoadInFlightByWorker.has(workerId)) {
396	        return existing;
397	      }
398	      // A live refresh may occupy the single-flight slot when the user
399	      // reaches the top of the viewport. Preserve that scroll-back intent and
400	      // start the older-page request after the refresh settles.
401	      return existing.then(
402	        () => this.loadOlder(workerId, limit),
403	        () => this.loadOlder(workerId, limit),
404	      );
405	    }
406	    const state = this.getState(workerId);
407	    if (!state.hasOlder || state.lowestSeq <= 1) {
408	      return Promise.resolve();
409	    }
410	    this.olderLoadInFlightByWorker.add(workerId);
411	    return this.fetchBefore(workerId, state.lowestSeq, limit).finally(() => {
412	      this.olderLoadInFlightByWorker.delete(workerId);
413	    });
414	  }
415	
416	  refresh(workerId: string): Promise<void> {
417	    const state = this.getState(workerId);
418	    return this.fetchForward(workerId, state.latestContiguousSeq);
419	  }
420	
421	  /**
422	   * Wake-up hint from the SSE stream. The body carries only
423	   * `{ workerId, seq }`; the entry payload is never trusted from here.
424	   * Triggers a refetch from the current contiguous cursor.
425	   */
426	  onWakeUp(args: { workerId: string; seq: number }): void {
427	    const state = this.getState(args.workerId);
428	    if (args.seq <= state.latestContiguousSeq) {
429	      // Already received; the wake-up was redundant (e.g. another tab fired).
430	      return;
431	    }
432	    this.updateState(args.workerId, {
433	      ...state,
434	      latestKnownSeq: Math.max(state.latestKnownSeq, args.seq),
435	    });
436	    this.markWake(args.workerId);
437	    void this.fetchForward(args.workerId, state.latestContiguousSeq);
438	  }
439	
440	  /**
441	   * Durable cursor hints from global event snapshots. These recover
442	   * from missed `worker.entry_appended` wake-up frames without carrying
443	   * worker transcript content on the global snapshot stream.
444	   */
445	  onKnownSeqs(workerEntrySeqs: Record<string, unknown> | null | undefined): void {
446	    if (!workerEntrySeqs) {
447	      return;
448	    }
449	
450	    for (const [workerId, rawSeq] of Object.entries(workerEntrySeqs)) {
451	      const seq = typeof rawSeq === "number" && Number.isFinite(rawSeq)
452	        ? Math.max(0, Math.floor(rawSeq))
453	        : 0;
454	      if (seq <= 0) {
455	        continue;
456	      }
457	
458	      const state = this.getState(workerId);
459	
460	      const latestKnownSeq = Math.max(state.latestKnownSeq, seq);
461	      if (latestKnownSeq <= state.latestContiguousSeq && state.status !== "error") {
462	        continue;
463	      }
464	
465	      this.updateState(workerId, {
466	        ...state,
467	        latestKnownSeq,
468	      });
469	      this.markWake(workerId);
470	      void this.fetchForward(workerId, state.latestContiguousSeq);
471	    }
472	  }
473	
474	  /**
475	   * Global SSE resync signal. Re-validates every worker we track by
476	   * refetching from each one's current contiguous cursor. A worker
477	   * whose `latestContiguousSeq === latestKnownSeq` will get an empty
478	   * fetch and stay loaded — a global resync is a no-op for it.
479	   */
480	  onStreamResync(): void {
481	    for (const workerId of this.stateByWorker.keys()) {
482	      const state = this.getState(workerId);
483	      void this.fetchForward(workerId, state.latestContiguousSeq);
484	    }
485	  }
486	
487	  reset(): void {
488	    this.stateByWorker.clear();
489	    this.listenersByWorker.clear();
490	    this.inFlightByWorker.clear();
491	    this.wakeVersionByWorker.clear();
492	    this.olderLoadInFlightByWorker.clear();
493	    this.everLoadedWorkers.clear();
494	    if (this.flushTimer !== null) {
495	      clearTimeout(this.flushTimer);
496	      this.flushTimer = null;
497	    }
498	    this.envelope = null;
499	  }
500	
501	  private markWake(workerId: string): void {
502	    this.wakeVersionByWorker.set(workerId, (this.wakeVersionByWorker.get(workerId) ?? 0) + 1);
503	  }
504	
505	  private fetchTail(workerId: string, limit: number): Promise<void> {
506	    const existing = this.inFlightByWorker.get(workerId);
507	    if (existing) return existing;
508	    const previous = this.getState(workerId);
509	    this.updateState(workerId, { ...previous, status: "loading", lastError: null });
510	    const promise = this.load({ workerId, limit }).then(
511	      (response) => {
512	        const current = this.getState(workerId);
513	        const sorted = [...response.entries].sort((a, b) => a.seq - b.seq);
514	        const latestContiguous = sorted.length > 0 ? sorted[sorted.length - 1]!.seq : 0;
515	        const lowest = sorted.length > 0 ? sorted[0]!.seq : 0;
516	        // Preserve any latestKnownSeq advance that landed via wake-up
517	        // while this tail load was in flight.
518	        const currentKnown = current.needsTailValidation ? 0 : current.latestKnownSeq;
519	        const latestKnown = Math.max(currentKnown, response.latestSeq ?? 0, latestContiguous);
520	        this.updateState(workerId, {
521	          ...current,
522	          entries: sorted,
523	          lowestSeq: lowest,
524	          latestContiguousSeq: latestContiguous,
525	          latestKnownSeq: latestKnown,
526	          hasOlder: response.hasOlder ?? (lowest > 1),
527	          status: "loaded",
528	          lastError: null,
529	          needsTailValidation: false,
530	        });
531	      },
532	      (error: unknown) => {
533	        const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);
534	        const failing = this.getState(workerId);
535	        this.updateState(workerId, { ...failing, status: "error", lastError: message });
536	      },
537	    ).finally(() => {
538	      this.inFlightByWorker.delete(workerId);
539	      // If the server reported newer entries than we got in the tail
540	      // window, chase forward from latestContiguousSeq.
541	      const after = this.getState(workerId);
542	      if (after.status === "loaded" && after.latestContiguousSeq < after.latestKnownSeq) {
543	        void this.fetchForward(workerId, after.latestContiguousSeq);
544	      }
545	    });
546	    this.inFlightByWorker.set(workerId, promise);
547	    return promise;
548	  }
549	
550	  private fetchBefore(workerId: string, beforeSeq: number, limit: number): Promise<void> {
551	    const existing = this.inFlightByWorker.get(workerId);
552	    if (existing) return existing;
553	    const previous = this.getState(workerId);
554	    this.updateState(workerId, { ...previous, status: "loading", lastError: null });
555	    const promise = this.load({ workerId, beforeSeq, limit }).then(
556	      (response) => {
557	        const current = this.getState(workerId);
558	        // Only accept entries strictly older than our current window and
559	        // adjacent to it (no gap to lowestSeq). If we'd introduce a gap,
560	        // drop the page — `loadOlder` can be retried.
561	        const sorted = [...response.entries]
562	          .filter((entry) => typeof entry.seq === "number" && entry.seq > 0 && entry.seq < current.lowestSeq)
563	          .sort((a, b) => a.seq - b.seq);
564	        if (sorted.length === 0) {
565	          this.updateState(workerId, {
566	            ...current,
567	            hasOlder: response.hasOlder ?? false,
568	            status: "loaded",
569	            lastError: null,
570	          });
571	          return;
572	        }
573	        const highestOlderSeq = sorted[sorted.length - 1]!.seq;
574	        if (current.lowestSeq > 0 && highestOlderSeq !== current.lowestSeq - 1) {
575	          // Gap — older page doesn't butt up to our window. Don't merge
576	          // (would violate the contiguity invariant). Still advance the
577	          // hasOlder hint if the server thinks there's nothing left.
578	          this.updateState(workerId, {
579	            ...current,
580	            hasOlder: response.hasOlder ?? current.hasOlder,
581	            status: "loaded",
582	            lastError: null,
583	          });
584	          return;
585	        }
586	        const lowest = sorted[0]!.seq;
587	        const merged = [...sorted, ...current.entries];
588	        this.updateState(workerId, {
589	          ...current,
590	          entries: merged,
591	          lowestSeq: lowest,
592	          hasOlder: response.hasOlder ?? (lowest > 1),
593	          status: "loaded",
594	          lastError: null,
595	        });
596	      },
597	      (error: unknown) => {
598	        const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);
599	        const failing = this.getState(workerId);
600	        this.updateState(workerId, { ...failing, status: "error", lastError: message });
601	      },
602	    ).finally(() => {
603	      this.inFlightByWorker.delete(workerId);
604	    });
605	    this.inFlightByWorker.set(workerId, promise);
606	    return promise;
607	  }
608	
609	  private fetchForward(workerId: string, afterSeq: number): Promise<void> {
610	    const existing = this.inFlightByWorker.get(workerId);
611	    if (existing) {
612	      return existing;
613	    }
614	    const previous = this.getState(workerId);
615	    this.updateState(workerId, { ...previous, status: "loading", lastError: null });
616	    const cursorBeforeFetch = previous.latestContiguousSeq;
617	    const knownBeforeFetch = previous.latestKnownSeq;
618	    const wakeVersionBeforeFetch = this.wakeVersionByWorker.get(workerId) ?? 0;
619	    const promise = this.load({ workerId, afterSeq }).then(
620	      (response) => {
621	        this.applyFetchResult(workerId, afterSeq, response);
622	      },
623	      (error: unknown) => {
624	        const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);
625	        const failing = this.getState(workerId);
626	        this.updateState(workerId, { ...failing, status: "error", lastError: message });
627	      },
628	    ).finally(() => {
629	      this.inFlightByWorker.delete(workerId);
630	      // If applyFetchResult advanced the contiguous prefix and a known
631	      // gap remains, chase it now. Requiring progress prevents an
632	      // infinite loop when the server reports `latestSeq` ahead of the
633	      // entries it returned (race or stale latest hint).
634	      const after = this.getState(workerId);
635	      const wakeUpAdvancedKnownSeq = after.latestKnownSeq > knownBeforeFetch;
636	      const wakeUpArrivedDuringFetch = (this.wakeVersionByWorker.get(workerId) ?? 0) > wakeVersionBeforeFetch;
637	      if (
638	        after.status === "loaded"
639	        && (after.latestContiguousSeq > cursorBeforeFetch || wakeUpAdvancedKnownSeq || wakeUpArrivedDuringFetch)
640	        && after.latestContiguousSeq < after.latestKnownSeq
641	      ) {
642	        void this.fetchForward(workerId, after.latestContiguousSeq);
643	      }
644	    });
645	    this.inFlightByWorker.set(workerId, promise);
646	    return promise;
647	  }
648	
649	  private applyFetchResult(
650	    workerId: string,
651	    cursorBeforeFetch: number,
652	    response: FetchResponse,
653	  ): void {
654	    const previous = this.getState(workerId);
655	    // The endpoint returns entries strictly newer than `afterSeq`. If
656	    // the caller's view advanced between request and response, drop
657	    // anything we've already merged.
658	    const incoming = response.entries
659	      .filter((entry) => entry.seq > previous.latestContiguousSeq)
660	      .sort((a, b) => a.seq - b.seq);
661	
662	    let nextContiguous = previous.latestContiguousSeq;
663	    const merged = [...previous.entries];
664	    for (const entry of incoming) {
665	      if (entry.seq === nextContiguous + 1) {
666	        merged.push(entry);
667	        nextContiguous = entry.seq;
668	      } else if (entry.seq > nextContiguous + 1) {
669	        // Gap. Don't include the entry; the next forward load (kicked off
670	        // below) will cover both this seq and the missing ones.
671	        break;
672	      }
673	    }
674	
675	    const nextKnown = Math.max(previous.latestKnownSeq, response.latestSeq, nextContiguous);
676	    // If we started with no entries, lowestSeq becomes the first merged
677	    // entry's seq; otherwise the window's lower bound is unchanged.
678	    const nextLowest = previous.lowestSeq > 0
679	      ? previous.lowestSeq
680	      : merged[0]?.seq ?? 0;
681	    const next: WorkerStreamState = {
682	      ...previous,
683	      // Reuse the previous array when the poll appended nothing. The spread
684	      // above always allocates, so an idle validation poll used to hand every
685	      // subscriber a brand-new `entries` identity and invalidate each memo
686	      // derived from it — the transcript merge and Terminal's activity
687	      // rebuild both re-ran for a byte-identical result.
688	      entries: merged.length > previous.entries.length ? merged : previous.entries,
689	      lowestSeq: nextLowest,
690	      latestContiguousSeq: nextContiguous,
691	      latestKnownSeq: nextKnown,
692	      // hasOlder is preserved; only tail-load or loadOlder updates it.
693	      // A forward fetch starting from lowestSeq=0 (empty window) can't
694	      // assert anything about whether older entries exist.
695	      status: "loaded",
696	      lastError: null,
697	      needsTailValidation: false,
698	    };
699	    this.updateState(workerId, next);
700	
701	    // Gap chasing happens in the fetch wrapper's .finally so that
702	    // inFlightByWorker is cleared before the recursive call.
703	  }
704	
705	  private updateState(workerId: string, next: WorkerStreamState): void {
706	    this.stateByWorker.set(workerId, next);
707	    if (next.status === "loaded" && !next.needsTailValidation) {
708	      this.everLoadedWorkers.add(workerId);
709	    }
710	    if (next.status === "loaded") {
711	      this.rememberState(workerId, next.entries);
712	    }
713	    const listeners = this.listenersByWorker.get(workerId);
714	    if (!listeners) {
715	      return;
716	    }
717	    for (const listener of listeners) {
718	      listener(next);
719	    }
720	  }
721	
722	  private getCachedEntries(workerId: string): WorkerEntry[] {
723	    const cached = this.readEnvelope().workers[workerId];
724	    if (!cached || !isWorkerEntryArray(cached.entries)) {
725	      return [];
726	    }
727	    // Trim to the tail window. The cache used to rehydrate full
728	    // transcripts which caused the worker view to render every entry
729	    // from seq 1 and then auto-scroll down — bad UX on long sessions.
730	    // We want the user to see the most recent N entries immediately,
731	    // then load older ones on scroll-back via loadOlder().
732	    if (cached.entries.length > DEFAULT_TAIL_LIMIT) {
733	      return cached.entries.slice(-DEFAULT_TAIL_LIMIT);
734	    }
735	    return cached.entries;
736	  }
737	
738	  private rememberState(workerId: string, entries: WorkerEntry[]): void {
739	    if (!this.storage) {
740	      return;
741	    }
742	
743	    const currentEnvelope = this.readEnvelope();
744	    if (entries.length === 0) {
745	      const { [workerId]: _removed, ...workers } = currentEnvelope.workers;
746	      this.envelope = { ...currentEnvelope, workers };
747	      this.scheduleFlush();
748	      return;
749	    }
750	
751	    this.envelope = this.pruneEnvelope({
752	      ...currentEnvelope,
753	      workers: {
754	        ...currentEnvelope.workers,
755	        [workerId]: {
756	          updatedAt: this.now(),
757	          entries,
758	        },
759	      },
760	    });
761	    this.scheduleFlush();
762	  }
763	
764	  /** Write the pending cache envelope to storage now. */
765	  flushCache(): void {
766	    if (this.flushTimer !== null) {
767	      clearTimeout(this.flushTimer);
768	      this.flushTimer = null;
769	    }
770	
771	    const envelope = this.envelope;
772	    if (!this.storage || !envelope) {
773	      return;
774	    }
775	
776	    const serialized = JSON.stringify({
777	      version: 1,
778	      workers: Object.fromEntries(
779	        Object.entries(envelope.workers).map(([id, cached]) => [id, {
780	          updatedAt: cached.updatedAt,
781	          entries: cached.entries.map(compactEntryForCache),
782	        }]),
783	      ),
784	    } satisfies WorkerEntriesCacheEnvelope);
785	    if (serialized.length > this.maxSerializedBytes) {
786	      return;
787	    }
788	
789	    try {
790	      this.storage.setItem(this.storageKey, serialized);
791	    } catch {
792	      // localStorage is a preview cache only; live server state remains authoritative.
793	    }
794	  }
795	
796	  private scheduleFlush(): void {
797	    if (this.flushTimer !== null) {
798	      return;
799	    }
800	
801	    if (this.flushIntervalMs <= 0) {
802	      this.flushCache();
803	      return;
804	    }
805	
806	    this.flushTimer = setTimeout(() => {
807	      this.flushTimer = null;
808	      this.flushCache();
809	    }, this.flushIntervalMs);
810	    (this.flushTimer as { unref?: () => void }).unref?.();
811	  }
812	
813	  private readEnvelope(): WorkerEntriesCacheEnvelope {
814	    if (!this.storage) {
815	      return emptyEnvelope();
816	    }
817	
818	    if (this.envelope) {
819	      return this.envelope;
820	    }
821	
822	    try {
823	      const raw = this.storage.getItem(this.storageKey);
824	      if (!raw) {
825	        this.envelope = emptyEnvelope();
826	        return this.envelope;
827	      }
828	
829	      const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as Partial<WorkerEntriesCacheEnvelope>;
830	      if (parsed.version !== 1 || typeof parsed.workers !== "object" || parsed.workers === null) {
831	        this.envelope = emptyEnvelope();
832	        return this.envelope;
833	      }
834	      this.envelope = {
835	        version: 1,
836	        workers: parsed.workers as Record<string, CachedWorkerStream>,
837	      };
838	      return this.envelope;
839	    } catch {
840	      try {
841	        this.storage.removeItem(this.storageKey);
842	      } catch {
843	        // Ignore cleanup failures.
844	      }
845	      this.envelope = emptyEnvelope();
846	      return this.envelope;
847	    }
848	  }
849	
850	  private pruneEnvelope(envelope: WorkerEntriesCacheEnvelope): WorkerEntriesCacheEnvelope {
851	    return {
852	      version: 1,
853	      workers: Object.fromEntries(
854	        Object.entries(envelope.workers)
855	          .sort((left, right) => {
856	            const updatedDelta = right[1].updatedAt - left[1].updatedAt;
857	            return updatedDelta !== 0 ? updatedDelta : left[0].localeCompare(right[0]);
858	          })
859	          .slice(0, this.maxWorkers),
860	      ),
861	    };
862	  }
863	}
864	
865	// A single process-wide instance is fine because manager state is keyed
866	// by workerId and the same worker is never owned by two tabs. The
867	// reset() method exists for tests; production code should not call it.
868	export const workerEntriesManager = new WorkerEntriesManager();
869	
870	/**
871	 * Subscribe to the unified worker conversation stream for a single
872	 * worker. Returns the live state plus a `isLoaded` boolean derived
873	 * from the manager's contiguous-prefix invariant.
874	 *
875	 * `workerId === null` returns a stable empty state and skips the
876	 * fetch.
877	 */
878	interface UseWorkerStreamOptions {
879	  refreshIntervalMs?: number | null;
880	}
881	
882	export function useWorkerStream(
883	  workerId: string | null,
884	  initialEntries: WorkerEntry[] = [],
885	  options: UseWorkerStreamOptions = {},
886	) {
887	  const runtimeApis = useRuntimeAPIs();
888	  workerEntriesManager.configure(runtimeApis.workers.listEntries);
889	  const state = useSyncExternalStore(
890	    useCallback((listener) => (
891	      workerId ? workerEntriesManager.subscribe(workerId, listener) : () => {}
892	    ), [workerId]),
```

```text
417	      const operationKey = key(runId, workerId);
418	      try {
419	        const result = await this.deps.readPlan(runId, workerId);
420	        const boundary = latestBoundary(result.entries);
421	        const binding = {
422	          sessionId: boundary?.acpSessionId ?? null,
423	          boundarySeq: boundary?.seq ?? 0,
424	          hydrated: true,
425	        } satisfies PlanBinding;
426	        this.bindings.set(operationKey, binding);
427	        return binding;
428	      } catch (error) {
429	        // Fail closed. A previously hydrated session must not remain usable
430	        // after a later lifecycle generation failed to re-establish durable
431	        // truth; subsequent callbacks are rejected as hydrating until a
432	        // successful hydration replaces this sentinel.
433	        this.bindings.set(operationKey, {
434	          sessionId: null,
435	          boundarySeq: 0,
436	          hydrated: false,
437	        });
438	        this.publish({
439	          kind: "error.surfaced",
440	          code: "worker.plan.binding_hydration_failed",
441	          message: `Failed to hydrate ACP plan session binding: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}`,
442	          surface: "log",
443	          runId,
444	          workerId,
445	          cause: error instanceof Error ? { name: error.name, message: error.message } : null,
446	        });
447	        throw error;
448	      }
449	    });
450	  }
451	
452	  async beginWorkerPlanSession(runId: string, workerId: string, sessionId: string): Promise<PlanBinding> {
453	    return this.serial(runId, workerId, async () => {
454	      const capturedSessionId = capturePlanSessionId(sessionId);
455	      if (capturedSessionId.kind !== "valid") {
456	        const message = capturedSessionId.kind === "oversized"
457	          ? `ACP plan session id exceeds ${MAX_ACP_PLAN_SESSION_ID_BYTES} bytes.`
458	          : "ACP plan session id is missing.";
459	        const error = new Error(message);
460	        this.publish({
461	          kind: "error.surfaced",
462	          code: "worker.plan.boundary_append_failed",
463	          message,
464	          surface: "log",
465	          runId,
466	          workerId,
467	          cause: { name: error.name, message: error.message },
468	        });
469	        throw error;
470	      }
471	      const normalizedSessionId = capturedSessionId.value;
472	      const operationKey = key(runId, workerId);
473	      const current = this.bindings.get(operationKey);
474	      if (current?.hydrated && current.sessionId === normalizedSessionId && current.boundarySeq > 0) {
475	        return current;
476	      }
477	
478	      const entry: Omit<WorkerEntry, "seq"> = {
479	        id: `acp-plan-boundary-${this.deps.createId()}`,
480	        type: "system_note",
481	        text: "",
482	        timestamp: nowIso(),
483	        raw: { kind: "acp.plan_session_boundary" },
484	        acpSessionId: normalizedSessionId,
485	        planProjection: "session_reset",
486	        diagnosticOnly: true,
487	      };
488	      const maximumSeq = Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER;
489	      const durableCandidate = { ...entry, seq: maximumSeq } satisfies WorkerEntry;
490	      const responseCandidate: WorkerPlanReadResponse = {
491	        latestSeq: maximumSeq,
492	        plan: {
493	          runId,
494	          workerId,
495	          acpSessionId: normalizedSessionId,
496	          planBoundarySeq: maximumSeq,
497	          lastEntrySeq: maximumSeq,
498	          lastAcceptedEntryId: null,
499	          visible: false,
```

```text
1	/**
2	 * Named lifecycle events emitted alongside the snapshot stream.
3	 *
4	 * See docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md for the
5	 * design rationale. The short version:
6	 *
7	 *   - Every server-side decision that the user, the UI, or a test client
8	 *     might want to observe is published here as a typed event.
9	 *   - Events share a single monotonic id namespace with the `update`
10	 *     snapshot frames emitted by /api/events. That keeps SSE
11	 *     `Last-Event-ID` resume unambiguous.
12	 *   - A bounded ring buffer remembers recent emissions so reconnecting
13	 *     clients can replay events they missed during disconnect.
14	 *
15	 * This module is intentionally synchronous and in-process. The ring
16	 * resets when the server restarts; clients then bootstrap via
17	 * `/api/events?snapshot=1` and resume from the new cursor.
18	 */
```

```text
1	import type * as acp from "@agentclientprotocol/sdk";
2	import type { OutputEntry } from "../types";
3	
4	export type NormalizedSessionUpdate =
5	  | { kind: "message_chunk"; role: "user" | "agent" | "thought"; text: string }
6	  | { kind: "entry"; entry: Omit<OutputEntry, "id" | "timestamp"> };
7	
8	function contentType(content: acp.ContentBlock) {
9	  return content.type;
10	}
11	
12	function contentText(content: acp.ContentBlock) {
13	  return content.type === "text" ? content.text : null;
14	}
15	
16	function planText(update: Extract<acp.SessionUpdate, { sessionUpdate: "plan" }>) {
17	  return update.entries.map((entry) => entry.content).join("\n");
18	}
19	
20	function planUpdateText(update: Extract<acp.SessionUpdate, { sessionUpdate: "plan_update" }>) {
21	  const plan = update.plan;
22	  if (plan.type === "items") return plan.entries.map((entry) => entry.content).join("\n");
23	  if (plan.type === "markdown") return plan.content;
24	  return plan.uri;
25	}
26	
27	export function normalizeSessionUpdate(update: acp.SessionUpdate): NormalizedSessionUpdate {
28	  switch (update.sessionUpdate) {
29	    case "user_message_chunk": {
30	      const text = contentText(update.content);
31	      return text !== null
32	        ? { kind: "message_chunk", role: "user", text }
33	        : { kind: "entry", entry: { type: "user_content", text: contentType(update.content), raw: update } };
34	    }
35	    case "agent_message_chunk": {
36	      const text = contentText(update.content);
37	      return text !== null
38	        ? { kind: "message_chunk", role: "agent", text }
39	        : { kind: "entry", entry: { type: "agent_content", text: contentType(update.content), raw: update } };
40	    }
41	    case "agent_thought_chunk": {
42	      const text = contentText(update.content);
43	      return text !== null
44	        ? { kind: "message_chunk", role: "thought", text }
45	        : { kind: "entry", entry: { type: "agent_content", text: contentType(update.content), status: "thought", raw: update } };
46	    }
47	    case "tool_call":
48	      return { kind: "entry", entry: {
49	        type: "tool_call",
50	        text: update.title || update.kind || update.toolCallId,
51	        toolCallId: update.toolCallId,
52	        toolKind: update.kind ?? undefined,
53	        status: update.status ?? undefined,
54	        raw: update,
55	      } };
56	    case "tool_call_update":
57	      return { kind: "entry", entry: {
58	        type: "tool_call_update",
59	        text: update.title || update.status || update.toolCallId,
60	        toolCallId: update.toolCallId,
61	        toolKind: update.kind ?? undefined,
62	        status: update.status ?? undefined,
63	        raw: update,
64	      } };
65	    case "plan":
66	      return { kind: "entry", entry: { type: "plan", text: planText(update), raw: update } };
67	    case "plan_update":
68	      return { kind: "entry", entry: { type: "plan_update", text: planUpdateText(update), raw: update } };
69	    case "plan_removed":
70	      return { kind: "entry", entry: { type: "plan_removed", text: update.id, raw: update } };
71	    case "available_commands_update":
72	      return { kind: "entry", entry: {
73	        type: "available_commands",
74	        text: update.availableCommands.map((command) => command.name).join("\n"),
75	        raw: update,
76	      } };
77	    case "current_mode_update":
78	      return { kind: "entry", entry: { type: "current_mode", text: update.currentModeId, raw: update } };
79	    case "config_option_update":
80	      return { kind: "entry", entry: { type: "config_option", text: update.configOptions.map((option) => option.name).join("\n"), raw: update } };
81	    case "session_info_update":
82	      return { kind: "entry", entry: { type: "session_info", text: update.title ?? update.updatedAt ?? "", raw: update } };
83	    case "usage_update":
84	      return { kind: "entry", entry: { type: "usage", text: `${update.used}/${update.size}`, raw: update } };
85	  }
86	}
87
```

```text
49	// Minimum wall-clock gap between full snapshot rebuilds on one stream. A

66	async function fetchWithTimeout(url: string, timeoutMs: number) {
67	  const controller = new AbortController();

74	        timeout = setTimeout(() => {
75	          controller.abort();
76	          reject(new Error(`Agent runtime list request timed out after ${timeoutMs}ms.`));
77	        }, timeoutMs);
78	      }),
79	    ]);
80	  } finally {
81	    if (timeout) {
82	      clearTimeout(timeout);
83	    }
84	  }
85	}
```

```text
1	/**
2	 * Thin helpers around `appendWorkerEntry` for the unified worker
3	 * conversation stream. Each helper:
4	 *
5	 *   1. Builds a `WorkerEntry` of the appropriate type with a generated
6	 *      uuid (server-produced entries don't have a stable upstream id).
7	 *   2. Appends via `appendWorkerEntry` (which assigns the seq and holds
8	 *      the per-worker chain).
9	 *   3. After fsync, emits a `worker.entry_appended` named event carrying
10	 *      only `{ workerId, seq }`. The full entry is fetched via the
11	 *      entries endpoint — the SSE frame is a wake-up hint, not a content
12	 *      carrier.
13	 *
14	 * Append-on-delivery semantics: ordinary follow-up callers MUST invoke
15	 * these only after `askAgent` (or the equivalent delivery primitive)
16	 * has resolved successfully. Initial direct/planning prompts are the
17	 * exception: the conversation has already accepted the message, and the
18	 * prompt must anchor the worker stream before bridge progress snapshots
19	 * can append output while the first ask is still in flight.
20	 */

25	  WorkerEntry,

27	  WorkerEntryAuthorRole,
28	  WorkerEntryChannel,
29	} from "@/server/workers/entries-types";

31	export type AppendUserInputArgs = {
32	  runId: string;
33	  workerId: string;
34	  text: string;
35	  deliveredAt: Date;
36	  authorRole?: WorkerEntryAuthorRole;
37	  attachments?: WorkerEntryAttachment[];
38	  /** Optional stable id (e.g. from a messages row) so dedup across recovery works. */

40	};
41

43	  runId: string;
44	  workerId: string;
45	  text: string;

89	function persistAndAnnounce(args: {
90	  runId: string;
91	  workerId: string;
92	  entry: Omit<WorkerEntry, "seq">;
93	}): Promise<WorkerEntry | null> {
94	  return appendWorkerEntryWithResult(args.runId, args.workerId, args.entry).then((result) => {
95	    const persisted = result.entry;
96	    if (!result.appended || typeof persisted.seq !== "number" || persisted.seq <= 0) {
97	      return persisted;
98	    }
99	    emitNamedEvent({
100	      kind: "worker.entry_appended",
101	      runId: args.runId,
102	      workerId: args.workerId,
103	      seq: persisted.seq,
104	    });
105	    return persisted;
106	  });
107	}
108

110	  return persistAndAnnounce({

114	      id: args.id ?? randomUUID(),
115	      type: "user_input",
```

```text
316	export function appendOutputEntry(record: AgentRecord, input: OutputEntryInput) {
317	  const archiveEntry = record.outputArchive.append(input);
318	  record.activeOutputEntryId = null;
319	  record.outputEntries.push(toLiveEntry(archiveEntry));
320	  pruneLiveEntries(record);
321	}
322	
323	/**
324	 * Reassemble raw archive records into the entries the worker stream stores.
325	 *
326	 * The archive keeps every streaming chunk as its own record; the worker stream
327	 * keeps the assembled message. This is the read-side inverse of
328	 * `appendMessageChunk` below — same rule: consecutive same-type
329	 * `message`/`thought` chunks join, any other record type ends the run.
330	 *
331	 * Used when recovering a transcript whose stream file lost its head; replaying
332	 * the archive without it produces a transcript shredded mid-word.
333	 */
334	export function reassembleArchivedEntries<T extends { type?: string; text?: string }>(entries: T[]): T[] {
335	  const out: T[] = [];
336	  let active: T | null = null;
337	  for (const entry of entries) {
338	    if (entry.type === "message" || entry.type === "thought") {
339	      if (active && active.type === entry.type) {
340	        active.text = `${active.text ?? ""}${entry.text ?? ""}`;
341	        continue;
342	      }
343	      const started = { ...entry };
344	      out.push(started);
345	      active = started;
346	      continue;
347	    }
348	    out.push({ ...entry });
349	    active = null;
350	  }
351	  return out;
352	}
353	
354	export function appendMessageChunk(record: AgentRecord, text: string, type: "message" | "thought") {
355	  const archiveEntry = record.outputArchive.append({ type, text });
356	  const activeEntry = record.activeOutputEntryId
357	    ? record.outputEntries.find((entry) => entry.id === record.activeOutputEntryId)
358	    : null;
359	
360	  if (activeEntry && activeEntry.type === type) {
361	    if (type === "thought") {
362	      activeEntry.text = appendBoundedThoughts(activeEntry.text, text, LIVE_OUTPUT_ENTRY_TEXT_CHARS);
363	    } else {
364	      activeEntry.text += text;
365	    }
366	    return;
367	  }
368	
369	  const liveEntry = toLiveEntry(archiveEntry);
370	  record.outputEntries.push(liveEntry);
371	  record.activeOutputEntryId = liveEntry.id;
372	  pruneLiveEntries(record);
373	}
```

```text
79	function compactRawValue(
80	  value: unknown,
81	  maxStringChars: number,
82	  depth = 0,
83	  seen = new WeakSet<object>(),
84	): unknown {
85	  if (typeof value === "string") {
86	    return truncateString(value, maxStringChars);
87	  }
88	  if (value == null || typeof value !== "object") {
89	    return value;
90	  }
91	  if (seen.has(value)) {
92	    return "[circular]";
93	  }
94	  if (depth >= RAW_DEPTH) {
95	    return "[truncated depth]";
96	  }
97	  seen.add(value);
98	
99	  if (Array.isArray(value)) {
100	    const compacted = value.slice(0, RAW_ARRAY_ITEMS).map((item) => compactRawValue(item, maxStringChars, depth + 1, seen));
101	    if (value.length > RAW_ARRAY_ITEMS) {
102	      compacted.push(`[truncated ${value.length - RAW_ARRAY_ITEMS} items]`);
103	    }
104	    return compacted;
105	  }
106	
107	  const compacted: Record<string, unknown> = {};
108	  const entries = Object.entries(value);
109	  for (const [key, nestedValue] of entries.slice(0, RAW_OBJECT_KEYS)) {
110	    compacted[key] = compactRawValue(nestedValue, maxStringChars, depth + 1, seen);
111	  }
112	  if (entries.length > RAW_OBJECT_KEYS) {
113	    compacted.__truncatedKeys = entries.length - RAW_OBJECT_KEYS;
114	  }
115	  return compacted;
```

```text
383	function compactHistoryRawValue(value: unknown): unknown {
384	  if (typeof value === "string") {
385	    return truncateHistoryString(value);
386	  }
387	  if (Array.isArray(value)) {
388	    return value.map(compactHistoryRawValue);
389	  }
390	  if (!isPlainRecord(value)) {
391	    return value;
392	  }
393	  const compressed = compressDiffContentPair(value);
394	  if (compressed) {
395	    // oldText/newText have already been compacted with diff-aware logic
396	    // that preserves their divergence. Skipping the generic per-string
397	    // truncator on those two fields is required — line-based truncation
398	    // would re-collapse them to identical head/tail strings and erase the
399	    // diff signal.
400	    return Object.fromEntries(
401	      Object.entries(compressed)
402	        .filter(([key]) => key !== "__diffCompressed")
403	        .map(([key, nested]) => (
404	          key === "oldText" || key === "newText"
405	            ? [key, nested]
406	            : [key, compactHistoryRawValue(nested)]
407	        )),
408	    );
409	  }
410	  return Object.fromEntries(
411	    Object.entries(value).map(([key, nested]) => [key, compactHistoryRawValue(nested)]),
412	  );
413	}
414	
415	type CompactableEntry = {
416	  type?: string;
417	  text?: string;
418	  raw?: unknown;
419	  [key: string]: unknown;
420	};
421	
422	export function compactEntryForHistory<T extends CompactableEntry>(entry: T): T {
423	  // Accepted ACP core plans are already ingress-bounded and their original raw
424	  // notification is part of the durable replay contract. Generic history
425	  // compaction must not silently rewrite that accepted evidence.
426	  if (entry.planProjection === "accepted_core") {
427	    return entry;
428	  }
429	  const text = (entry.type === "tool_call" || entry.type === "tool_call_update") && typeof entry.text === "string"
430	    ? truncateHistoryString(entry.text)
431	    : entry.text;
432	  if (entry.raw === undefined && text === entry.text) {
433	    return entry;
434	  }
435	  return {
436	    ...entry,
437	    text,
438	    raw: entry.raw === undefined ? undefined : compactHistoryRawValue(entry.raw),
439	  };
440	}
441	
442	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
```

```text
121	    resolve = res;
```

```text
Excellent. Now I have the full picture. Let me now verify one more time what `truncateHistoryString` does to a base64 string in an image payload:

So when `compactHistoryRawValue` encounters any string (like a base64-encoded image in `raw.content.data`), it calls `truncateHistoryString` at line 385, which will:
1. Split by lines (RAW_HISTORY_LINE_LIMIT = 8)
2. If it exceeds 8 lines, truncate to head + tail with omission marker
3. Then call `truncateHistoryStringByChars` which applies RAW_HISTORY_CHAR_LIMIT (4,000 chars)
```

```text
2144	  runId: string,
2145	  workerId: string,
2146	): Promise<number> {
2147	  const key = chainKey(runId, workerId);

2150	  // nextSeqByKey. If it's set, the latest persisted seq is nextSeq - 1
2151	  // and no FS access is required. All writers to these files live in
2152	  // the same Node process as this reader (see writeWorkerOutputEntries
2153	  // call sites — sync.ts, snapshots.ts, recovery-reconciler.ts), so
2154	  // this cache cannot fall behind disk.

2157	    return nextSeq - 1;
2158	  }
```

````text
Perfect. Now let me finalize my findings by creating the comprehensive report:

## Comprehensive Trace of Worker Output WRITE Path for Image Entries

I've traced the complete path for an ACP `agent_content` entry containing an image (where `raw.content.type === "image"` and `raw.content.data` contains base64) from agent runtime through to the canonical unified worker stream JSONL. Here are the answers:

### 1. `toLiveEntry` and `createArchiveEntry` Calls

**`createArchiveEntry` is called:**
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/agent-runtime/output-store.ts:213` — in `AgentOutputArchive.append()` method
  - This creates an archive entry with truncated `raw` via `compactRawValue()` with `maxStringChars = ARCHIVE_RAW_STRING_CHARS (1,000,000)` for regular entries or `ARCHIVE_TOOL_RAW_STRING_CHARS (8,000)` for tool entries (line 128)
  - The result is written to disk and returned

**`toLiveEntry` is called:**
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/agent-runtime/output-store.ts:319` — in `appendOutputEntry()` after archive append
  - Applied to the archive entry to create a "live" version with tighter limits: `LIVE_RAW_STRING_CHARS = 4,000` (line 141)
  - This live version is stored in `record.outputEntries` for the in-memory snapshot
- `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/agent-runtime/output-store.ts:369` — in `appendMessageChunk()` for streaming message chunks

**The call chain for `agent_content` entries:**
- `src/server/agent-runtime/acp/runtime-client.ts:558` calls `appendOutputEntry(record, normalized.entry)` when an image/non-text content arrives
- `src/server/agent-runtime/output-store.ts:316` — `appendOutputEntry()` function:
  1. Line 317: Calls `record.outputArchive.append(input)` → returns archive entry with compacted raw (max 1M chars for regular entries)
  2. Line 319: Calls `toLiveEntry(archiveEntry)` → compacts raw further to 4,000 chars max
  3. Pushes the live entry to `record.outputEntries`

### 2. Bridge from Agent Runtime to Worker Stream

The bridge is **`src/server/agent-runtime/acp/plan-stream.ts`** which uses the worker stream append function.

**Call chain:**
- `src/server/agent-runtime/acp/plan-stream.ts:69` — sets up `append: appendWorkerEntryWithResult` as a dependency
- Other callers that sync agent entries to the worker stream:
  - `src/server/conversations/sync.ts:651, 703, 782` — calls `writeWorkerOutputEntries(run.id, worker.id, agent.outputEntries)`
  - `src/server/workers/snapshots.ts:139` — calls `writeWorkerOutputEntries()` from snapshot persistence
  - `src/server/workers/human-input-entries.ts:130, 193` — calls `writeWorkerOutputEntries()`
  - `src/server/runs/recovery-reconciler.ts:418` — calls `writeWorkerOutputEntries()`

**The exact bridge function is:**
- `src/server/workers/output-store.ts:1129` — `writeWorkerOutputEntries()` which:
  1. Takes `agent.outputEntries` (the live truncated entries from agent-runtime)
  2. For each entry, calls `bridgeOutputEntryFingerprint()` to deduplicate exact replays
  3. Converts to `AgentOutputEntry` type
  4. Calls `appendWorkerEntryWithResult()` internally for each new/changed entry

### 3. Truncation/Compaction in Worker Stream Write

**YES, `appendWorkerEntryWithResult` applies its OWN compaction:**

In `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/workers/output-store.ts:1060`:
```
const compact = compactEntryForHistory(persistedEntry as unknown as CompactableEntry) as unknown as WorkerEntry;
const line = JSON.stringify(compact) + "\n";
```

This calls `compactEntryForHistory()` (line 422) which:
- **For entries with `type !== "tool_call" | "tool_call_update"`:** Does NOT touch `text` (line 438 only compacts if type is tool_call/tool_call_update)
- **For ALL entries:** Applies `compactHistoryRawValue()` to `entry.raw` (line 438)

**What `compactHistoryRawValue()` does (line 383):**
- Recursively walks the `raw` object
- For ANY string value (including base64 image data at `raw.content.data`):
  - Calls `truncateHistoryString()` (line 385) which:
    1. Splits by `\n` and keeps only first 4 lines + last 4 lines if > 8 lines total
    2. Then calls `truncateHistoryStringByChars()` which applies `RAW_HISTORY_CHAR_LIMIT = 4,000` chars
    3. Result: A base64 image string > 4,000 chars is truncated to ~2,000 head + "[omitted X chars]" + ~2,000 tail

**So for an `agent_content` image entry:**
- `raw.content.data` (base64 string) → gets truncated to max 4,000 chars on **write to canonical JSONL**
- This is separate from and AFTER the live truncation (4,000 chars) applied in agent-runtime
- The canonical stream gets the tighter bound of 4,000 chars

**Field applicability:**
- `RAW_HISTORY_CHAR_LIMIT (4,000)` applies to **any string anywhere in `entry.raw`** for all entry types except accepted core plans
- `DIFF_SIDE_CHAR_LIMIT (4,000)` applies only to `oldText`/`newText` fields in `entry.raw` for diff-type tool entries (compressDiffContentPair)
- These do NOT touch `agent_content` image payloads specially — they truncate like any other string

### 4. Periodic Compaction Sweep

**YES, there is a periodic compaction sweep in `src/server/workers/output-store.ts:2479`:**

`compactStaleWorkerOutputs()` function:
- **Triggers:** Called on stale worker files (not touched for `minAgeMs = 5 * 60 * 1000` = 5 minutes by default, see line 47)
- **Targets:** Only `.jsonl` files for workers in TERMINAL_WORKER_STATUSES (line 37-45: "completed", "failed", "cancelled", "error", "stopped", "done")
- **What it does (line 2375, `compactWorkerOutputFileInternal`):**
  1. Reads the plaintext `.jsonl` file entirely (line 2379)
  2. Parses all lines with `parseWorkerEntryLines()` (line 2388)
  3. Normalizes entries with `normalizePersistedEntries()` to assign seq numbers to legacy entries (line 2389)
  4. **DOES NOT apply additional compaction** — just re-serializes and gzips as-is (lines 2390-2395)
  5. Writes to `.gz` compressed file and deletes the plaintext `.jsonl`
  6. Does NOT rewrite/re-compact entries — preserves all `raw` fields exactly as they were persisted

**Critical detail:** The compaction sweep is **NOT** a rewrite pass that re-applies `compactEntryForHistory()`. It only:
- Normalizes legacy seq assignments
- Compresses the file (gzip)
- Does NOT drop or truncate large entries further

So large image payloads persist at their 4,000-char truncated size through the sweep.

### 5. Constants at Top of `src/server/agent-runtime/output-store.ts` (lines 1-20)

| Constant | Value | Purpose |
|----------|-------|---------|
| `LIVE_TEXT_FIELD_CHARS` | 100,000 | Max chars for accumulating `.text` fields in live agent state (e.g., currentText, lastText) |
| `LIVE_OUTPUT_ENTRY_LIMIT` | 80 | Max number of output entries to keep in memory (`record.outputEntries` array) |
| `LIVE_OUTPUT_ENTRY_TEXT_CHARS` | 5,000 | Max chars for `.text` field per live output entry (except message type which is uncapped) |
| `LIVE_RAW_STRING_CHARS` | 4,000 | Max chars for any string in `.raw` field of live entries (applied by `toLiveEntry()`) |
| `ARCHIVE_ENTRY_TEXT_CHARS` | 1,000,000 | Max chars for `.text` field in archived entries (regular types) |
| `ARCHIVE_RAW_STRING_CHARS` | 1,000,000 | Max chars for any string in `.raw` field of archived entries (regular types) |
| `ARCHIVE_TOOL_ENTRY_TEXT_CHARS` | 2,000 | Max chars for `.text` field in archived tool_call/tool_call_update/permission/elicitation entries |
| `ARCHIVE_TOOL_RAW_STRING_CHARS` | 8,000 | Max chars for any string in `.raw` field of archived tool entries |
| `TOOL_CALL_UPDATE_SUMMARY_CHARS` | 2,000 | Max chars for summarizing tool call updates (currently unused in the codebase) |
| `RAW_ARRAY_ITEMS` | 200 | Max array items to keep when compacting raw values (arrays truncated beyond this) |
| `RAW_OBJECT_KEYS` | 100 | Max object keys to keep when compacting raw values (objects truncated beyond this) |
| `RAW_DEPTH` | 8 | Max nesting depth for raw value compaction (deeper levels marked "[truncated depth]") |
| `ARCHIVE_MARKER_ID` | "output-archive-marker" | Synthetic id for UI hint that older archive entries have been pruned (not persisted) |
| `DEFAULT_PAGE_LIMIT` | 100 | Default number of entries per page when reading archive |
| `MAX_PAGE_LIMIT` | 500 | Max entries per page when reading archive |

### Summary of Image Flow with Truncation Points

For an ACP `agent_content` image entry (`raw.content.type === "image"`, `raw.content.data = base64`):

1. **ACP Runtime** (runtime-client.ts:558) → Creates entry with full `raw.content.data`
2. **Agent Archive** (output-store.ts:213) → `compactRawValue()` truncates raw to 1M chars (usually fine for single images)
3. **Live Snapshot** (output-store.ts:319) → `toLiveEntry()` further truncates raw to 4,000 chars
4. **Worker Stream Write** (workers/output-store.ts:1060) → `compactEntryForHistory()` applies `truncateHistoryString()` to raw.content.data, enforcing 4,000-char limit with head+tail preservation
5. **Canonical JSONL** → Entry persisted with truncated base64 string (max 4,000 chars)
6. **Stale Compaction** (workers/output-store.ts:2479) → Gzips the file without further rewriting; large entries stay at 4,000 chars

The **tightest bound** applied at write time is **RAW_HISTORY_CHAR_LIMIT = 4,000 characters** on the entire `raw` object's strings.
````

````text
Perfect. Now I have enough information to compile my findings. Let me create a comprehensive report:

## SERVE PATH FOR WORKER ENTRIES - COMPLETE TRACE

### 1. Route Handler and Query Modes

File: `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/runtime/http/routes/worker-entries.ts` (lines 65-194)

**Full Route Handler:** `handleWorkerEntriesRequest` (lines 65-194)

**Query Modes and Response Shapes:**

- **`contentEntryId` query param** (lines 99-135): Cold path for binary content retrieval
  - Calls `readWorkerEntryContent(workerId, contentEntryId)` 
  - Returns raw binary data (Uint8Array wrapped in Response)
  - Headers: `Content-Type`, `Cache-Control: private, max-age=3600`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`
  - Status: 200 (success) or error statuses (404, 413, 415, 500)

- **`view=plan` query param** (lines 136-154): Plan snapshot retrieval
  - Calls `readWorkerPlan(runId, workerId)`
  - Returns JSON of plan snapshot or 503 error

- **`beforeSeq` query param** (lines 163-166): Scroll-back path (older entries)
  - Calls `readWorkerEntriesBefore(runId, workerId, beforeSeq, limit)`
  - Returns: `{ entries: WorkerEntry[]; latestSeq: number; hasOlder: boolean }`

- **`limit` query param with `afterSeq=0`** (lines 172-178): Tail-first initial load
  - Calls `readWorkerEntriesTail(runId, workerId, limit)`
  - Returns: `{ entries: WorkerEntry[]; latestSeq: number; hasOlder: boolean }`
  - Falls back to full read if tail can't prove boundary

- **`afterSeq` query param (default path)** (lines 182-184): Live-tail cursor-based reads
  - Calls `readWorkerEntriesSince(runId, workerId, afterSeq)`
  - Returns: `{ entries: WorkerEntry[]; latestSeq: number; _path?: ReadWorkerEntriesPath }`
  - `_path` indicates which internal codepath was taken (for debugging/observability)

**Entry Response Building:** Normal (non-content, non-plan) entries are built by:
1. `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/workers/output-store.ts:1414` - `readWorkerEntriesSince` function
2. Entry sources: disk file (plaintext `.jsonl`), sparse index, cached in-memory parsed entries
3. Entries pass through `readCanonicalPersistedEntries()` which applies `normalizePersistedEntries()` for deduplication and seq normalization
4. **No field stripping occurs before sending to client** - full `WorkerEntry` objects (including `raw` field) are returned

---

### 2. Field Elision/Stripping on the Serve Path

Search Results: **Field stripping occurs at TWO locations:**

#### **Location 1: Disk Persistence (Compaction)**
File: `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/workers/output-store.ts` (lines 422-440)

Function: `compactEntryForHistory<T extends CompactableEntry>(entry: T): T`

**What is truncated/compacted before disk write:**
- `text` field: Large strings in tool_call/tool_call_update entries are truncated by line count and character limit
  - Limits: `RAW_HISTORY_LINE_LIMIT=8`, `RAW_HISTORY_CHAR_LIMIT=4000` characters
  - Preserves head (4 lines) + tail (4 lines) with omission markers
- Diff tool payloads: `oldText`/`newText` in edit entries are intelligently compressed to preserve divergent regions with context
- Generic `raw` field: Recursively truncated by `compactHistoryRawValue()` (lines 383-413)
- **Exception:** Entries with `planProjection === "accepted_core"` are NOT compacted - preserved for durable replay

This compaction happens in `appendWorkerEntry()` (line 1060) **before persisting to disk**, so by the time entries are read back via the route handler, they already have compacted history.

#### **Location 2: Frontend Cache (Optional)**
File: `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/WorkerEntriesManager.ts` (lines 229-234)

Function: `compactEntryForCache(entry: WorkerEntry): WorkerEntry`

```typescript
return {
  ...entry,
  raw: undefined,  // <-- raw field stripped when caching to localStorage
};
```

**This is NOT applied on the HTTP response.** It's only applied when persisting to browser localStorage as an optional preview cache (which is opt-in; defaultStorage() returns null for production).

---

### 3. SSE Stream Path for Worker Entry Appends

**Does worker entry content travel over SSE?** No.

File: `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/workers/stream-writer.ts` (lines 89-107)

SSE frame format for appended entries (line 99-104):
```typescript
emitNamedEvent({
  kind: "worker.entry_appended",
  runId: args.runId,
  workerId: args.workerId,
  seq: persisted.seq,  // <-- Only seq and workerId sent, NOT the full entry
});
```

Type Definition: `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/events/named-events.ts` (line 365)
```typescript
| { kind: "worker.entry_appended"; runId: string; workerId: string; seq: number }
```

**SSE Wake-up Behavior:**
- Each appended entry emits ONE wake-up frame with only `{ workerId, seq }`
- Frame is a "delta-only" wake-up: does NOT trigger full snapshot rebuild (see `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/events/live-updates.ts` line 26-30)
- Frontend receives the frame as a cursor hint and triggers refetch via `GET /api/workers/:workerId/entries?afterSeq=<latestContiguousSeq>`
- Large `raw.content.data` base64 strings (image data, etc.) **NEVER travel over SSE**

Documentation pointer: `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/runtime/http/routes/worker-entries.ts` (line 6-8) explicitly states this design.

---

### 4. Frontend Manager: WorkerEntriesManager

File: `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/WorkerEntriesManager.ts`

**What it fetches:**
- Calls `listEntries` runtime API (line 276-280) which maps to HTTP `GET /api/workers/:workerId/entries`
- Parameters: `{ workerId, limit?, afterSeq?, beforeSeq? }`
- Returns: `{ entries: WorkerEntry[]; latestSeq: number; hasOlder?: boolean }`

**In-Memory Storage:**
- Holds full `WorkerEntry` objects (line 239): `stateByWorker: Map<string, WorkerStreamState>`
- Each state contains complete `entries: WorkerEntry[]` array (line 195)
- **Includes full `raw` field** - NO stripping occurs in the manager's in-memory storage
- Maintains contiguous window invariant: `[lowestSeq..latestContiguousSeq]`
- SSE `worker.entry_appended` frames trigger `onWakeUp()` (line 426) which advances `latestKnownSeq` and triggers forward refetch

**Cache Behavior:**
- Optional localStorage cache (disabled by default) compacts entries by stripping `raw: undefined` (line 781)
- Cache is preview only; server state is authoritative
- Cache writes are coalesced to a trailing timer (DEFAULT_FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS = 1000ms) to avoid main-thread churn (line 129)

---

### 5. Test Coverage for Worker Entry Image Content

**Test Files Found:**

1. **`/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/tests/api/worker-entry-content-route.test.ts`** (lines 23-193)
   - Test: "serves a Codex-generated image referenced by a persisted worker entry" (lines 35-118)
     - Creates entry with `type: "agent_content"`, `raw.content.type: "image"`
     - Tests URI-based image loading with realpath security checks
     - Verifies O_NOFOLLOW flag usage
   - Test: "refuses a generated-image entry whose URI resolves outside that worker's content root" (lines 120-192)
     - Security test for symlink/path-traversal attacks
     - Verifies 403 error emission

2. **`/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/tests/app/worker-entries-manager.test.ts`** (lines 1-863)
   - Tests coalesceWorkerEntriesById() logic (lines 5-80)
   - Tests manager state management, caching, fetch/refresh paths (lines 127-863)
   - **Does NOT directly test image content** - focuses on entry metadata and stream continuity

3. **`/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/tests/api/worker-entries-hot-path.test.ts`** (lines 23-85)
   - Tests performance hot-path: avoids DB import for inferred run-id workers (lines 63-84)
   - Verifies entries can be served from `.jsonl` file without database lookup
   - **Does NOT test image content specifically**

4. **`/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/tests/runtime-api/adapter-contract.test.ts`** (mentions found but not examined)

5. **`/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/tests/app/acp/protocol-activity.test.ts`** (mentions found but not examined)

6. **`/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/tests/server/acp/session-updates.test.ts`** (mentions found but not examined)

**What the tests assert:**
- Image entry type validation: `type === "agent_content"` and `raw.content.type === "image"`
- MIME type filtering (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP supported)
- Base64 inline data decoding and truncation detection
- File system path security (realpath, TOCTOU checks, O_NOFOLLOW)
- Error emission and HTTP status codes (403, 404, 413, 415, 500)
- Entry persistence through `appendWorkerEntry()` JSONL append

---

### Summary Table

| Phase | Location | Field Handling | Size Impact |
|-------|----------|---|---|
| **Append to disk** | output-store.ts:1060 | `compactEntryForHistory()` truncates large `text` and `raw` | Reduces disk I/O for large outputs |
| **Read from disk** | output-store.ts:1414+ | Returns full entry after dedup/normalization | No further stripping |
| **HTTP response** | worker-entries.ts:184 | `Response.json()` sends full WorkerEntry | Complete `raw` field included |
| **SSE frame** | stream-writer.ts:99 | Emits `{ workerId, seq }` only | ~50 bytes vs full entry size |
| **Frontend fetch** | WorkerEntriesManager:276 | Stores full entries in state | Memory holds complete objects |
| **Optional localStorage** | WorkerEntriesManager:781 | Strips `raw: undefined` on cache write | Preview cache only, not used in production |
| **Content endpoint** | worker-entries.ts:99-114 | Binary blob returned separately via `?contentEntryId=` | Images served on dedicated route |
````

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-24T20:01:03.062Z

I'm realizing there are actually two separate truncation points at play—one in the agent-runtime's toLiveEntry function and another in compactEntryForHistory—both using a 4000 character limit, but the persisted data's truncation format matches the agent-runtime's version specifically.

To fix this, I need to exempt image content data at both truncation points, but also handle elision on the serve path since the HTTP entries response carries the full raw content including base64 image data, which would balloon into multi-MB JSON blobs otherwise. I'm now sketching the serve-path elision design in worker-entries.ts, where I'll map over entries before sending the JSON response to strip out the heavy content for agent_content entries.

I'm deciding where this elision logic should live given multiple read paths in the route, and concluding the route itself is the right boundary since that's where the client interface lives. I'm also thinking through whether to fully drop the data field versus truncating it, favoring dropping it since the client can fall back to the reference when displaying images.

I'm now weighing storage design more broadly — whether keeping full base64 in the canonical JSONL is wise given file size growth, versus writing image bytes to a sidecar file per entry and storing just a URI reference, though that adds complexity around garbage collection and deletion.

But CLAUDE.md's architecture rule against parallel persistence layers settles this: the unified worker stream is the sole persistence authority, so image content belongs inline just like message text does under the existing precedent that conversation content is exempt from truncation. I'm deciding to exempt image data from both truncation points, elide it at the HTTP boundary, and add an archive fallback for already-truncated streams — while still keeping some size guard in mind so persisted content doesn't grow unbounded.

Actually it's better to centralize the shared predicate and limit in one module so both truncation points and elision logic agree — src/shared/worker-entries.ts looks like the right place since it's already a client-safe type module, so I'll add an `isImageContentRaw(raw)` helper there for reuse across client and server. I need to check the exact shape of `entry.raw.content` (type, data, mimeType) and how `agent_content` entries are structured, then figure out how to exempt image data from the generic `compactRawValue` recursion — simplest approach is restoring the original `raw.content.data` after compaction if the entry was flagged as an image.

I'll write a shared helper, `preserveImageContentData`, that re-attaches the full image payload to the compacted raw data when it's within the cap, and apply it consistently in both `createArchiveEntry`/`toLiveEntry` and `compactEntryForHistory` in workers/output-store.ts. Then I need to look at the reader fallback logic to handle cases where inline data was truncated.

Now I'm also planning fixes: in request.ts, always attempt to read and parse the error body as text/JSON even for blob or arrayBuffer response types, and in WorkerEntryContentUrlManager I need to properly extract the message from RuntimeApiError, possibly by adding a shared runtimeErrorMessage helper if one doesn't already exist. I should also check whether any new user-facing error strings need to be added across all locale files.

Since normalizeRuntimeHttpError already produces an untranslated "Runtime request failed with HTTP ${status}" message, I can avoid adding new i18n strings by just surfacing the existing `.message`. My edit plan: add a compact/restore image-content-data helper to worker-entries.ts, and update output-store.ts accordingly.

Error message surfacing, plus tests to cover the changes. Before making edits, I want to actually read through the relevant files: shared/worker-entries.ts, the agent-runtime output-store chunk, workers/output-store.ts around compactEntryForHistory and the archive-read helper, and the tail of the route handler.

```text
379	function joinParts(parts: Array<string | null | undefined>) {
380	  return parts.filter((part): part is string => typeof part === "string" && part.length > 0).join("\n");
381	}
382	
383	function compactHistoryRawValue(value: unknown): unknown {
384	  if (typeof value === "string") {
385	    return truncateHistoryString(value);
386	  }
387	  if (Array.isArray(value)) {
388	    return value.map(compactHistoryRawValue);
389	  }
390	  if (!isPlainRecord(value)) {
391	    return value;
392	  }
393	  const compressed = compressDiffContentPair(value);
394	  if (compressed) {
395	    // oldText/newText have already been compacted with diff-aware logic
396	    // that preserves their divergence. Skipping the generic per-string
397	    // truncator on those two fields is required — line-based truncation
398	    // would re-collapse them to identical head/tail strings and erase the
399	    // diff signal.
400	    return Object.fromEntries(
401	      Object.entries(compressed)
402	        .filter(([key]) => key !== "__diffCompressed")
403	        .map(([key, nested]) => (
404	          key === "oldText" || key === "newText"
405	            ? [key, nested]
406	            : [key, compactHistoryRawValue(nested)]
407	        )),
408	    );
409	  }
410	  return Object.fromEntries(
411	    Object.entries(value).map(([key, nested]) => [key, compactHistoryRawValue(nested)]),
412	  );
413	}
414	
415	type CompactableEntry = {
416	  type?: string;
417	  text?: string;
418	  raw?: unknown;
419	  [key: string]: unknown;
420	};
421	
422	export function compactEntryForHistory<T extends CompactableEntry>(entry: T): T {
423	  // Accepted ACP core plans are already ingress-bounded and their original raw
424	  // notification is part of the durable replay contract. Generic history
425	  // compaction must not silently rewrite that accepted evidence.
426	  if (entry.planProjection === "accepted_core") {
427	    return entry;
428	  }
429	  const text = (entry.type === "tool_call" || entry.type === "tool_call_update") && typeof entry.text === "string"
430	    ? truncateHistoryString(entry.text)
431	    : entry.text;
432	  if (entry.raw === undefined && text === entry.text) {
433	    return entry;
434	  }
435	  return {
436	    ...entry,
437	    text,
438	    raw: entry.raw === undefined ? undefined : compactHistoryRawValue(entry.raw),
439	  };
440	}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-24T20:01:38.949Z

I'm writing a shared helper module that keeps generated-image payloads intact in the transcript, avoiding truncation from generic raw-string limits and instead using a bound sized well above a typical screenshot, matching the 25 MB read-side cap.

Now I'm considering whether the image-preservation logic should only apply to agent-generated content or also cover user-submitted content, since both count as conversation content — deciding it's simpler to key off the raw content shape itself.

I realize a compaction pass over arbitrary raw values could hit false positives, but tool call updates use array-shaped content, so that's safe. I'll narrow this by explicitly gating on entry type instead, restricting it to agent and user content types.

I also want a shared helper that swaps an inline image payload for a pointer referencing the worker and entry IDs, so the HTTP boundary can serve it separately rather than inlining large image data.

Now I'm checking that the terminal client already expects that pointer shape and just needs to be wired up. I also realize the generated-images gallery reads image data directly, so eliding it there could break unowned entries with no fallback worker — better to have that gallery builder also recognize the pointer as a valid reference source instead of assuming raw data is always present.

Now I'm laying out the implementation order: adding pointer helpers to the shared worker-entries module, threading preservation through the archive/live entry creation and compaction logic in both output-store files, updating entry-content fallback handling, eliding raw data at the HTTP route boundary, teaching the runtime-api request layer to parse error JSON for blob responses, surfacing a clear message in the URL manager, and finally updating the generated-image activity to read from the pointer.

Now moving on to writing tests, starting with the shared worker-entries module since it currently has minimal imports so adding pure helper functions there should be straightforward.

The cap\n// is expressed in base64 characters and sized to match the 25 MB decoded ceiling\n// enforced on the read side by `src/server/workers/entry-content.ts`.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/** 25 MB of decoded bytes, expressed as base64 characters (4 chars per 3 bytes). */\nexport const IMAGE_CONTENT_DATA_CHARS = Math.ceil((25 * 1024 * 1024) / 3) * 4;\n\nconst IMAGE_CONTENT_TYPES: ReadonlySet<WorkerEntryType> = new Set<WorkerEntryType>([\n  \"agent_content\",\n  \"user_content\",\n]);\n\n/** Pointer the client resolves through `GET /api/workers/:id/entries?contentEntryId=`. */\nexport type WorkerEntryContentPointer = {\n  workerId: string;\n  entryId: string;\n};\n\nfunction asContentRecord(value: unknown): Record<string, unknown> | null {\n  return typeof value === \"object\" && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value)\n    ? value as Record<string, unknown>\n    : null;\n}\n\n/**\n * The inline base64 image payload on this entry's raw notification, or null\n * when the entry does not carry one.\n */\nexport function inlineImageContentData(\n  entryType: WorkerEntryType | string | undefined,\n  raw: unknown,\n): string | null {\n  if (!IMAGE_CONTENT_TYPES.has(entryType as WorkerEntryType)) {\n    return null;\n  }\n  const content = asContentRecord(asContentRecord(raw)?.content);\n  if (content?.type !== \"image\" || typeof content.data !== \"string\") {\n    return null;\n  }\n  return content.data;\n}\n\n/**\n * Re-attach the untruncated image payload to an already-compacted `raw`.\n *\n * Compaction stays generic — it still walks and bounds every other string in\n * the notification — and this restores only the one field the transcript\n * cannot regenerate. Payloads past the cap keep whatever the compactor\n * produced, so the read side reports them as unavailable rather than handing\n * back a corrupt image.\n */\nexport function preserveInlineImageContentData(\n  entryType: WorkerEntryType | string | undefined,\n  originalRaw: unknown,\n  compactedRaw: unknown,\n): unknown {\n  const data = inlineImageContentData(entryType, originalRaw);\n  if (data === null || data.length > IMAGE_CONTENT_DATA_CHARS) {\n    return compactedRaw;\n  }\n  const compacted = asContentRecord(compactedRaw);\n  const content = asContentRecord(compacted?.content);\n  if (!compacted || !content) {\n    return compactedRaw;\n  }\n  return { ...compacted, content: { ...content, data } };\n}\n\n/**\n * Swap an inline image payload for a content pointer before the entry crosses\n * the HTTP boundary.\n *\n * The stream stores whole images; sending them inline would put megabytes of\n * base64 into every transcript page. ## Claude thinking · 2026-08-24T20:02:49.477Z

Now looking at item #4, the archive fallback for entry-content.ts: I'm tracing how it reads worker output entries, finds the matching entry by ID, and validates it's an image-type agent_content entry before proceeding to decode the inline body and determine the mime type.

I want to add a fallback so that if the inline body is missing, the code tries the runtime archive for the same entryId before falling back to the codex-specific path. I confirmed that archive entries preserve the same ID as canonical stream entries since `toLiveEntry` spreads the original entry, and `reassembleArchivedEntries` keeps agent_content entries unchanged with their ID intact — so the ID matching should work. Now I'm also wondering whether I should extend the accepted entry types beyond agent_content to include user_content.

I'm realizing there's a subtle consistency risk: if the eliding logic strips user_content image data while my preserving logic keeps it, but the reader only accepts agent_content, the reader would 404 when a client fetches a user_content image. To keep things safe and consistent, I should restrict both the elider and the preserver to agent_content only, matching the reader's scope — that keeps all three components in sync.

I realize passing entry?.type into the archive decode is fragile since a missing canonical entry means no type gate check happens. I should restructure this into a dedicated function that fetches the archived entry itself and does its own type check and decode, rather than relying on the canonical entry's type.

Now I'm cleaning up the validation logic: the entry must be a content-type entry (agent_content or user_content) and content.type must equal "image" — my earlier version weakened this check, so I'm rewriting it to enforce both conditions together, then adding the mime-type validation and base64 decoding.

I'm falling back to the archived output when inline image data isn't found, adding a helper that looks up the entry from the runtime output archive and extracts its inline image content if present. Now I'm making sure `findLatestEntryById` and `inlineImageContentData` are typed correctly against the archived entries before rewriting this section of the file.

I'll add a helper function that safely extracts a message string from an unknown rejection value, checking if it's an Error instance first, then falling back to a message property, and finally stringifying as a last resort.   "old_string": "export function isRuntimeTransportFailure(error: unknown) {\n  return typeof (error as { code?: unknown } | null | undefined)?.code !== \"string\";\n}",
```json
  "new_string": "export function isRuntimeTransportFailure(error: unknown) {\n  return typeof (error as { code?: unknown } | null | undefined)?.code !== \"string\";\n}\n\n/**\n * Human-readable text for a rejection from the runtime API.\n *\n * `RuntimeApiError` is a plain object literal, never an `Error`, so the usual\n * `error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)` shorthand renders\n * it as \"[object Object]\" and throws away the server's message.\n */\nexport function runtimeErrorMessage(error: unknown): string {\n  if (error instanceof Error) {\n    return error.message;\n  }\n  const message = (error as { message?: unknown } | null | undefined)?.message;\n  return typeof message === \"string\" && message.length > 0 ? message : String(error);\n}"
}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-24T20:06:15.515Z

I'll now write tests covering how a full-size base64 image survives through appendWorkerEntry and gets served correctly, how entries responses elide the payload with a pointer instead, and how a stream with a truncated payload falls back to the runtime archive. I also want unit tests for the shared helpers, the request.ts blob error handling, and the manager message logic, so let me check what exists already.

I'm splitting this into two focused files: a server-side test for persistence, elision, and archive fallback, and a client-side test verifying blob error bodies surface properly and get stored by the manager. For the archive fallback case, I'll need to write directly to the runtime archive path using a random worker ID to avoid collisions, cleaning it up afterward, and generate a large base64 payload (over 4000 chars, using a valid image mime type) to simulate what would have previously been truncated.

I need to check whether `readWorkerEntryContent` requires a worker type—it shouldn't for inline data since that skips the codex branch, so I'll use "claude" as the type. For the elision test, I'll fetch entries without a contentEntryId and confirm the raw content data is stripped while the omniWorkerContent reference with workerId and entryId remains. For the archive fallback, I'll append a truncated entry, then write the full untruncated data to a matching archive JSONL line, and verify the GET request reconstructs the original bytes—though I need to first confirm the exact shape `parseWorkerEntryLines` expects (id, type, text, timestamp, raw) and check the `workerEntriesRoute` test helper before finalizing this.

I also need to verify whether `appendWorkerEntry` depends on prior artifact stream or run rows, since the existing test seeds plans/runs/workers first—I'll follow that same setup pattern in my new test file.
